The differentials of the first and last components give −*d*(*w* sinh *Ht*)² + *d*(*w* cosh *Ht*)² = *dw*² − *w*²*H*²*dt*², the differential of *r***n** gives *dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), and *dw* = −*r* *dr*/*w*. The sum is −*w*²*H*²*dt*² + (1 + *r*²/*w*²)*dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), and *w*²*H*² = *c*²(1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*²), 1 + *r*²/*w*² = 1/(1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*²). A linear map preserving the form preserves the metric it induces on *Y*. (iii) The same argument applies, with ⟨*ξ*₀, *ξ*₀⟩ < 0 removing one negative square, so that *E* again has one negative and three positive squares. For the map: −*w*² + *r*² = −*L*²; the first two components give −*dw*² − *w*²*c*²*dt*²/*L*², with *dw* = *r* *dr*/*w*, and the sum is −(*w*²/*L*²)*c*²*dt*² + (1 − *r*²/*w*²)*dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), with *w*²/*L*² = *F* and 1 − *r*²/*w*² = 1/*F*. By Theorem II.8.2.1(ii) with *A* = *F*, *B* = 1/*F*, as in the proof of Theorem II.8.6.17(ii), *F*″ + 2*F*′/*r* = 6/*L*² and 1 − *F* − *rF*′ = −3*r*²/*L*², so *R*_μν = −(3/*L*²)*g*_μν and *G*_μν − (3/*L*²)*g*_μν = 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.6.43.** The field equations of §II.9.2 contain the constant Λ, the ratio *β*/*α* of Theorem II.9.2.2, whose value the present paper does not derive. For Λ = 0 the solution without matter is the spacetime of Theorem II.6.28 with its pair (ISO⁺(1,3), SO⁺(1,3)); for Λ > 0 and Λ < 0 the solutions of greatest symmetry carry the pairs of this theorem, with the same subgroup O(1,3) fixing an event. Which of the three is the pair of the universe is the question of the value of Λ.

**Theorem II.6.44 (Curved spacetime: the pair of Theorem II.6.28 at each event, and the condition for a Klein pair of the whole).** (i) Let *g*_μν be a Lorentzian metric. At each event the coordinate systems adapted to it (Lemma II.9.2.1(i)) exist, and the Jacobian matrix at the event of the change from one adapted system to another is a matrix Λ with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*; every such Λ occurs, and each is the Jacobian matrix of exactly one linear change. So the group O(1,3), the group fixing an event in Theorem II.6.28 together with the reflections, acts on the adapted systems at each event, carrying each to every other. (ii) If a group of transformations preserving the metric carries every event to every other, then every scalar formed from the metric and its derivatives by the rules of tensor algebra has the same value at all events. (iii) For the Schwarzschild metric of Theorem II.8.2.1 with *r*_s > 0 the scalar *R*_μνρσ*R*^μνρσ equals 12*r*_s²/*r*⁶. Hence no group of transformations preserving that metric carries every event to every other: this spacetime is the space of no Klein pair of such transformations.

*Proof.* (i) In two adapted systems the metric at the event is *η* in both, and the transformation law of the metric at the event reads Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* for the Jacobian matrix Λ. Conversely, if *y* is adapted and Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*, the linear change *y*′ = Λ⁻¹*y* gives the metric Λᵀ*g*Λ, which equals *η* at the event and has vanishing first derivatives there. (ii) Such a scalar is defined by the same formula in all coordinates, and a transformation preserving the metric carries the coordinates at one event to coordinates at the image event in which the metric is the same function; so the scalar has the same value at both. (iii) With the connection coefficients of Theorem II.8.2.1(i) and *A* = 1/*B* = 1 − *r*_s/*r*, the components of the curvature tensor referred to the unit vectors along the coordinate lines are, up to the symmetries of the tensor: *r*_s/*r*³ in magnitude for the pair of directions (*t*, *r*) and for the pair (*θ*, *φ*), and *r*_s/(2*r*³) in magnitude for the four pairs (*t*, *θ*), (*t*, *φ*), (*r*, *θ*), (*r*, *φ*). Each such component occurs four times in the full contraction, so the scalar is 4[2(*r*_s/*r*³)² + 4(*r*_s/(2*r*³))²] = 12*r*_s²/*r*⁶. It takes different values at different *r*, and (ii) applies. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.6.44.** Mass curves and stretches *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, and part (iii) shows that the curved spacetime of a mass is the space of no Klein pair of its own. What the Principle supplies at every event of every solution is part (i): the group of Theorem II.6.28 acting on the systems in which the interval at that event is *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄². This is the sense in which Cartan generalized Klein’s programme. The theory of the connection that joins the groups at neighbouring events, and the group of all smooth transformations of spacetime, are not treated here.

### **Mathematical Physics on a Categorical Foundation.**

The consequence for mathematical physics is a change of foundation rather than of notation. Standard formulations posit each arena — Minkowski space, Hilbert space, phase space, gauge bundle, Fock space, operator algebra — as a separate object, with morphisms (Wick rotation, geometric quantization, second quantization, Dirac factorization, gauge covariantization) added by hand. Inside **McG** these arenas appear as objects of one category and the morphisms as canonical functors generated by the McGucken Operator D_M. The arena hierarchy is no longer a list of axioms; it is a diagram in **McG**, and each arrow in the diagram is an instance of the source law.

This is what is meant by completing the Erlangen Programme: not extending its catalogue of geometries, but supplying the source from which the Klein pairs of spacetime, of the space of states and of phase space, with the Euclidean, Galilean, spin and conformal pairs (Theorems II.6.28, II.6.30, II.6.33, II.6.34 and II.6.36–II.6.39) — and the arenas built on them — are co-generated by a single law [88, 91, 89, 90]. Klein gave the classification; Cartan, Weyl, and Ehresmann gave the differential geometry; Mac Lane, Eilenberg, and Grothendieck gave the categorical vocabulary; the McGucken Principle supplies the source from which the classified objects arise.

### **Why This Completion Is Unique.**

Several features distinguish this completion from the partial extensions that came before it. *First*, it is generated by a single one-line law rather than by a list of axioms; the primitive count of the framework, examined in the next section, is one. *Second*, the law generates the **pair** (McGucken Space ℳ_G, McGucken Operator D_M) simultaneously and reciprocally — a feature with no precedent in the Erlangen tradition, where space and operator are always introduced separately. *Third*, the completion holds in two independent languages at once: the group-theoretic language of 1872 and the category-theoretic language of 1945. *Fourth*, the law is simultaneously a *mathematical axiom* — the source of the formal category **McG** — and a *physical principle* — the statement that the fourth dimension expands at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner. The completion is mathematical and physical at the same time, by the same one-line equation.

It is worth emphasizing how unusual this is. Klein in 1872 had a meta-principle (geometry = invariants of a group) but no law generating the group. Hilbert in 1900 had the demand for a generating law but no candidate. The constructive programme of the twentieth century supplied region-specific axiomatic systems but no unifying source. The Erlangen Programme stood open for 154 years, the Sixth Problem for 126. The McGucken Principle closes both at once, in both languages, with one equation. That convergence — one law completing two long-standing programmes in two independent formal frameworks — is the fact celebrated in this section.

With the categorical foundation in place we may now ask the quantitative question Hilbert posed first: how many independent primitives does the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework actually require? We turn to that count in the next section.

### II.7. Comparative Axiomatics: Counting Primitives

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

Hilbert’s first demand on the Sixth Problem was that the axiomatization proceed “by a small number of axioms.” To make this requirement quantitative we count *independent primitive structures*: objects, relations, or operators that must be specified before any theorem can be stated. We count a *law* as one primitive only when its variables are quantities defined elsewhere in the framework; if the variables are themselves uninterpreted symbols, they are also primitives. By this rule, Newton’s *F* = *m***a** is one primitive law over the previously defined kinematic primitives (position, time, mass), not four primitives {*F*, *m*, **a**, =}. The McGucken Principle is one primitive law over the quantities (*x*₄, *t*) and the constants (*i*, *c*), all of which are previously available in any physics curriculum. The action *h* is in the Principle itself, set by the wavelength of the expanding McGucken Sphere; Newton’s constant *G* enters only as the measured coupling *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit in §II.8.2.

Table 7.1 below tabulates the count for the major foundational programmes of the twentieth and early twenty-first centuries. The qualitative point is that no prior programme reaches one primitive; the McGucken Principle does.

| Programme                         | Year          | Count        | Primitives                                                      | Derives arena?                                           |
|-----------------------------------|---------------|--------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|
| **McGucken Principle**            | **2008–2026** | **1**        | ***dx*₄/*dt* = i*c***                                           | **Yes (McGucken Space ℳ_G, *H*, McGucken Operator D_M)** |
| Newton (mechanics)                | 1687          | 4+           | absolute space, absolute time, mass, force law                  | No (space & time given)                                  |
| Hilbert (1899) geometry           | 1899          | 5 groups     | incidence, order, congruence, parallels, continuity             | Plane only                                               |
| Kolmogorov (probability)          | 1933          | 3 + 4        | sample space, σ-algebra, measure; K1–K4                         | No (sample space given)                                  |
| von Neumann (QM)                  | 1932          | 5+           | Hilbert space, observables, states, evolution, measurement      | No (*H* given)                                           |
| Wightman / Gårding–Wightman (QFT) | 1956 / 1964   | 6            | Hilbert space, Poincaré rep, vacuum, fields, locality, spectrum | No (Minkowski given)                                     |
| Haag–Kastler (algebraic QFT)      | 1964          | 4 + 4 axioms | net of C*-algebras + isotony, locality, covariance, spectrum   | No (Minkowski given)                                     |
| Causal sets                       | 1987          | 2            | set, partial order                                              | Lorentzian causal structure only                         |
| Spectral triples (Connes)         | 1980s         | 3            | algebra *A*, Hilbert space *H*, Dirac operator *D*              | Geometry only; no QM dynamics                            |

*Table 7.1. Primitive count and arena-generation status of foundational programmes.*

Two clarifications. First, the count assigned to McGucken is one only if the symbols (*x*₄, *t*, *i*, *c*) are taken from prior physical and mathematical vocabulary—the same way *F*, *m*, **a** are taken in *F* = *m***a**. To insist they be re-derived from outside is to demand the law be derived, which Hilbert’s notion of axiomatization does not require (“the axioms so set up are at the same time the definitions of those elementary ideas”). Second, the entry under “derives arena” is the distinguishing feature: every other entry takes the manifold or the Hilbert space as a separate primitive in addition to its dynamical axioms. The McGucken Principle generates both arena and dynamics from a single law, which is exactly the step Hilbert’s Sixth Problem requires.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Axiom Counting: Primitive-Law Complexity *C* = 1.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the count of axioms the Principle predicts the value one: the proper axiom is the single statement *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, and the carrier *E*₄, the McGucken Sphere and the origin convention *x*₄(0) = 0 specify the framework in which that statement is written. Definitions 6, 9 and 21 and Theorem 22 above state the count as a definition and a theorem.

**Definition II.7.1 (Derivational closure; Definitions 6 and 9 above).** The derivational closure Der(McGucken Space ℳ_G) is the set of mathematical structures obtainable from (McGucken Space ℳ_G, McGucken Operator D_M) by finite sequences of applications of the closure operations: (O1) constraint imposition, (O2) quotient projection, (O3) slicing along the flow parameter, (O4) bundle formation, (O5) section formation, (O6) cotangent lift, (O7) complexification, (O8) representation formation, (O9) quantization, (O10) completion, (O11) tensor product, (O12) Fock construction, (O13) operator-algebra construction, (O14) connection formation.

**Definition II.7.2 (Primitive-law complexity; Definition 21 above).** For a foundational mathematical system *X*, the primitive-law complexity *C*(*X*) is the minimum number of independent primitive axioms required to generate the closure Der(*X*) associated with *X*, where independence is measured in the standard sense of axiomatic systems (no axiom is derivable from the others).

**Theorem II.7.3 (Minimal primitive-law complexity; Theorem 22 above).** *C*(McGucken Space ℳ_G) = 1 ≤ *C*(*X*) for every nontrivial generative system *X*.

*Proof.* *Lower bound.* Any nontrivial generative system requires at least one proper axiom specific to itself; a system with zero proper axioms generates only the structure already present in its underlying logical or mathematical apparatus, which does not constitute a generative system in the sense of Definition II.7.2. Hence *C*(*X*) ≥ 1.

*Upper bound for the McGucken* dx*₄/*dt *= i*c *framework, with a careful counting convention.* We adopt the standard counting convention used in foundational mathematics: *C*(*X*) counts the proper axioms specific to *X*, with the underlying logical apparatus and the universal mathematical apparatus (sets, functions, the standard operations on them) not counted. By this convention: ZFC has *C* = 9, counting the axioms of extensionality, pairing, union, power set, infinity, separation, replacement, foundation, choice [each axiom schema counted once]; the underlying first-order logic with equality is not counted. Peano arithmetic has *C* = 9 [Peano’s 1889 list, four of whose nine axioms concern equality] or *C* = 5 (second-order formulation), counting the axioms about 0, the successor function, and induction [in first-order Peano arithmetic induction is a schema, counted once]; the underlying logic is not counted. Hilbert’s geometry (*Grundlagen der Geometrie*) has *C* = 5 groups of axioms.

By the same counting convention applied to the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework: the proper axiom specific to the framework is the single statement *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The closure operations (O1)–(O14) are part of the universal mathematical apparatus (constraint imposition is selection of a subset by a definable predicate, available in any sufficiently rich set-theoretic foundation; complexification, Cauchy completion, tensor product, Fock construction, and operator-algebra construction are standard constructions of differential geometry and functional analysis; bundle and connection formation are standard differential-geometric constructions). [Quantization (O9) applied to the McGucken Operator D_M is the term iℏ · McGucken Operator D_M of the formal language ℒ_M of Definition 2 above, formed with the scalar-operator multiplication and the constant ℏ of that language; it is a term of the language and adds no axiom.] The framework structures (the carrier *E*₄ and the McGucken Sphere) and Convention κ are framework-specifying components analogous to the choice of vocabulary in ZFC (the symbol ∈) or the choice of a base point in pointed categories; they are not counted as proper axioms. Hence *C*(McGucken Space ℳ_G) = 1 by the standard counting convention. Combined with the lower bound: *C*(McGucken Space ℳ_G) = 1, the absolute floor. □

**The two inputs of the Hilbert-space sector.** Theorem 14 above constructs the complex Hilbert space from the McGucken Space ℳ_G together with two inputs, which Theorem 14 names the Born postulate (B) and Huygens’ principle (H), and the remark following that theorem states that with both reduced to theorems the construction is unconditional. Both are theorems in the sources of the present paper. (B) is Theorem 7.2 of [114], proved in §II.9.3 from the conservation law of the McGucken Sphere. (H) is the strong Huygens principle for the d’Alembertian in three space dimensions, a theorem of classical analysis through Kirchhoff’s formula [19], listed in the corpus as Theorem 2.8 of [129]. In [120, Part IV] the retarded Green’s function of the wave operator is *G* = *δ*(*t* − *t*′ − |**x** − **x**′|/*c*)/|**x** − **x**′|, supported on the sphere |**x** − **x**′| = *c*(*t* − *t*′), and the solution at (**x**, *t*) from data on the surface *t*′ is *ψ*(**x**, *t*) = (1/4*πc*²) ∫ [*G* ∂*ψ*/∂*t*′ − *ψ* ∂*G*/∂*t*′] *d*³*x*′. Kirchhoff’s formula states that the solution at an event is an integral of the data over a sphere; the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* provides the physical mechanism for Huygens’ principle, the spherically symmetric expansion of *x*₄ at rate *c* from every event. With (B) and (H) as theorems, the count of Theorem II.7.3 covers the Hilbert-space sector with no added axiom.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Table 7.1.** Table 7.1 counts primitive structures (objects, relations and operators that must be specified before a theorem can be stated); Definition II.7.2 counts proper axioms. The McGucken Principle has the value one under both conventions: one law, *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, in Table 7.1, and one proper axiom in Theorem II.7.3. The programmes in the remaining rows of Table 7.1 take their arena as a separate primitive, which Theorem II.4.1 derives from the same law.

### II.8. The Derivation Lattice: How Physics Emerges from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

We outline the principal derivation chains. Each is established as an explicit theorem chain in McGucken’s papers—general relativity in [71, 71], quantum mechanics in [73], the joint GR–QM unification in [72], thermodynamics and the second law in [80, 80], the symmetries and the Erlangen completion in [74], the action and the Lagrangian in [77], spacetime’s foundational atoms together with its sphere–amplituhedron structure in [75], the source-pair reciprocal generation in [86, 87, 86, 87, 91], and the joint Space–Operator co-generation underwriting the categorical foundation in [88, 89, 90]. We present the structure rather than the full computation, as the latter occupies several hundred pages across the source documents.

**II.8.1 Special Relativity.** From the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* the line element *ds*² = *dx*₄² + *d***x**² = *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² follows (§II.9.1). With proper time defined by *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*², the master equation *u*^μ *u*_μ = −*c*² follows, expressing that every object’s four-velocity has fixed magnitude *c*. The four-velocity budget (*dx*₄/*dτ*)² + |*d***x**/*dτ*|² = −*c*², equivalently (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + |*d***x**/*dt*|² = *c*² in coordinate time, where *c* *dτ*/*dt* = *c*/*γ* is the standard time-dilation factor, yields, by elementary algebra, time dilation *dt*/*dτ* = γ, length contraction *L* = *L*₀/γ, mass-energy equivalence *E*₀ = *mc*² (from the temporal component of four-momentum *p*₄ = iγ*mc* = i*E*/*c*), and the Lorentz transformation. None of these are postulates; all follow from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* by direct calculation. The full derivation, with the Lorentz transformation obtained as an explicit theorem rather than a postulate, is presented in McGucken [84, 71, 72].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Special Relativity: The Interval, Inertial Coordinates, Proper Time, Time Dilation, the Master Equation, the Lorentz Transformation, Length Contraction, the Constancy of *c*, and *E* = *mc*², with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the kinematics of special relativity the Principle predicts the following: because *x*₄ advances at the rate i*c* against *t* and is perpendicular to *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, distances in the four coordinates are measured by *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² − *c*²*dt*², and every relation of special relativity is a property of that one quadratic form. The statements below follow GR Theorem 1 and GR Corollary 1 of [72] and Parts I–II of [120]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Lemma II.8.1.1 (The interval).** With *x*₄ = i*ct*, the four-coordinate form *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄² equals *ds*² = *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² − *c*²*dt*².

*Proof.* This is Lemma II.6.25: *dx*₄ = i*c* *dt*, so *dx*₄² = −*c*²*dt*². □

**Definition II.8.1.2 (Inertial coordinate system).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is a statement about the fourth dimension *x*₄; it refers to no state of motion and holds from every event. An inertial coordinate system is a system of coordinates (*t*, *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃) on the events, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, in which the Principle reads *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* and the four-coordinate form is *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄². By Lemma II.8.1.1 the interval between two neighbouring events is *ds*² = *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² in every inertial coordinate system.

**Lemma II.8.1.3 (Transformations between inertial coordinate systems).** Let (*t*, **x**) and (*t*′, **x**′) be two inertial coordinate systems on the same events, and write *x*^μ = (*ct*, *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃), *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1). Then *x*′ = *Λx* + *a* with *a* constant and *Λ* a constant matrix satisfying *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*.

*Proof.* Both systems assign to a pair of neighbouring events the same interval (Definition II.8.1.2), so with *J*^α_μ = ∂*x*′^α/∂*x*^μ, *η*_αβ *J*^α_μ *J*^β_ν = *η*_μν at every event. Differentiate with respect to *x*^ρ and write *S*_ρμν = (∂_ρ *J*^α_μ) *η*_αβ *J*^β_ν. Since ∂_ρ *J*^α_μ = ∂_ρ ∂_μ *x*′^α, *S*_ρμν is symmetric in its first two indices, and the differentiated identity reads *S*_ρμν + *S*_ρνμ = 0. Then *S*_ρμν = −*S*_ρνμ = −*S*_νρμ = *S*_νμρ = *S*_μνρ = −*S*_μρν = −*S*_ρμν, so *S*_ρμν = 0. The matrix *η*_αβ *J*^β_ν is invertible because its product with *J*ᵀ is *η*, so ∂_ρ ∂_μ *x*′^α = 0: the map is affine, *x*′ = *Λx* + *a*, and the identity for *J* = *Λ* is *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*. □

**Definition II.8.1.4 (Proper time).** Along a worldline **x**(*t*) with speed *v* = |*d***x**/*dt*| < *c*, the proper time *τ* is defined by *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*², with *τ* increasing with *t*. By Lemma II.8.1.3 the interval is the same in every inertial coordinate system, so *τ* is the same in every inertial coordinate system.

**Theorem II.8.1.5 (Time dilation; equation (XII) of [120]).** Along a worldline of speed *v* < *c*, *dt*/*dτ* = *γ* with *γ* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(−1/2).

*Proof.* By Lemma II.8.1.1 and Definition II.8.1.4, *c*²*dτ*² = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**² = *c*²*dt*² (1 − *v*²/*c*²). Hence (*dτ*/*dt*)² = 1 − *v*²/*c*², and since *τ* increases with *t*, *dτ*/*dt* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(1/2) = 1/*γ*. A clock moving at speed *v* advances by *dτ* = *dt*/*γ* while the coordinate time advances by *dt*. □

**Theorem II.8.1.6 (Master equation and four-velocity budget; GR Theorem 1 and GR Corollary 1 of [72]).** Along a worldline of speed *v* < *c* let *u*₄ = *dx*₄/*dτ* and **u** = *d***x**/*dτ*. Then (i) *u*₄ = i*cγ* and **u** = *γ***v**; (ii) the sum *u*₄² + |**u**|² equals −*c*², which in the coordinates *x*^μ = (*ct*, **x**) is the master equation *u*^μ *u*_μ = −*c*²; (iii) the budget |*dx*₄/*dτ*|² − |*d***x**/*dτ*|² = *c*² holds, and in coordinate time (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + |*d***x**/*dt*|² = *c*².

*Proof.* (i) By the chain rule and the Principle, *dx*₄/*dτ* = (*dx*₄/*dt*)(*dt*/*dτ*) = i*c* *γ*, using Theorem II.8.1.5; likewise *d***x**/*dτ* = **v** *γ*. (ii) Squaring, *u*₄² + |**u**|² = (i*cγ*)² + *γ*²*v*² = −*γ*²(*c*² − *v*²) = −*c*², because *γ*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²) = 1. With *u*⁰ = *c* *dt*/*dτ* = *cγ*, *u*₄ = i*u*⁰ and *u*^μ *u*_μ = −(*u*⁰)² + |**u**|² = *u*₄² + |**u**|² = −*c*². (iii) Here |*u*₄|² = *c*²*γ*² and |**u**|² = *γ*²*v*², so |*u*₄|² − |**u**|² = *c*². Multiplying the identity *c*²*dτ*² = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**² of Theorem II.8.1.5 by 1/*dt*² gives (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + *v*² = *c*². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the budget.** The quantity *c* *dτ*/*dt* = *c*/*γ*, the standard time-dilation factor multiplied by *c*, is the share of the fixed total *c* that the worldline does not spend on spatial motion. For a body at rest in space it equals *c*. For light, *v* = *c* and the budget gives *c*² − *v*² = 0: the photon is at rest in *x*₄, while the expansion *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* holds at every event of its worldline. The proper-time relation *dx*₄/*dτ* = i*cγ* of part (i) is a consequence of the Principle through the chain rule; the Principle itself is *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in the coordinate time *t*.

**Theorem II.8.1.7 (Lorentz transformation; equation (XVI) of [120]).** Let (*t*, *x*, *y*, *z*) and (*t*′, *x*′, *y*′, *z*′) be inertial coordinate systems whose origins coincide at *t* = *t*′ = 0, with *y*′ = *y*, *z*′ = *z*, with (*t*′, *x*′) depending on (*t*, *x*) alone, with *x*′ increasing with *x* and *t*′ increasing with *t*, and with the spatial origin *x*′ = 0 moving along *x* = *vt*, |*v*| < *c*. Then *x*′ = *γ*(*x* − *vt*) and *t*′ = *γ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²).

*Proof.* By Lemma II.8.1.3 and the coincidence of origins the transformation is linear and preserves *ds*². With *y*′ = *y*, *z*′ = *z* it preserves *x*² + *x*₄², where *x*₄ = i*ct* and *x*₄′ = i*ct*′. Write *x*′ = *ax* + *bx*₄ and *x*₄′ = *ex* + *fx*₄. Equating *x*′² + *x*₄′² with *x*² + *x*₄² for all (*x*, *x*₄) gives *a*² + *e*² = 1, *b*² + *f*² = 1 and *ab* + *ef* = 0. The third equation states that the vector (*b*, *f*) is orthogonal to the vector (*a*, *e*), which is nonzero because *a*² + *e*² = 1; hence (*b*, *f*) = *k*(−*e*, *a*) for a constant *k*. The second equation then gives *k*²(*e*² + *a*²) = *k*² = 1. Hence *e* = −*kb* and *f* = *ka* with *k* = ±1, and *a*² + *b*² = *a*² + *e*² = 1. The origin *x*′ = 0 moves along *x* = *vt*, where *x*₄ = i*ct*: 0 = *a* *vt* + *b* i*ct* for all *t*, so *b* = −*av*/(i*c*) = i*av*/*c*. Then *a*² + *b*² = *a*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²) = 1, so *a* = ±*γ*, and *a* = *γ* because *x*′ increases with *x*. Hence *b* = i*γv*/*c* and *x*′ = *γx* + (i*γv*/*c*)(i*ct*) = *γ*(*x* − *vt*). Further *x*₄′ = *k*(−*bx* + *ax*₄) = *k*(−i*γvx*/*c* + i*γct*) = *k* i*cγ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²), so *t*′ = *kγ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²), and *k* = +1 because *t*′ increases with *t*. The coefficients *a* = *γ* and *b* = i*γv*/*c* are those of a rotation of the (*x*, *x*₄) plane through the angle *θ* with cos *θ* = *γ*, sin *θ* = i*γv*/*c*, as in Appendix II.A. □

**Theorem II.8.1.8 (Length contraction; equation (XIII) of [120]).** A rod at rest along the *x*′-axis of the second system of Theorem II.8.1.7, of length *L*₀ there, has length *L* = *L*₀/*γ* in the first system.

*Proof.* The ends of the rod are at *x*′ = 0 and *x*′ = *L*₀ for all *t*′. Its length in the first system is the difference *Δx* of the positions of its ends at one time *t*. By Theorem II.8.1.7, *x*′ = *γ*(*x* − *vt*) for each end at that *t*, so *L*₀ = *γ* *Δx* and *L* = *Δx* = *L*₀/*γ*. □

**Theorem II.8.1.9 (Constancy of the velocity of light and invariance of the McGucken Sphere).** A signal that moves with speed *c* in one inertial coordinate system moves with speed *c* in every inertial coordinate system. The McGucken Spheres of an event *p*, the spheres of radius *c*(*t* − *t*₀) about *p* at the times *t* > *t*₀, are in every inertial coordinate system with *t*′ increasing with *t* the spheres of radius *c*(*t*′ − *t*₀′) about *p* at the times *t*′ > *t*₀′.

*Proof.* A signal of speed *c* satisfies |*d***x**| = *c* *dt*, that is *ds*² = 0. By Lemma II.8.1.3, the interval *ds*² is the same in every inertial coordinate system, so *d***x**′² − *c*²*dt*′² = 0 and |*d***x**′/*dt*′| = *c*. For the second statement, an event *q* lies on the McGucken Sphere of *p* exactly when |**x**_q − **x**_p|² − *c*²(*t*_q − *t*_p)² = 0 with *t*_q > *t*_p. The left side is the quadratic form *η* evaluated on the difference of the coordinates of *q* and *p*, which is preserved by *x*′ = *Λx* + *a* because *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*. It remains to show *t*′_q > *t*′_p. Write *n* for the difference of the coordinates of *q* and *p*, so *n*⁰ = |**n**| > 0, and let *λ*₀ = *Λ*⁰₀ and **λ** = (*Λ*⁰₁, *Λ*⁰₂, *Λ*⁰₃). The inverse of *Λ* is *η*⁻¹*Λ*ᵀ*η*, so *Λη*⁻¹*Λ*ᵀ = *η*⁻¹, whose 00 component reads *λ*₀² = 1 + |**λ**|²; with *t*′ increasing with *t*, *λ*₀ > |**λ**|. Then (*Λn*)⁰ = *λ*₀*n*⁰ + **λ**·**n** ≥ (*λ*₀ − |**λ**|) *n*⁰ > 0. Hence |**x**′_q − **x**′_p| = *c*(*t*′_q − *t*′_p). □

**Theorem II.8.1.10 (Energy, momentum and *E*₀ = *mc*²; equation (XV) of [120]).** For the free particle of mass *m* with the action *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ* of Proposition II.8.7.1: (i) the momentum is **p** = *γm***v** and the conserved energy is *E* = *γmc*²; (ii) the four-momentum *m*(**u**, *u*₄) has fourth component *p*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*; (iii) the relation *E*² = |**p**|²*c*² + *m*²*c*⁴ holds; (iv) at rest, *E*₀ = *mc*².

*Proof.* (i) By Theorem II.8.1.5 the action is ∫ *L* *dt* with *L* = −*mc*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(1/2). The momentum is **p** = ∂*L*/∂**v** = *m***v**(1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(−1/2) = *γm***v**. The energy is *E* = **p**·**v** − *L* = *γmv*² + *mc*²/*γ* = *γm*(*v*² + *c*² − *v*²) = *γmc*². It is conserved: *dE*/*dt* = (*d***p**/*dt*)·**v** + **p**·(*d***v**/*dt*) − (∂*L*/∂**v**)·(*d***v**/*dt*) = (*d***p**/*dt*)·**v**, and the Euler–Lagrange equation *d***p**/*dt* = ∂*L*/∂**x** = 0 gives *dE*/*dt* = 0. (ii) By Theorem II.8.1.6(i), *m***u** = *γm***v** = **p** and *mu*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*. (iii) By Theorem II.8.1.6(ii), *p*₄² + |**p**|² = *m*²(*u*₄² + |**u**|²) = −*m*²*c*², that is −*E*²/*c*² + |**p**|² = −*m*²*c*². (iv) At *v* = 0, *γ* = 1 and *E* = *mc*². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Einstein’s two postulates.** Einstein’s 1905 derivation took the principle of relativity and the constancy of the velocity of light as two postulates. Here the first is Definition II.8.1.2, the statement that the Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* holds from every event and refers to no state of motion, and the second is Theorem II.8.1.9. The rest energy *E*₀ = *mc*² of Theorem II.8.1.10 is the energy of a body at rest in space, whose whole budget *c* of Theorem II.8.1.6 lies in the advance of *x*₄.

**II.8.2 General Relativity.** The McGucken-Invariance Lemma asserts that *dx*₄/*dt* is metric-independent: ∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν = 0. In the chart adapted to the foliation by spatial slices, where the invariant *x*₄-advance gives *g*₄₄ = 1 and *g*₄ᵢ = 0 (the synchronous, lapse-fixed gauge of [71, §1.4]), the Christoffel symbols carrying both lower indices in the *x*₄ direction vanish, Γ^λ₄₄ = ½*g*^λσ(2∂₄*g*_σ4 − ∂_σ*g*₄₄) = 0 (writing 4 as shorthand for *x*₄), and every remaining connection coefficient is built from the spatial metric *g*ᵢⱼ alone. The geodesic principle *S* = −*mc* ∫ √−*g*_μν ẋ^μẋ^ν *dλ* emerges as the variational extremum of proper-time arc length along *x*₄. The Einstein field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν follow from the factorisation of diffeomorphism invariance by the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (Theorem 9 of [71], reproduced in §II.9.2) and Lovelock’s 1971 uniqueness theorem (the unique divergence-free symmetric (0,2)-tensor in 4D constructed from the metric and its first two derivatives is *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν) with the coupling *κ* = 8π*G*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit. The McGucken Principle supplies what Lovelock requires: the 4-dimensional Lorentzian metric arena, generated by integration of the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Schwarzschild geometry, gravitational time dilation, redshift, light bending Δφ = 4*GM*/*c*²*b*, the Mercury perihelion precession Δφ = 6π*GM*/*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²), gravitational waves (transverse-traceless polarizations only), the FLRW cosmology, and Bekenstein–Hawking thermodynamics all follow as downstream theorems. Each of these is given an explicit theorem-chain derivation from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in McGucken [71, 71], with the joint GR–QM unification developed in [72] and the underlying Light–Time–Dimension framework in [84].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Schwarzschild Geometry and the Classical Tests: The Static Spherically Symmetric Solution, Gravitational Time Dilation and Redshift, the Orbit Equation, the Perihelion Advance and the Bending of Light, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. In the presence of mass the advance of *x*₄ keeps its invariant rate and the three spatial dimensions are curved; the field equations of §II.9.2 state how. For a single spherical mass they predict the metric of Theorem II.8.2.1, and from that metric the four classical tests follow. The statements follow GR Theorems 12–16 of [72]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Theorem II.8.2.1 (The static spherically symmetric vacuum solution; GR Theorem 12 of [72]).** Consider the metric *ds*² = −*A*(*r*) *c*²*dt*² + *B*(*r*) *dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²) with *A*, *B* > 0, and the field equations of §II.9.2 with *T*_μν = 0 and Λ = 0, which are *R*_μν = 0. (i) The non-zero connection coefficients are Γ^t_tr = *A*′/(2*A*), Γ^r_tt = *c*²*A*′/(2*B*), Γ^r_rr = *B*′/(2*B*), Γ^r_θθ = −*r*/*B*, Γ^r_φφ = −*r* sin²*θ*/*B*, Γ^θ_rθ = Γ^φ_rφ = 1/*r*, Γ^θ_φφ = −sin *θ* cos *θ*, Γ^φ_θφ = cos *θ*/sin *θ*. (ii) The Ricci tensor is diagonal, with *R*_tt = *c*²[*A*″/(2*B*) − (*A*′/(4*B*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*) + *A*′/(*rB*)], *R*_rr = −*A*″/(2*A*) + (*A*′/(4*A*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*) + *B*′/(*rB*), *R*_θθ = 1 − 1/*B* − (*r*/(2*B*))(*A*′/*A* − *B*′/*B*), and *R*_φφ = sin²*θ* *R*_θθ. (iii) *R*_μν = 0 holds exactly when, after a constant rescaling of *t*, *A* = 1 − *r*_s/*r* and *B* = 1/*A* for a constant *r*_s. (iv) The motion of a slow body far from the centre is the Newtonian motion in the potential −*GM*/*r* exactly when *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*².

*Proof.* (i) and (ii) follow from Γ^λ_μν = ½ *g*^λσ(∂_μ *g*_σν + ∂_ν *g*_σμ − ∂_σ *g*_μν) and *R*_μν = ∂_λ Γ^λ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^λ_μλ + Γ^λ_λσ Γ^σ_μν − Γ^λ_νσ Γ^σ_μλ by direct substitution of the diagonal metric. (iii) From (ii), *R*_tt/(*c*²*A*) + *R*_rr/*B* = (1/(*rB*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*). If *R*_μν = 0 this vanishes, so (ln(*AB*))′ = 0 and *AB* is a constant *K*; replacing *t* by *t*/√*K* makes *AB* = 1. Then *B*′/*B* = −*A*′/*A*, and *R*_θθ = 1 − *A* − *rA*′ = 1 − (*rA*)′. Its vanishing gives *rA* = *r* − *r*_s with a constant *r*_s, that is *A* = 1 − *r*_s/*r*. Conversely, with *B* = 1/*A* the formula for *R*_tt becomes (*c*²*A*/(2*r*²))(*r*²*A*′)′, and *r*²*A*′ = *r*_s is constant, so *R*_tt = 0; then *R*_rr = 0 by the identity at the start of (iii), and *R*_θθ = *R*_φφ = 0 by construction. (iv) The action of a body is *S* = −*mc* ∫ (−*g*_μν *dx*^μ *dx*^ν)^(1/2) = ∫ *L* *dt* with *L* = −*mc*² (*A* − *w*²/*c*²)^(1/2), where *w*² = (*dr*/*dt*)²/*A* + *r*²((*dθ*/*dt*)² + sin²*θ* (*dφ*/*dt*)²). For *r*_s/*r* and *w*²/*c*² small, (1 − *r*_s/*r* − *w*²/*c*²)^(1/2) = 1 − *r*_s/(2*r*) − *w*²/(2*c*²) to first order and *w*² = *v*² to the same order, so *L* = −*mc*² + *mv*²/2 + *mc*²*r*_s/(2*r*). This is the Newtonian Lagrangian with potential energy −*mc*²*r*_s/(2*r*), which equals −*GMm*/*r* exactly when *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*². □

**Theorem II.8.2.2 (Gravitational time dilation and redshift; GR Theorems 13 and 14 of [72]).** In the metric of Theorem II.8.2.1: (i) a clock at rest at radius *r* advances by *dτ* = *A*(*r*)^(1/2) *dt*; (ii) light emitted with frequency *ν*_e by a source at rest at *r*_e is received by an observer at rest at *r*_o with frequency *ν*_o = *ν*_e (*A*(*r*_e)/*A*(*r*_o))^(1/2).

*Proof.* (i) For a clock at rest *dr* = *dθ* = *dφ* = 0, so *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*² = *A* *c*²*dt*². (ii) The metric does not depend on *t*, so if one signal follows the path **x**(*t*) from emitter to receiver, the path **x**(*t* − Δ*t*) shifted in *t* is also a signal path. Two successive wave crests therefore leave and arrive with the same separation Δ*t* in coordinate time. By (i) the proper period at the emitter is *A*(*r*_e)^(1/2) Δ*t* and at the receiver *A*(*r*_o)^(1/2) Δ*t*; the frequencies are the reciprocals. □

**Theorem II.8.2.3 (The orbit equation).** For motion in the plane *θ* = *π*/2 in the metric of Theorem II.8.2.1, let *u* = 1/*r* as a function of *φ*. (i) Along a geodesic, parametrized by proper time for a body and by an affine parameter for light, *A* *dt*/*dλ* = *e* and *r*² *dφ*/*dλ* = *ℓ* are constant. (ii) For a body, *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = *GM*/*ℓ*² + 3*GMu*²/*c*². (iii) For light, *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = 3*GMu*²/*c*².

*Proof.* (i) The geodesics are the extremals of ∫ ½ *g*_μν (*dx*^μ/*dλ*)(*dx*^ν/*dλ*) *dλ*. The integrand does not contain *t* or *φ*, so the Euler–Lagrange equations give the constancy of −*Ac*² *dt*/*dλ* and of *r*² sin²*θ* *dφ*/*dλ*; the equation for *θ* is satisfied by *θ* = *π*/2. (ii) and (iii) Along the geodesic *g*_μν (*dx*^μ/*dλ*)(*dx*^ν/*dλ*) = −*κc*², with *κ* = 1 for a body and *κ* = 0 for light. With (i) this reads −*c*²*e*²/*A* + (*dr*/*dλ*)²/*A* + *ℓ*²/*r*² = −*κc*², so (*dr*/*dλ*)² = *c*²*e*² − *A*(*κc*² + *ℓ*²*u*²). Since *dr*/*dλ* = (*dr*/*dφ*)(*ℓ*/*r*²) = −*ℓ* *du*/*dφ*, this is *ℓ*²(*du*/*dφ*)² = *c*²*e*² − (1 − *r*_s*u*)(*κc*² + *ℓ*²*u*²). Differentiating with respect to *φ* and dividing by 2*ℓ*² *du*/*dφ* gives *d*²*u*/*dφ*² = *κc*²*r*_s/(2*ℓ*²) + (*r*_s/2)*u*² − *u* + *r*_s*u*², that is *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = *κc*²*r*_s/(2*ℓ*²) + (3/2)*r*_s*u*². With *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*² this is (ii) for *κ* = 1 and (iii) for *κ* = 0. □

**Theorem II.8.2.4 (Perihelion advance; GR Theorem 16 of [72]).** To first order in *GM*/(*c*²*r*), a bound orbit of semi-major axis *a* and eccentricity *e* advances its perihelion by Δ*φ* = 6*πGM*/(*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²)) per revolution.

*Proof.* Without the term 3*GMu*²/*c*² the solution of Theorem II.8.2.3(ii) is the Kepler ellipse *u*₀ = (*GM*/*ℓ*²)(1 + *e* cos *φ*), for which *a*(1 − *e*²) = *ℓ*²/(*GM*). Put *u* = *u*₀ + *u*₁ with *u*₁ of first order. Then *u*₁″ + *u*₁ = 3*GMu*₀²/*c*² = (3*G*³*M*³/(*c*²*ℓ*⁴))(1 + 2*e* cos *φ* + *e*² cos²*φ*). The constant and the cos²*φ* terms produce bounded periodic terms in *u*₁. The term 2*e* cos *φ* produces *e* *φ* sin *φ*, since (*φ* sin *φ*)″ + *φ* sin *φ* = 2 cos *φ*. Hence, apart from bounded periodic terms, *u* = (*GM*/*ℓ*²)[1 + *e* cos *φ* + *δ* *e* *φ* sin *φ*] with *δ* = 3*G*²*M*²/(*c*²*ℓ*²), and to first order in *δ* this is (*GM*/*ℓ*²)[1 + *e* cos((1 − *δ*)*φ*)]. Successive perihelia occur when (1 − *δ*)*φ* increases by 2*π*, that is when *φ* increases by 2*π*(1 + *δ*) to first order. The advance per revolution is 2*πδ* = 6*πG*²*M*²/(*c*²*ℓ*²) = 6*πGM*/(*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²)). □

**Theorem II.8.2.5 (Bending of light; GR Theorem 15 of [72]).** To first order in *GM*/(*c*²*b*), a light ray passing a mass *M* with impact parameter *b* is deflected through the angle 4*GM*/(*c*²*b*).

*Proof.* Without the right side the solution of Theorem II.8.2.3(iii) is the straight line *u*₀ = sin *φ*/*b*. Put *u* = *u*₀ + *u*₁. Then *u*₁″ + *u*₁ = (3*GM*/(*c*²*b*²)) sin²*φ*, and a solution is *u*₁ = (*GM*/(*c*²*b*²))(1 + cos²*φ*), since (1 + cos²*φ*)″ + (1 + cos²*φ*) = −2 cos 2*φ* + 1 + cos²*φ* = 3 sin²*φ*. The ray is at infinity where *u* = 0. Near *φ* = 0, with *φ* = −*α* small, *u* = −*α*/*b* + 2*GM*/(*c*²*b*²) to first order, which vanishes at *α* = 2*GM*/(*c*²*b*). By the symmetry of the solution under *φ* → *π* − *φ* the other end is at *φ* = *π* + *α*. The two directions differ from a straight line by 2*α* = 4*GM*/(*c*²*b*). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the classical tests.** In Theorem II.8.2.2 the clock rate *A*^(1/2) is the factor by which the proper time of a clock at rest in the curved spatial geometry differs from the coordinate time. In Theorems II.8.2.4 and II.8.2.5 the term 3*GMu*²/*c*² of the orbit equation is the contribution of the spatial curvature *B* = 1/*A*; without it the orbit equation is Newton’s. The Schwarzschild metric used in Theorem II.8.6.12 is that of Theorem II.8.2.1.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Gravitational Waves and of the Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker Cosmology, with Proofs.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. By the McGucken-Invariance Lemma the rate of *x*₄ is unchanged by gravitation, and in the chart of §II.8.2 the metric has *g*₀₀ = −1 and *g*_{0j} = 0 with *x*⁰ = *ct*: mass curves and stretches *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃. For weak fields the Principle therefore predicts waves that distort the three spatial dimensions alone, transverse to their direction of travel, moving at the rate *c* of the expansion of *x*₄ (Theorem II.8.2.6). For a uniform universe it predicts a spatial scale factor *a*(*t*) governed by the Friedmann equations, with the coordinate *t* of the Principle as cosmic time (Theorem II.8.2.7). These are GR Theorems 19 and 20 of [72].

**Theorem II.8.2.6 (Linearized field equations and gravitational waves; GR Theorem 19 of [72]).** Let *g*_μν = *η*_μν + *s* *h*_μν with *h*_μν a smooth symmetric field, and denote by a superscript (1) the coefficient of *s* in the expansion in powers of *s*. Write *h* = *η*^μν*h*_μν, □ = *η*^μν∂_μ∂_ν, and *H*_μν = *h*_μν − ½*η*_μν*h*, the trace-reversed field. (i) The Ricci tensor is *R*_μν^(1) = ½(∂^ρ∂_μ*h*_ρν + ∂^ρ∂_ν*h*_ρμ − ∂_μ∂_ν*h* − □*h*_μν) and *R*^(1) = ∂^ρ∂^σ*h*_ρσ − □*h*. (ii) The change of coordinates *x*^μ → *x*^μ + *s* *ξ*^μ replaces *h*_μν by *h*_μν − ∂_μ*ξ*_ν − ∂_ν*ξ*_μ and leaves *R*_μν^(1) unchanged. (iii) The linearized Einstein tensor is *G*_μν^(1) = −½□*H*_μν + ½(∂_μ∂^ρ*H*_ρν + ∂_ν∂^ρ*H*_ρμ − *η*_μν∂^ρ∂^σ*H*_ρσ). Hence, when ∂^ρ*H*_ρν = 0, the field equations with Λ = 0 and a matter tensor *s* *T*_μν are, to first order, □*H*_μν = −(16π*G*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν. (iv) In the chart of the McGucken-Invariance Lemma, *h*_00 = 0 and *h*_{0j} = 0. Let *h*_jk be the real part of *ε*_jk exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)) with *ω* ≠ 0 and a constant symmetric matrix *ε*_jk ≠ 0. Then *h* satisfies the vacuum equations *R*_μν^(1) = 0 if and only if *ε*_jj = 0, *k*_j*ε*_jk = 0 and *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|². For **k** along the third axis these matrices are the combinations of the two with *ε*₁₁ = −*ε*₂₂ = 1 and with *ε*₁₂ = *ε*₂₁ = 1, all other entries zero.

*Proof.* (i) To first order the connection coefficients are Γ^ρ_μν = ½*s*(∂_μ*h*^ρ_ν + ∂_ν*h*^ρ_μ − ∂^ρ*h*_μν), and the Ricci tensor ∂_ρ Γ^ρ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^ρ_μρ plus products of two connection coefficients has the first-order part ½(∂_ρ∂_μ*h*^ρ_ν + ∂_ρ∂_ν*h*^ρ_μ − □*h*_μν) − ½∂_ν∂_μ*h*, because Γ^ρ_μρ = ½*s*∂_μ*h*. Contracting with *η*^μν gives *R*^(1). (ii) The metric in the new coordinates is (∂*x*^α/∂*x*′^μ)(∂*x*^β/∂*x*′^ν)*g*_αβ, and ∂*x*^α/∂*x*′^μ = δ^α_μ − *s*∂_μ*ξ*^α to first order, which gives the stated change of *h*. Inserting ∂_μ*ξ*_ν + ∂_ν*ξ*_μ for *h*_μν in (i): the first two terms give □∂_μ*ξ*_ν + □∂_ν*ξ*_μ + 2∂_μ∂_ν∂^ρ*ξ*_ρ, the third gives −2∂_μ∂_ν∂^ρ*ξ*_ρ and the fourth −□∂_μ*ξ*_ν − □∂_ν*ξ*_μ; the sum is zero. (iii) Since *η*^μν*H*_μν = −*h*, *h*_μν = *H*_μν − ½*η*_μν*H* with *H* = *η*^μν*H*_μν. Inserting this in (i), the terms −½∂_μ∂_ν*H* − ½∂_μ∂_ν*H* from the first two terms cancel the term +∂_μ∂_ν*H* from the third, and *R*_μν^(1) = ½(∂_μ∂^ρ*H*_ρν + ∂_ν∂^ρ*H*_ρμ − □*H*_μν + ½*η*_μν□*H*). Its trace is *R*^(1) = ∂^ρ∂^σ*H*_ρσ + ½□*H*, and *G*_μν^(1) = *R*_μν^(1) − ½*η*_μν*R*^(1) is the stated expression. The field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν hold at order zero for *η* with Λ = 0 and no matter, and at first order they read *G*_μν^(1) = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν.

(iv) Since *G*_μν^(1) = 0 is equivalent to *R*_μν^(1) = 0 (take the trace), use (i) with ∂₀ = *c*⁻¹∂ₜ, *h*_{0μ} = 0 and *h* = *h*_jj. The component 00 is *R*_00^(1) = −½∂₀∂₀*h*_jj, which for the wave is (*ω*²/2*c*²) times the real part of *ε*_jj exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)); it vanishes exactly when *ε*_jj = 0. The components 0*j* are *R*_{0j}^(1) = ½∂₀(∂_k*h*_kj − ∂_j*h*_kk), which for the wave is the real part of (*ω*/2*c*)(*k*_k*ε*_kj − *k*_j*ε*_kk) times the exponential; with *ε*_kk = 0 it vanishes exactly when *k*_k*ε*_kj = 0. With these two conditions the first three terms of (i) vanish in the components *jk*, and *R*_jk^(1) = −½□*h*_jk, which is ½(|**k**|² − *ω*²/*c*²) times *h*_jk; it vanishes exactly when *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|². For **k** = (0, 0, *k*) the condition *k*_j*ε*_jk = 0 leaves the entries with indices 1, 2, and *ε*₁₁ + *ε*₂₂ = 0 leaves the two stated matrices. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.8.2.6.** In general relativity without the Principle the conditions *h*_00 = 0, *h*_{0j} = 0 are a choice of coordinates. Here they are the chart in which *x*₄ advances at its invariant rate, and part (iv) shows that in this chart the vacuum equations themselves make every wave transverse and traceless, with two polarizations, travelling at *c*. The wave is an oscillation of *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃; the rate of *x*₄ is unchanged by it.

**Theorem II.8.2.7 (Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker cosmology; GR Theorem 20 of [72]).** Consider the metric *ds*² = −*c*²*dt*² + *a*(*t*)²[*dr*²/(1 − *kr*²) + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²)] with *a*(*t*) > 0 and a constant *k*, write *γ*_jk for the metric in the square brackets, and denote *d*/*dt* by a prime. (i) The metric *γ*_jk has the Ricci tensor 2*k* *γ*_jk: the slices *t* = constant have the same curvature at every point and in every direction. (ii) The curvature is *R*_tt = −3*a*″/*a*, *R*_jk = (*aa*″ + 2*a*′² + 2*kc*²)*γ*_jk/*c*², the mixed components vanish, *R* = (6/*c*²)(*a*″/*a* + *a*′²/*a*² + *kc*²/*a*²), *G*_tt = 3(*a*′² + *kc*²)/*a*² and *G*_jk = −(2*aa*″ + *a*′² + *kc*²)*γ*_jk/*c*². (iii) For matter of density *ρ*(*t*) and pressure *p*(*t*) at rest in these coordinates, *T*_μν = (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²)*u*_μ*u*_ν + *p* *g*_μν with *u*^μ = (1, 0, 0, 0), the field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν are equivalent to the two Friedmann equations

(*a*′/*a*)² = (8π*G*/3)*ρ* − *kc*²/*a*² + Λ*c*²/3,

*a*″/*a* = −(4π*G*/3)(*ρ* + 3*p*/*c*²) + Λ*c*²/3.

(iv) They imply *ρ*′ = −3(*a*′/*a*)(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²). (v) For *k* = 0 and Λ = 0, with *a*(0) = 0 and *a*′ > 0: if *p* = 0 then *ρa*³ is constant, *a* is proportional to *t*^(2/3) and (*a*′/*a*)*t* = 2/3; if *p* = *ρc*²/3 then *ρa*⁴ is constant, *a* is proportional to *t*^(1/2) and (*a*′/*a*)*t* = 1/2. For *ρ* = 0, *p* = 0, *k* = 0 and Λ > 0, *a* is proportional to exp(±*Ht*) with *H* = *c*(Λ/3)^(1/2). (vi) On any interval of time on which (8π*G*/3)*ρ* + Λ*c*²/3 − *kc*²/*a*² > 0, *a*′ has one sign throughout.

*Proof.* (i), (ii) The connection coefficients that do not vanish are Γ^t_rr = *aa*′/(*c*²(1 − *kr*²)), Γ^t_θθ = *aa*′*r*²/*c*², Γ^t_φφ = *aa*′*r*² sin²*θ*/*c*², Γ^r_tr = Γ^θ_tθ = Γ^φ_tφ = *a*′/*a*, Γ^r_rr = *kr*/(1 − *kr*²), Γ^r_θθ = −*r*(1 − *kr*²), Γ^r_φφ = −*r*(1 − *kr*²) sin²*θ*, Γ^θ_rθ = Γ^φ_rφ = 1/*r*, Γ^θ_φφ = −sin*θ* cos*θ*, Γ^φ_θφ = cos*θ*/sin*θ*, and those obtained by exchanging the lower indices; each follows from Γ^λ_μν = ½*g*^λσ(∂_μ*g*_σν + ∂_ν*g*_σμ − ∂_σ*g*_μν) for the diagonal metric. Those with three spatial indices are the connection coefficients of *γ*_jk. Inserting them in *R*_μν = ∂_ρ Γ^ρ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^ρ_μρ + Γ^ρ_ρσ Γ^σ_μν − Γ^ρ_νσ Γ^σ_μρ gives for *γ*_jk the Ricci tensor 2*k* *γ*_jk and for the four-dimensional metric the stated components. For example, *R*_tt = −∂ₜ(3*a*′/*a*) − 3(*a*′/*a*)² = −3*a*″/*a*. The scalar is *R* = −*R*_tt/*c*² + *γ*^jk*R*_jk/*a*² with *γ*^jk*γ*_jk = 3, and *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½*g*_μν*R* with *g*_tt = −*c*², *g*_jk = *a*²*γ*_jk. (iii) Lowering the index, *u*_t = −*c*² and *u*_j = 0, so *T*_tt = (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²)*c*⁴ − *pc*² = *ρc*⁴ and *T*_jk = *pa*²*γ*_jk. The component *tt* of the field equations is 3(*a*′² + *kc*²)/*a*² − Λ*c*² = 8π*Gρ*, the first Friedmann equation. The components *jk* are −(2*aa*″ + *a*′² + *kc*²)/*c*² + Λ*a*² = 8π*Gpa*²/*c*⁴; replacing *a*′² + *kc*² by its value from the first equation gives the second, and conversely. The mixed components vanish on both sides.

(iv) Differentiating the first equation, 2(*a*′/*a*)(*a*″/*a* − *a*′²/*a*²) = (8π*G*/3)*ρ*′ + 2*kc*²*a*′/*a*³. By the two equations, *a*″/*a* − *a*′²/*a*² = −4π*G*(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²) + *kc*²/*a*². Inserting this and cancelling the terms in *k* gives (8π*G*/3)*ρ*′ = −8π*G*(*a*′/*a*)(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²). (v) For *p* = 0, (iv) gives *ρ*′/*ρ* = −3*a*′/*a*, so *ρa*³ = *C*; then *a*′² = (8π*GC*/3)/*a*, so *a*^(1/2)*a*′ is constant and *a*^(3/2) is proportional to *t*. For *p* = *ρc*²/3, *ρ*′/*ρ* = −4*a*′/*a*, so *ρa*⁴ = *C*; then *aa*′ is constant and *a*² is proportional to *t*. For a power *a* = *Bt*^n, (*a*′/*a*)*t* = *n*. In the third case (*a*′/*a*)² = Λ*c*²/3. (vi) By the first Friedmann equation *a*′² is the stated positive quantity times *a*², so *a*′ has no zero on the interval, and a continuous function without a zero on an interval has one sign. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.8.2.7.** The coordinate *t* of the metric is the *t* of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*: *g*_tt = −*c*² at every event, the invariant rate of *x*₄. The factor *a*(*t*) scales *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃ alone. That the slices have the form *γ*_jk is the statement of uniformity, part (i); the theorem that every three-dimensional metric with the same curvature at every point and in every direction has this form locally is an external theorem of Riemannian geometry and is not used in the proof.

**II.8.3 Quantum Mechanics.** The wave equation □*ψ* = 0 follows from Huygens’ principle applied to the spherically symmetric expansion of *x*₄. The de Broglie relation *p* = *h*/*λ* expresses the *x*₄-phase accumulated per unit of spatial motion; the Planck–Einstein relation *E* = *hν* expresses one quantum of action per *x*₄-cycle; the Compton frequency ω_C = *mc*²/ℏ expresses the rest-mass *x*₄-oscillation rate. From the Klein–Gordon equation (factored from the mass-shell condition *E*²/*c*² − |**p**|² = *m*²*c*²) the non-relativistic limit yields the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂*ψ*/∂*t* = *Ĥψ*. The Dirac equation arises as the Clifford-square-root of Klein–Gordon, with the matter orientation condition (an even-grade multivector in Cl(1,3) carrying matter *x*₄-orientation at Compton frequency) yielding spin-1/2 via 4π-periodicity of the spinor representation. The canonical commutator [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ follows by Stone’s theorem on the Hamiltonian side and by path-integral derivation on the Lagrangian side. The Born rule *P* = |*ψ*|² emerges as the unique phase-invariant quadratic measure on complex amplitudes—complex because of the i in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Tsirelson’s bound 2√2, the path integral ∫*𝒟x* exp(i*S*/ℏ) (as iterated McGucken Spheres), and second quantization with Pauli exclusion (via the same 4π-periodicity that produced spin) all follow. The full derivation of quantum mechanics from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*—wave equation, de Broglie, Planck–Einstein, Klein–Gordon, Schrödinger, Dirac, canonical commutator, and Born rule—is given as a theorem chain in McGucken [73], with the joint GR–QM unification in [72].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Quantum Mechanics: The Action Clause, the Compton Frequency, the Planck–Einstein and de Broglie Relations, the Klein–Gordon Equation, the Schrödinger Equation, the Canonical Commutator and the Uncertainty Relation, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For quantum mechanics the Principle predicts the following: because each wavelength of the expansion of *x*₄ carries the action *h*, a body whose action is *S* carries the phase *S*/ℏ, and the frequency, the wavelength, the wave equations and the commutator of quantum mechanics are properties of that phase. The statements below follow Proposition 2.2, Definition 2.2.bis.1, Proposition 2.2.bis.1, Theorem 5.1 and Theorem 9.0 of [129] and QM Theorems 2, 3, 5, 7 and 8 of [72]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**The action clause of the McGucken Principle (Proposition 2.2 of [129]).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* has two dimensional parameters. (i) The constant *c* is the rate of the expansion: |*dx*₄/*dt*| = *c*. (ii) The constant *h* is the action carried per oscillation of that expansion: the expansion is a wavefront whose wavelength sets the action, each oscillatory cycle of *x*₄-advance carrying one quantum of action *h* = 2*π*ℏ. A cycle is a phase advance of 2*π*, so the phase of a wavefront that has accumulated the action *S* is *S*/ℏ, and the amplitude it contributes is exp(i*S*/ℏ). The McGucken wavefunction (Definition 2.6 of [114], §II.9.3) is the sum of these amplitudes over paths, *ψ* = Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ); sums of such amplitudes are again amplitudes.

**Theorem II.8.3.1 (Compton frequency and the Compton cycle; Definition 2.2.bis.1, Proposition 2.2.bis.1 and Corollary 2.2.bis.1 of [129]; QM Theorem 5 of [72]).** For a body of rest mass *m* at rest in an inertial coordinate system: (i) its amplitude is *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) with the Compton angular frequency *ω*_C = *mc*²/ℏ; (ii) one cycle lasts Δ*t*_C = 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²); (iii) during one cycle *x*₄ advances by Δ*x*₄ = i*λ*_C, where *λ*_C = *h*/(*mc*) is the Compton wavelength; (iv) during one cycle the action accumulated has magnitude *h*, whatever the mass *m*.

*Proof.* (i) By Proposition II.8.7.1 the action of the body is *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ*, and at rest *τ* = *t* (Theorem II.8.1.5 with *v* = 0), so *S* = −*mc*²*t*. By the action clause the amplitude is exp(i*S*/ℏ) = exp(−i*mc*²*t*/ℏ) = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*). (ii) The phase *ω*_C *t* advances by 2*π* in the time 2*π*/*ω*_C = 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²). (iii) By the Principle *x*₄ advances at the rate i*c*, so in one cycle Δ*x*₄ = i*c* Δ*t*_C = 2*π*iℏ/(*mc*) = i*h*/(*mc*) = i*λ*_C. (iv) The magnitude |*S*| grows at the rate *mc*², so in one cycle it grows by *mc*² · 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²) = 2*π*ℏ = *h*; the factors *mc*² cancel, so the result holds for every *m*. □

**Theorem II.8.3.2 (Planck–Einstein relation *E* = *hν* and de Broglie relation *p* = *h*/*λ*; QM Theorems 2 and 3 of [72]).** Let a body of rest mass *m* move with velocity **v** in an inertial coordinate system (*t*, **x**), with energy *E* = *γmc*² and momentum **p** = *γm***v** (Theorem II.8.1.10). (i) Its amplitude is *ψ* = exp(i(**p**·**x** − *Et*)/ℏ). (ii) The angular frequency of this wave is *ω* = *E*/ℏ and its wave vector is **k** = **p**/ℏ; hence *E* = ℏ*ω* = *hν* and |**p**| = ℏ|**k**| = *h*/*λ*, with *ν* = *ω*/2*π* the frequency and *λ* = 2*π*/|**k**| the wavelength. (iii) The frequency and wave vector satisfy *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² + *ω*_C². (iv) The action of the straight worldline from the origin to the event (*t*, **x**), *S*(*t*, **x**) = −*mc* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(1/2), has gradient ∇*S* = **p** and time derivative ∂ₜ*S* = −*E*.

*Proof.* (i) Let (*t*′, **x**′) be the inertial coordinate system in which the body is at rest, with the *x*-axis along **v**. By Theorem II.8.3.1 the amplitude there is exp(−i*mc*²*t*′/ℏ). By Theorem II.8.1.7, *t*′ = *γ*(*t* − **v**·**x**/*c*²). Hence −*mc*²*t*′ = −*γmc*²*t* + *γm* **v**·**x** = **p**·**x** − *Et*, and the amplitude is exp(i(**p**·**x** − *Et*)/ℏ). (ii) A wave exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)) has angular frequency *ω* and wave vector **k**; comparison with (i) gives *ω* = *E*/ℏ and **k** = **p**/ℏ. (iii) By Theorem II.8.1.10(iii), *E*² = |**p**|²*c*² + *m*²*c*⁴; dividing by ℏ² gives *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² + (*mc*²/ℏ)². (iv) Along the straight worldline from the origin to (*t*, **x**) the velocity is **v** = **x**/*t* and the proper time is *τ* = *t*/*γ* = (*t*² − |**x**|²/*c*²)^(1/2), so *S* = −*mc*²*τ* = −*mc* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(1/2). Differentiating, ∇*S* = *mc* **x** (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(−1/2) = *m***x**/*τ* = *γm***v** = **p**, and ∂ₜ*S* = −*mc*·*c*²*t* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(−1/2) = −*mc*²*t*/*τ* = −*γmc*² = −*E*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of light.** For *m* → 0 at fixed **p**, part (iii) becomes *ω* = *c*|**k**| and the relations *E* = ℏ*ω*, **p** = ℏ**k** of part (ii) are unchanged, with *E* = |**p**|*c*. The Compton frequency is zero: the photon is at rest in *x*₄ (Theorem II.8.1.6), and its wave advances with the McGucken Sphere at speed *c*.

**Theorem II.8.3.3 (Klein–Gordon equation; Theorem 9.0 of [129]; QM Theorem 8 of [72]).** Write □ = ∇² − *c*⁻²∂ₜ² and *μ* = *mc*/ℏ. (i) Every amplitude of Theorem II.8.3.2 satisfies (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0. (ii) Every superposition *ψ*(*t*, **x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ω*(**k**)*t*)) *d*³*k*, with *a* a Schwartz function and *ω*(**k**) = *c* √(|**k**|² + *μ*²), satisfies (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0. (iii) Conversely, every solution of (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 that is a Schwartz function of **x** at each *t* is *ψ* = ∫ [*a*(**k**) exp(−i*ω*(**k**)*t*) + *b*(**k**) exp(i*ω*(**k**)*t*)] exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*. (iv) For *m* = 0 the equation is the wave equation □*ψ* = 0.

*Proof.* (i) For *ψ* = exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)), ∇²*ψ* = −|**k**|²*ψ* and ∂ₜ²*ψ* = −*ω*²*ψ*, so □*ψ* = (*ω*²/*c*² − |**k**|²)*ψ*. By Theorem II.8.3.2(iii), *ω*²/*c*² − |**k**|² = *ω*_C²/*c*² = *m*²*c*²/ℏ² = *μ*². (ii) For a Schwartz function *a* the integral and its derivatives of every order converge absolutely, so differentiation under the integral is permitted, and each integrand satisfies the equation by (i). (iii) Take the Fourier transform in **x**: *ψ*(*t*, **x**) = ∫ *A*(*t*, **k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*. The equation becomes ∂ₜ²*A* = −*c*²(|**k**|² + *μ*²)*A* = −*ω*(**k**)²*A* for each **k**, an ordinary differential equation in *t* whose solutions are *a*(**k**) exp(−i*ω*(**k**)*t*) + *b*(**k**) exp(i*ω*(**k**)*t*). (iv) Set *μ* = 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the Klein–Gordon equation.** The term *μ*² = *ω*_C²/*c*² is the square of the Compton frequency of Theorem II.8.3.1 divided by *c*²: the mass term of the equation is the rate at which the body’s phase advances as *x*₄ expands. The equation is of second order because the interval of Lemma II.8.1.1 is quadratic. In part (iii) the amplitudes exp(−i*ω*t) are those of Theorem II.8.3.2, with phase *S*/ℏ for the orientation +i*c* of the Principle; the amplitudes exp(+i*ω*t) are their complex conjugates.

**Theorem II.8.3.4 (Schrödinger equation; QM Theorem 7 of [72]; Theorem 9.1 of [129]).** Let *ψ* be a superposition of the form of Theorem II.8.3.3(ii) and put *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) *φ*. (i) Exactly, iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²) ∂ₜ²*φ*. (ii) Exactly, iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* − *Rφ*, where *R* multiplies the Fourier transform of *φ* by *r*(**k**) with 0 ≤ *r*(**k**) ≤ ℏ⁴|**k**|⁴/(8*m*³*c*²). (iii) In a potential energy *V*(**x**), entering through the covariant derivative of §II.9.4 as ∂ₜ → ∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ, the equation of part (i) becomes iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²)(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)²*φ*. The Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* is the equation that remains when the terms carrying the factor 1/*c*² are dropped.

*Proof.* (i) We have ∂ₜ²*ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*)(−*ω*_C²*φ* − 2i*ω*_C ∂ₜ*φ* + ∂ₜ²*φ*) and ∇²*ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) ∇²*φ*. Substituting in (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 and using *ω*_C²/*c*² = *μ*², the terms in *φ* without derivatives cancel, leaving ∇²*φ* + (2i*ω*_C/*c*²) ∂ₜ*φ* − *c*⁻²∂ₜ²*φ* = 0. Since 2*ω*_C/*c*² = 2*m*/ℏ, multiplying by ℏ²/2*m* gives iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²) ∂ₜ²*φ*. (ii) The Fourier transform of *φ* is *a*(**k**) exp(−i(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C)*t*), so iℏ ∂ₜ multiplies it by ℏ(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C). Put *x* = |**k**|²/*μ*². Then ℏ(*ω* − *ω*_C) = *mc*²((1 + *x*)^(1/2) − 1) and ℏ²|**k**|²/2*m* = *mc*² *x*/2, and for *x* ≥ 0, 0 ≤ 1 + *x*/2 − (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≤ *x*²/8. [Both bounds follow from (1 + *x*/2)² − (1 + *x*) = *x*²/4 ≥ 0: dividing by (1 + *x*/2) + (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≥ 2 gives the difference, which is at most *x*²/8.] Hence *r*(**k**) = ℏ²|**k**|²/2*m* − ℏ(*ω* − *ω*_C) satisfies 0 ≤ *r* ≤ *mc*² *x*²/8 = ℏ⁴|**k**|⁴/(8*m*³*c*²). (iii) Replacing ∂ₜ by ∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ in the equation (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 and repeating the computation of (i) with ∂ₜ*φ* replaced by (∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)*φ* gives iℏ(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²)(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)²*φ*, and iℏ · i*V*/ℏ = −*V*. □

**Theorem II.8.3.5 (Momentum operator and canonical commutator; Theorem 5.1 of [129]).** (i) There is exactly one linear vector operator *p̂* = (*p̂*₁, *p̂*₂, *p̂*₃) on the superpositions *f*(**x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*, *a* a Schwartz function, that multiplies each wave exp(i**k**·**x**) by its momentum ℏ**k** of Theorem II.8.3.2: it is *p̂* = −iℏ∇. (ii) With *q̂*_j the multiplication by *x*_j, [*q̂*_j, *p̂*_k] *f* = iℏ δ_jk *f* for every Schwartz function *f*.

*Proof.* (i) An operator that is linear and multiplies exp(i**k**·**x**) by ℏ**k** sends *f* to ∫ ℏ**k** *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*, which determines it on every superposition. Since −iℏ∇ exp(i**k**·**x**) = ℏ**k** exp(i**k**·**x**), and differentiation under the integral is permitted for Schwartz *a*, that integral equals −iℏ∇*f*. Every Schwartz function *f* is such a superposition, by Fourier inversion. (ii) By the product rule, *p̂*_k(*x*_j *f*) = −iℏ δ_jk *f* − iℏ *x*_j ∂_k *f*, and *x*_j *p̂*_k *f* = −iℏ *x*_j ∂_k *f*. Subtracting, *x*_j *p̂*_k *f* − *p̂*_k(*x*_j *f*) = iℏ δ_jk *f*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of iℏ.** In [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ the factor i is the i of the phase exp(i*S*/ℏ), which is the i of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, the mark of the perpendicularity of *x*₄ to the three spatial dimensions; the factor ℏ is the action per cycle of the action clause (Theorem 5.1 of [129]).

**Theorem II.8.3.6 (Uncertainty relation).** For a Schwartz function *f* with ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x* = 1, let ⟨*A*⟩ = ∫ *f*∗ *Af* *d*³*x*, and let *σ*_q² = ⟨(*q̂* − ⟨*q̂*⟩)²⟩ and *σ*_p² = ⟨(*p̂* − ⟨*p̂*⟩)²⟩ for *q̂* = *q̂*_j and *p̂* = *p̂*_j with the same index *j*. Then *σ*_q *σ*_p ≥ ℏ/2.

*Proof.* Put *A* = *q̂* − ⟨*q̂*⟩ and *B* = *p̂* − ⟨*p̂*⟩. Both are symmetric on Schwartz functions: for *q̂* because *x*_j is real, for *p̂* by integration by parts, so ⟨*q̂*⟩ and ⟨*p̂*⟩ are real and ∫ (*Ag*)∗ *h* = ∫ *g*∗ *Ah*, likewise for *B*. Constants commute with everything, so [*A*, *B*] = [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ by Theorem II.8.3.5. Then iℏ = ⟨[*A*, *B*]⟩ = ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf* − ∫ (*Bf*)∗ *Af* = 2i Im ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf*. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, ℏ = 2 |Im ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf*| ≤ 2 ‖*Af*‖ ‖*Bf*‖ = 2 *σ*_q *σ*_p, since ‖*Af*‖² = ∫ *f*∗ *A*²*f* = *σ*_q² and ‖*Bf*‖² = *σ*_p². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the 1900–1926 postulates.** Planck introduced *E* = *hν* in 1900 and de Broglie introduced *p* = *h*/*λ* in 1924 as postulates, and Schrödinger’s equation of 1926 was written down to fit them. Here *E* = *hν* and *p* = *h*/*λ* are Theorem II.8.3.2, proved from the action clause of the Principle and the Lorentz transformation of Theorem II.8.1.7; the Klein–Gordon and Schrödinger equations are Theorems II.8.3.3 and II.8.3.4; and the commutator and the uncertainty relation are Theorems II.8.3.5 and II.8.3.6.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Spin, the Sum over Paths, the Tsirelson Bound and Exclusion, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the four results below the Principle supplies the objects on which they are proved: the Dirac matrices of Theorem II.8.5.3, the amplitude exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause, and the Hilbert space of Theorem II.8.4.3. The statements follow QM Theorems 9, 13, 15 and 20 of [72].

**Theorem II.8.3.7 (Spin ½ and the 4*π* period; QM Theorem 9 of [72]).** Let *γ*^μ be matrices as in Theorem II.8.5.3, put *J* = *γ*¹*γ*² and *S*₃ = (iℏ/2)*J*, and let *R*(*θ*) = exp(−i*θS*₃/ℏ). (i) *J*² = −1 and *S*₃² = ℏ²/4, so the eigenvalues of *S*₃ are ±ℏ/2. (ii) *R*(*θ*) = cos(*θ*/2) + *J* sin(*θ*/2). (iii) *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*¹ cos *θ* + *γ*² sin *θ*, *R*(*θ*) *γ*² *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*² cos *θ* − *γ*¹ sin *θ*, and *R*(*θ*) commutes with *γ*⁰ and *γ*³: *R*(*θ*) acts on the Dirac matrices as the rotation through the angle *θ* in the (*x*₁, *x*₂) plane. (iv) *R*(2*π*) = −1 and *R*(4*π*) = 1.

*Proof.* (i) By the relation of Theorem II.8.5.3, *γ*¹*γ*² = −*γ*²*γ*¹ and (*γ*¹)² = (*γ*²)² = −1. Hence *J*² = *γ*¹*γ*²*γ*¹*γ*² = −(*γ*¹)²(*γ*²)² = −1, and *S*₃² = (iℏ/2)² *J*² = ℏ²/4. A matrix with *S*₃² = ℏ²/4 has eigenvalues among ±ℏ/2. (ii) −i*θS*₃/ℏ = (*θ*/2) *J*, and since *J*² = −1 the exponential series separates into cos(*θ*/2) + *J* sin(*θ*/2). (iii) *J* *γ*¹ = *γ*¹*γ*²*γ*¹ = −(*γ*¹)²*γ*² = *γ*², and *γ*¹ *J* = (*γ*¹)²*γ*² = −*γ*², so *J* *γ*¹ = −*γ*¹ *J*. Therefore *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*¹ (cos(*θ*/2) − *J* sin(*θ*/2)) = *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹, and *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *R*(*θ*)² *γ*¹ = (cos *θ* + *J* sin *θ*) *γ*¹ = *γ*¹ cos *θ* + *γ*² sin *θ*. In the same way *J* *γ*² = *γ*¹(*γ*²)² = −*γ*¹ gives the second formula. *γ*⁰ and *γ*³ each anticommute with *γ*¹ and with *γ*², hence commute with *J* and with *R*(*θ*). (iv) *R*(2*π*) = cos *π* + *J* sin *π* = −1, and *R*(4*π*) = cos 2*π* = 1. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of spin.** A rotation of the coordinates through *θ* carries a solution *ψ* of the Dirac equation of Theorem II.8.5.3 into *R*(*θ*)*ψ* evaluated at the rotated coordinates, by part (iii). A rotation through 2*π* returns the coordinates to themselves and multiplies *ψ* by −1; only a rotation through 4*π* returns *ψ*. The component of angular momentum carried by *ψ* itself has the values ±ℏ/2.

**Theorem II.8.3.8 (The free propagator as a sum over paths; QM Theorem 15 of [72]).** For *t* > 0 let *U*(*t*) be the free evolution of Theorem II.8.4.1, which multiplies the Fourier transform by exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*), and let *K*_t(**x**) = (*m*/(2*π*iℏ*t*))^(3/2) exp(i*m*|**x**|²/(2ℏ*t*)). (i) The exponent of *K*_t is i*S*/ℏ, where *S* = *m*|**x**|²/(2*t*) is the action ∫ (*mv*²/2) *dt* of the straight path from the origin to **x** in the time *t*. (ii) For a Schwartz function *φ*₀, (*U*(*t*)*φ*₀)(**x**) = ∫ *K*_t(**x** − **y**) *φ*₀(**y**) *d*³*y*. (iii) *U*(*t*) = *U*(*t*/*N*)^N for every *N*, so that (*U*(*t*)*φ*₀)(**x**) is the integral over **x**₀, …, **x**_(N−1), with **x**_N = **x**, of (*m*/(2*π*iℏΔ))^(3*N*/2) exp((i/ℏ) Σ_j *m*|**x**_(j+1) − **x**_j|²/(2Δ)) *φ*₀(**x**₀), Δ = *t*/*N*: a sum, over the broken-line paths through **x**₀, …, **x**_N, of exp(i*S*/ℏ) with *S* the action of the path.

*Proof.* (i) The straight path has constant velocity **x**/*t*, so ∫ (*mv*²/2) *dt* = *m*|**x**|²/(2*t*); this is the action of Proposition II.8.7.1 with the constant −*mc*² removed and terms of order *v*⁴/*c*² dropped. (ii) For *a* > 0 the function exp(−*a*|**x**|²/2) has Fourier transform, in the convention of Theorem II.8.3.3, (2*πa*)^(−3/2) exp(−|**k**|²/(2*a*)); both sides are analytic in *a* for Re *a* > 0 and continuous up to *a* = −i*m*/(ℏ*t*), where the identity states that *K*_t has Fourier transform (2*π*)⁻³ exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*). A convolution multiplies Fourier transforms, with the factor (2*π*)³ of the convention, so convolution with *K*_t multiplies the Fourier transform of *φ*₀ by exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*). (iii) exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*) = (exp(−iℏ|**k**|²Δ/2*m*))^N, so *U*(*t*) = *U*(Δ)^N; writing each factor *U*(Δ) as the convolution of (ii) gives the stated integral, in which the exponents add to (i/ℏ) times the sum of the actions of the *N* straight segments. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the path integral.** Each factor *K*_Δ is the amplitude exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause for one straight segment, and the composition of Theorem II.8.3.8(iii) is the composition of expansions: each point reached is the origin of the next. With a potential, the same formula with *S* replaced by the action ∫ (*mv*²/2 − *V*) *dt* of the broken-line path holds in the limit *N* → ∞ by the Trotter product formula [118, Vol. I].

**Theorem II.8.3.9 (CHSH inequality and Tsirelson bound; QM Theorem 13 of [72]).** (i) For numbers *a*₁, *a*₂, *b*₁, *b*₂ each equal to ±1, *a*₁*b*₁ + *a*₁*b*₂ + *a*₂*b*₁ − *a*₂*b*₂ = ±2; hence the mean of this expression over any probability distribution lies between −2 and 2. (ii) Let *A*₁, *A*₂, *B*₁, *B*₂ be self-adjoint operators on a Hilbert space with squares equal to 1 and with each *A*_j commuting with each *B*_k, and let *C* = *A*₁*B*₁ + *A*₁*B*₂ + *A*₂*B*₁ − *A*₂*B*₂. Then *C*² = 4 − [*A*₁, *A*₂][*B*₁, *B*₂], and |⟨*f*|*Cf*⟩| ≤ 2√2 for every unit vector *f*. (iii) The value 2√2 is attained.

*Proof.* (i) The expression is *a*₁(*b*₁ + *b*₂) + *a*₂(*b*₁ − *b*₂); one of *b*₁ + *b*₂, *b*₁ − *b*₂ is 0 and the other is ±2. (ii) Write *C* = *A*₁(*B*₁ + *B*₂) + *A*₂(*B*₁ − *B*₂). Using *A*_j² = *B*_k² = 1 and the commutation of the *A* with the *B*, *C*² = (*B*₁ + *B*₂)² + (*B*₁ − *B*₂)² + *A*₁*A*₂(*B*₁ + *B*₂)(*B*₁ − *B*₂) + *A*₂*A*₁(*B*₁ − *B*₂)(*B*₁ + *B*₂). The first two terms add to 4. (*B*₁ + *B*₂)(*B*₁ − *B*₂) = −[*B*₁, *B*₂] and (*B*₁ − *B*₂)(*B*₁ + *B*₂) = [*B*₁, *B*₂], so the last two terms add to −[*A*₁, *A*₂][*B*₁, *B*₂]. Each of *A*₁*A*₂, *A*₂*A*₁ has norm at most 1, so ‖[*A*₁, *A*₂]‖ ≤ 2, likewise for the *B*, and ‖*C*²‖ ≤ 4 + 4 = 8. *C* is self-adjoint, so ‖*C*‖² = ‖*C*²‖ ≤ 8 and |⟨*f*|*Cf*⟩| ≤ ‖*C*‖ ≤ 2√2. (iii) On ℂ² ⊗ ℂ² with the Pauli matrices *σ*_x, *σ*_z, take the unit vector *s* = (*e*₁ ⊗ *e*₂ − *e*₂ ⊗ *e*₁)/√2, for which (**σ** ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ **σ**)*s* = 0. Then for unit vectors **a**, **b**, ⟨*s*|(**a**·**σ** ⊗ **b**·**σ**)*s*⟩ = −⟨*s*|(1 ⊗ (**a**·**σ**)(**b**·**σ**))*s*⟩ = −**a**·**b**, because (**a**·**σ**)(**b**·**σ**) = **a**·**b** + i(**a** × **b**)·**σ** and ⟨*s*|(1 ⊗ **σ**)*s*⟩ = 0. With *A*₁ = *σ*_z ⊗ 1, *A*₂ = *σ*_x ⊗ 1, *B*₁ = −1 ⊗ (*σ*_z + *σ*_x)/√2, *B*₂ = 1 ⊗ (*σ*_x − *σ*_z)/√2, the four means are 1/√2, 1/√2, 1/√2 and −1/√2, and ⟨*s*|*Cs*⟩ = 4/√2 = 2√2. □

**Theorem II.8.3.10 (Exclusion for antisymmetric amplitudes; QM Theorem 20 of [72]).** Let Ψ(*ξ*₁, *ξ*₂) be an amplitude for two bodies, *ξ* standing for the position and the spin component, with Ψ(*ξ*₂, *ξ*₁) = −Ψ(*ξ*₁, *ξ*₂). Then Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*) = 0, and for one-body amplitudes *f*, *g* the antisymmetric combination *f*(*ξ*₁)*g*(*ξ*₂) − *g*(*ξ*₁)*f*(*ξ*₂) is identically zero when *f* = *g*.

*Proof.* Setting *ξ*₁ = *ξ*₂ = *ξ* in the hypothesis gives Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*) = −Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*). For *f* = *g* the two products are equal. □

That the amplitude of two identical bodies of spin ½ is antisymmetric is the spin–statistics theorem, an external theorem proved from the Wightman axioms in [116]. Its hypotheses are the six axioms listed in §II.5, which [129] states as theorems of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; the sign −1 of Theorem II.8.3.7(iv) under a rotation through 2*π* is the sign that the theorem relates to the sign under exchange.

**II.8.4 The Hilbert Space Itself.** The complex Hilbert space, which von Neumann had taken as primitive, is now *derived*. The i in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* supplies complex amplitudes ψ(*x*) ~ exp i(*k* · *x* − ω*t*). The McGucken Sphere Σ_M(τ) supplies linear superposition. The spherical measure on the McGucken Sphere supplies the inner product ⟨*ψ*|*φ*⟩ = ∫ *ψ*∗ *φ* *d*³*x* (Theorem 12.1 of [86]). Completion under the resulting norm yields *H* = *L*²(**R**³); the tensor/Fock construction yields multi-particle Hilbert space. The theorem is this (Theorem 6.1 of [129]): the Hilbert space of nonrelativistic single-particle quantum mechanics is the unique irreducible separable Hilbert-space representation of the Weyl form of the McGucken-derived canonical commutator algebra, *U*(*a*) *V*(*b*) = exp(−i(*a* · *b*)/ℏ) *V*(*b*) *U*(*a*), *a*, *b* ∈ **R**³, with iℏ supplied by Theorem 5.1 of [129] (i from *x*₄-perpendicularity; ℏ from the action-quantization clause of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, Proposition 2.2 of [129]). By the Stone–von Neumann uniqueness theorem (Stone 1930; von Neumann 1931; refs. 108 and 109 of [129]), this representation is unitarily equivalent to the Schrödinger representation on *L*²(**R**³, *d*³*x*), with the Weyl unitaries acting as (*U*(*a*)*ψ*)(*x*) = exp(i(*a* · *x*)/ℏ) *ψ*(*x*) and (*V*(*b*)*ψ*)(*x*) = *ψ*(*x* + *b*). The Geometric Channel route, from the overlap of McGucken-Sphere wavefronts and with no use of the Stone–von Neumann theorem, is Theorem 6.1.5 of [129]. The von Neumann arena is no longer primitive; it is downstream of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The explicit co-generation of the joint Space–Operator pair, of which the complex Hilbert space is the spherical specialization, is the subject of McGucken [88, 86, 87].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Hilbert Space: The Conserved Norm, the Inner Product and the Completion, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the Hilbert space the Principle predicts each of its three defining features: the amplitudes are complex because the phase factor is exp(i*S*/ℏ) with the i of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; they form a linear space because amplitudes add; and the norm is the total of the density |*ψ*|², which the expansion of *x*₄ conserves.

**Theorem II.8.4.1 (Conservation of the total of |*φ*|²; Theorem 9.2 of [129]).** (i) For the free evolution of Theorem II.8.3.4(ii), ∫ |*φ*(*t*, **x**)|² *d*³*x* is the same at every *t*. (ii) For a solution *φ* of the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* with real *V*, that is a Schwartz function of **x** at each *t*, ∫ |*φ*|² *d*³*x* is the same at every *t*.

*Proof.* (i) For *f*(**x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k* with *a* a Schwartz function, Plancherel’s identity is ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x* = (2*π*)³ ∫ |*a*|² *d*³*k*. The free evolution multiplies *a*(**k**) by exp(−i(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C)*t*), a number of modulus 1, so ∫ |*a*|² *d*³*k* is unchanged. (ii) Differentiating under the integral, (*d*/*dt*) ∫ |*φ*|² *d*³*x* = 2 Re ∫ *φ*∗ ∂ₜ*φ* *d*³*x* = (2/ℏ) Im ∫ *φ*∗ (−(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ*) *d*³*x*. Integrating by parts, ∫ *φ*∗ ∇²*φ* *d*³*x* = −∫ |∇*φ*|² *d*³*x*, which is real, and ∫ *V* |*φ*|² *d*³*x* is real. The imaginary part is zero. □

**Theorem II.8.4.2 (The inner product is determined by the conserved norm).** On the complex vector space of Schwartz functions on ℝ³ put *N*(*f*) = ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x*, the total of the Born density of §II.9.3. There is exactly one form ⟨*f*|*g*⟩, linear in *g* and conjugate-linear in *f*, with ⟨*f*|*f*⟩ = *N*(*f*) for all *f*: it is ⟨*f*|*g*⟩ = ∫ *f*∗ *g* *d*³*x*.

*Proof.* The form ∫ *f*∗ *g* *d*³*x* has the stated linearity and gives *N*(*f*) at *g* = *f*. For uniqueness, let ⟨·|·⟩ be any such form. Expanding, *N*(*f* + *g*) − *N*(*f* − *g*) = 2⟨*f*|*g*⟩ + 2⟨*g*|*f*⟩ and *N*(*f* + i*g*) − *N*(*f* − i*g*) = 2i⟨*f*|*g*⟩ − 2i⟨*g*|*f*⟩. Hence 4⟨*f*|*g*⟩ = [*N*(*f* + *g*) − *N*(*f* − *g*)] − i[*N*(*f* + i*g*) − *N*(*f* − i*g*)]: the form is given by *N*. □

**Theorem II.8.4.3 (The Hilbert space; Theorem 6.1 of [129]).** The completion of the Schwartz functions on ℝ³ in the norm ‖*f*‖ = *N*(*f*)^(1/2) is the complex separable Hilbert space *L*²(ℝ³, *d*³*x*). On it (i) the evolutions of Theorem II.8.4.1 extend to unitary operators, and (ii) the operators *q̂*_j and *p̂*_j of Theorem II.8.3.5 are symmetric on the Schwartz functions: ⟨*f*|*q̂*_j *g*⟩ = ⟨*q̂*_j *f*|*g*⟩ and ⟨*f*|*p̂*_j *g*⟩ = ⟨*p̂*_j *f*|*g*⟩.

*Proof.* The Schwartz functions are dense in *L*²(ℝ³), and *L*²(ℝ³) is complete (the Riesz–Fischer theorem) and separable [118, Vol. I]; a complete space in which a normed space is dense is its completion. (i) By Theorem II.8.4.1 each evolution preserves the norm on a dense subspace and maps it onto itself, the inverse being the evolution for the opposite time; a norm-preserving linear map of a dense subspace onto a dense subspace extends uniquely to a norm-preserving map of the whole space onto itself, which is a unitary operator. (ii) For *q̂*_j: ∫ *f*∗ *x*_j *g* = ∫ (*x*_j *f*)∗ *g* because *x*_j is real. For *p̂*_j: integrating by parts, ∫ *f*∗ (−iℏ ∂_j *g*) = ∫ (iℏ ∂_j *f*∗) *g* = ∫ (−iℏ ∂_j *f*)∗ *g*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of von Neumann’s arena.** Von Neumann’s 1932 axioms take the complex separable Hilbert space as given. Here it is constructed: the vectors are the amplitudes of Theorem II.8.3.2 and their superpositions, the norm is the quantity conserved in Theorem II.8.4.1, the inner product is fixed by that norm in Theorem II.8.4.2, and the space is the completion of Theorem II.8.4.3.

**II.8.5 The Operator Hierarchy.** Quantization of the McGucken operator yields *M̂* = iℏD_M = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄, and the quantum McGucken constraint *M̂*Ψ = 0 links energy and the fourth momentum, *Ĥ*Ψ = i*c* *p̂*₄Ψ (Definition 7.1 of [87]). Lorentzian projection (∂₄ = −i ∂ₜ/*c*) converts the Euclidean Δ₄ = ∂₄² + Δ into the d’Alembertian □_M = Δ − *c*⁻²∂ₜ², which is the □ of §II.2 (Definition 8.1 and Theorem 8.2 of [87]). The Clifford square-root yields the Dirac–McGucken operator 𝒟_M = iℏ*c* γ^μ∂_μ − *mc*², for which (iγ^μ∂_μ − *m*)(iγ^ν∂_ν + *m*) = □_M − *m*² in natural units, with the Clifford relation *γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −2*η*^μν (Theorem II.8.5.3; Definition 11.1 and Theorem 11.2 of [87]).
