Write *P*(*X*, *Y*) = ⟨*ΘX*, *Y*⟩ with Θ a linear map that is symmetric for the inner product of Definition II.8.7.5. Both forms are invariant, so Θ commutes with every map *X* → *gXg*⁻¹. Θ has a real eigenvalue *λ*, and its eigenspace *V* is carried into itself by every *X* → *gXg*⁻¹; taking *g* = exp(*sZ*) and differentiating at *s* = 0, *ZX* − *XZ* lies in *V* for every *X* in *V* and every *Z* in the Lie algebra. For U(1) the Lie algebra has dimension 1 and *V* is all of it. For SU(*N*) the Lie algebra is simple: a non-zero subspace *V* with this property is the whole Lie algebra. In both cases Θ = *λ* and *P*_ab = *λ* δ_ab, so Φ₂ = −(*λ*/2) *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν, which is the stated form with *α* = −*λ*/2.

The field equation. By Definition II.8.7.5, *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν = −tr(*F*_μν*F*^μν). A variation δ*A*_ν changes *F*_μν by ∇_μ(δ*A*_ν) − ∇_ν(δ*A*_μ), where ∇_μ*X* = ∂_μ*X* + *A*_μ*X* − *XA*_μ. So the variation of tr(*F*_μν*F*^μν) is 4 tr(*F*^μν∇_μ(δ*A*_ν)). Since the trace of a product is unchanged by a cyclic permutation, tr(*F*^μν(*A*_μδ*A*_ν − δ*A*_ν*A*_μ)) = −tr((*A*_μ*F*^μν − *F*^μν*A*_μ)δ*A*_ν), hence tr(*F*^μν∇_μ(δ*A*_ν)) = ∂_μ tr(*F*^μνδ*A*_ν) − tr((∇_μ*F*^μν)δ*A*_ν). The divergence does not contribute to the variation of the action for δ*A*_ν vanishing outside a bounded region, so the action is stationary exactly when ⟨∇_μ*F*^μν, δ*A*_ν⟩ integrates to zero for every such δ*A*_ν with values in the Lie algebra. ∇_μ*F*^μν has values in the Lie algebra and the inner product is positive, so ∇_μ*F*^μν = 0. For U(1) the matrices commute and the equation is ∂_μ*F*^μν = 0, Maxwell’s equations without sources. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.8.7.8.** The connection *A*_μ records how the origin of the *x*₄-phase at one event is compared with the origin at a neighbouring event, and the field strength is the commutator of two covariant derivatives (Lemma II.8.7.6(ii)), the gauge-covariant form of the McGucken Operator *D*_M of Theorem II.8.5.4. Lemma II.8.7.6(iv) shows that local invariance leaves the field strength as the only quantity on which a Lagrangian of *A*_μ can depend, and the Lorentz transformations of §II.8.1 with parity leave the one combination *E*^a·*E*^a − *B*^a·*B*^a. Three inputs are hypotheses of the theorem and are stated as such: the local form of the invariance, hypothesis (a), whose ground is Proposition 9.3.2 and Theorem 123 of [133]; the degree condition (d); and parity invariance (e) of the term containing *A*_μ alone. The simplicity of the Lie algebra of SU(*N*) is a theorem of the theory of Lie algebras and is used as an external result.

**Theorem II.8.7.9 (Four-fold uniqueness of the McGucken Lagrangian; Theorem VI.1 of [77]).** The McGucken Lagrangian, the sum of the free-particle kinetic sector −*mc* √(−∂_μ *x*₄ ∂^μ *x*₄), the Dirac matter sector, the Yang–Mills gauge sector −¼ *F*_μν *F*^μν and the Einstein–Hilbert gravitational sector (*c*⁴/16*πG*) *R*, is the unique Lorentz-invariant, reparametrization-invariant, first-order local Lagrangian consistent with the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Each sector is forced by a specific uniqueness subtheorem: (a) the free-particle kinetic term by Proposition IV.1 of [77] (Proposition II.8.7.1 above); (b) the Dirac matter sector by Theorem 8.7.4 above, which is Proposition V.1 of [77] (the unique first-order Lagrangian on Clifford-algebra fields); (c) the Yang–Mills gauge sector by Theorem 8.7.8 above, which is Proposition VI.2 of [77] (the unique Lagrangian consistent with local *x*₄-phase invariance for a given compact Lie group), with the specific Standard Model gauge group U(1) × SU(2) × SU(3) an empirical input in [77], and the subject of the derivation in [133]; (d) the Einstein–Hilbert gravitational sector by Proposition VI.3 of [77] (Lovelock’s theorem). Each uniqueness subtheorem reduces to the McGucken Principle combined with a specific minimal consistency requirement — Lorentz invariance for (a) and (b), local gauge invariance for (c), diffeomorphism invariance for (d).

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Hilbert’s 1915 action.** Hilbert’s variational principle of §II.3, *S* = ∫ (*R* + *L*_matter) √−*g* *d*⁴*x*, took the spacetime arena and the form of the Lagrangian as given. In the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework the arena is generated by Theorem II.4.1, the gravitational sector is sector (d) of Theorem II.8.7.9 with Lovelock’s hypotheses derived in §II.9.2, the gauge sector is sector (c) with the connection of §II.9.4, and the free-particle sector is Proposition II.8.7.1, the accumulated *x*₄-advance of the worldline.

### II.9. Explicit Derivations: Lorentzian Signature, Lovelock Closure, the Born Rule, the Gauge Connection, and the Strict Second Law

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

The derivation lattice of §II.8 sketched the chains by which the canonical content of twentieth-century physics descends from the McGucken Principle. To meet Hilbert’s requirement that “the correctness of the solution” be establishable “by means of a finite number of steps,” we now exhibit five of the most foundationally sensitive derivations in full. The Lorentzian signature is what distinguishes spacetime from a Riemannian 4-manifold; the Lovelock closure is what fixes the Einstein tensor uniquely; the Born rule is what identifies |*ψ*|² as a probability; §II.9.4 and §II.9.5 add the gauge connection and the strict second law with the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy. Each is derived from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* below in a self-contained calculation.

### II.9.1 Lorentzian Signature from the Imaginary Unit

**Claim.** The metric induced by the McGucken Principle on **R**⁴ with coordinates (*t*, **x**) has signature (−, +, +, +).

**Derivation.** Take the Euclidean line element on the four coordinates (*x*₄, *x*¹, *x*², *x*³):

*dσ*² = *dx*₄² + (*dx*¹)² + (*dx*²)² + (*dx*³)².

This is unambiguously positive-definite: it is the standard inner product on **R**⁴. Now apply the McGucken Principle. Differentiating *x*₄ = i*ct* gives *dx*₄ = i*c* *dt*, and therefore *dx*₄² = (i*c*)²*dt*² = −*c*²*dt*². Substituting:

*ds*² = −*c*²*dt*² + *d***x**².

This is the Minkowski line element with signature (−, +, +, +). The negative sign in front of *c*²*dt*² is not a separately postulated convention; it is a calculated consequence of *i*² = −1 applied to the rate *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The light cone, the causal partial order, and the distinction between timelike, null, and spacelike vectors all follow from this single sign. □

**Remark.** The same derivation, with *i* replaced by 1, would have produced a Riemannian (Euclidean) 4-manifold with no light cones. This is what Wick rotation is: the formal substitution *t* → −*iτ* that converts a Minkowskian path-integral into a Euclidean one. From the McGucken viewpoint Wick rotation is not a trick but the inverse of the McGucken substitution: *x*₄ = i*ct* is what gives ordinary spacetime its Lorentzian character; un-doing the *i* returns it to Euclidean form. This signature derivation is part of the original Light–Time–Dimension framework [84] and is used in the GR derivation chain [71, 71] and the GR–QM unification [72]. It is Lemma 1 of [74], where the four-coordinate inner product *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄² is the labelled Hypothesis A3 and the signature (3,1) of the resulting form follows by Sylvester’s law of inertia.

### II.9.2 Lovelock’s Theorem and the Closure of the Field Equations

**Theorem (Lovelock 1971)** [117]. Let *M* be a 4-dimensional smooth Lorentzian manifold and let *A*_μν(*g*) be a symmetric (0,2)-tensor that is (i) constructed locally from the metric *g*_μν and its first two derivatives, (ii) divergence-free: ∇^μ*A*_μν = 0. Then *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν for some constants *α*, *β*, where *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½ *g*_μν*R* is the Einstein tensor.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Uniqueness of the Einstein Tensor: Proof for Tensors Linear in the Second Derivatives of the Metric.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the field equations the Principle supplies the four-dimensional Lorentzian metric (§II.9.1) and, through the McGucken-Invariance Lemma below, the vanishing divergence of the matter tensor. The theorem that turns these into the Einstein tensor is proved here in full under the hypothesis that the tensor is linear in the second derivatives of the metric; this form is due to Vermeil [135], Cartan [136] and Weyl. Lovelock’s theorem [117], stated above, removes that hypothesis in four dimensions and is used as an external theorem. Throughout, *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1), indices are raised with the inverse metric, and *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½*g*_μν*R*.

**Lemma II.9.2.1 (Coordinates adapted to a point; the curvature there; the contracted Bianchi identity).** Let *g*_μν be a smooth Lorentzian metric near a point *x*₀ of a four-dimensional manifold. (i) There are coordinates *y* with *y*(*x*₀) = 0 in which *g*_μν(0) = *η*_μν and ∂_ρ*g*_μν(0) = 0. Call them adapted to *x*₀, and write *h*_{μνρσ} = ∂_ρ∂_σ*g*_μν(0), the last two indices being those of the derivatives. (ii) In adapted coordinates the connection coefficients vanish at 0, and there *R*_μν = ½*η*^ρσ(*h*_{ρμνσ} + *h*_{ρνμσ} − *h*_{ρσμν} − *h*_{μνρσ}) and *R* = *η*^μν*η*^ρσ(*h*_{μρνσ} − *h*_{μνρσ}). (iii) If *c*^μ_{αβγ} are constants symmetric in *α*, *β*, *γ*, the coordinates *z*^μ = *y*^μ + (1/6)*c*^μ_{αβγ} *y*^α*y*^β*y*^γ are adapted to *x*₀, and in them *h*_{μνρσ} is replaced by *h*_{μνρσ} − *c*_{μνρσ} − *c*_{νμρσ}, where *c*_{μνρσ} = *η*_μλ*c*^λ_{νρσ}. (iv) For every metric, ∇^μ*R*_μν = ½∂_ν*R*, that is, ∇^μ*G*_μν = 0.

*Proof.* (i) The matrix *g*_μν(*x*₀) is symmetric with one negative and three positive eigenvalues, so by Sylvester’s law of inertia a linear change of coordinates brings it to *η*_μν. Let Γ^μ_αβ be the connection coefficients at the point in these coordinates and put *y*′^μ = *y*^μ + ½Γ^μ_αβ*y*^α*y*^β. The Jacobian matrix of this change is the identity at 0, so *g*(0) is unchanged, and the transformation law of the connection coefficients, new = transformed old minus the second derivatives ∂²*y*′^μ/∂*y*^α∂*y*^β, gives the new coefficients Γ^μ_αβ − Γ^μ_αβ = 0 at 0. Since ∂_ρ*g*_μν = *g*_μλ Γ^λ_νρ + *g*_νλ Γ^λ_μρ, the first derivatives vanish at 0. (ii) With the first index lowered, Γ_μνσ = ½(∂_σ*g*_μν + ∂_ν*g*_μσ − ∂_μ*g*_νσ), and Γ(0) = 0, so the curvature tensor at 0 is *R*_{μνρσ} = ∂_ρ Γ_μνσ − ∂_σ Γ_μνρ = ½(*h*_{μσνρ} + *h*_{νρμσ} − *h*_{μρνσ} − *h*_{νσμρ}). Contracting *μ* with *ρ* by *η* gives *R*_νσ, and contracting again gives *R*. (iii) The inverse change is *y*^μ = *z*^μ − (1/6)*c*^μ_{αβγ}*z*^α*z*^β*z*^γ plus terms of the fifth order, so ∂*y*^α/∂*z*^μ = δ^α_μ − ½*c*^α_{μρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ plus terms of the fourth order. The metric in the coordinates *z* is (∂*y*^α/∂*z*^μ)(∂*y*^β/∂*z*^ν)*g*_αβ = *g*_μν − ½*c*_{νμρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ − ½*c*_{μνρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ plus terms of the third order. Its value and first derivatives at 0 are unchanged and its second derivatives change as stated. (iv) In adapted coordinates the products of connection coefficients in the curvature tensor have vanishing first derivatives at 0, so there ∇_λ*R*^μ_{νρσ} = ∂_λ∂_ρ Γ^μ_νσ − ∂_λ∂_σ Γ^μ_νρ. The sum of this expression over the three cyclic orders of *λ*, *ρ*, *σ* is zero term by term. So ∇_λ*R*^μ_{νρσ} + ∇_ρ*R*^μ_{νσλ} + ∇_σ*R*^μ_{νλρ} = 0 at *x*₀, and, being an equation between tensors, in all coordinates at every point. Contracting *μ* with *ρ* gives ∇_λ*R*_νσ + ∇_μ*R*^μ_{νσλ} − ∇_σ*R*_νλ = 0; contracting *ν* with *λ* by the inverse metric and using the antisymmetry of the curvature tensor in each pair of indices gives 2∇^μ*R*_μσ − ∂_σ*R* = 0. □

**Theorem II.9.2.2 (The Einstein tensor is the only divergence-free tensor linear in the second derivatives; Vermeil 1917, Cartan 1922, Weyl 1922).** Let *A*_μν be a symmetric tensor field that, for every Lorentzian metric on a four-dimensional manifold and in every coordinate system, is given by one fixed function of the values at the point of *g*_αβ, ∂_ρ*g*_αβ and ∂_ρ∂_σ*g*_αβ, this function being the sum of a part linear in the second derivatives and a part free of them. If ∇^μ*A*_μν = 0 for every metric, then *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν with constants *α*, *β*.

*Proof.* Step 1. Fix a metric and a point *x*₀ and use adapted coordinates (Lemma II.9.2.1(i)). There *A*_μν = *L*_μν^{αβρσ} *h*_{αβρσ} + *N*_μν, where the numbers *L* and *N* are the values of the fixed function at *g* = *η*, ∂*g* = 0; they do not depend on the metric or the point. Step 2. A linear change of coordinates by a matrix Λ that preserves *η* keeps the coordinates adapted, transforms *h*_{αβρσ} as a tensor of rank four and *A*_μν as a tensor of rank two, and leaves *L*, *N* the same numbers. Hence *L* and *N* are tensors invariant under the full group of matrices preserving *η*. By the first main theorem of the theory of invariants for the orthogonal group of a non-degenerate quadratic form (Weyl [134], Chapter II), every such invariant tensor is a linear combination of products of the *η*^μν. So *N*_μν = *β* *η*_μν, and, taking into account the symmetry of *h*_{αβρσ} in *α*, *β* and in *ρ*, *σ* and of *A*_μν in *μ*, *ν*, the fifteen ways of pairing the six indices give five expressions: *T*₁ = *η*_μν*η*^αβ*η*^ρσ*h*_{αβρσ}, *T*₂ = *η*_μν*η*^αρ*η*^βσ*h*_{αβρσ}, *T*₃ = *η*^ρσ*h*_{μνρσ}, *T*₄ = *η*^αβ*h*_{αβμν}, *T*₅ = *η*^ρσ(*h*_{ρμνσ} + *h*_{ρνμσ}). Therefore *A*_μν = *a*₁*T*₁ + *a*₂*T*₂ + *a*₃*T*₃ + *a*₄*T*₄ + *a*₅*T*₅ + *β* *η*_μν with constants *a*₁, …, *a*₅.

Step 3. The change of coordinates of Lemma II.9.2.1(iii) has Jacobian matrix the identity at *x*₀, so it leaves the components *A*_μν(*x*₀) unchanged while it changes *h*. Take *c*_{μνρσ} = *p*_μ*q*_ν*q*_ρ*q*_σ with vectors *p*, *q*, and write *p*·*q* and *q*·*q* for the products formed with *η*. The change of *h*_{μνρσ} is −(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ)*q*_ρ*q*_σ, and the changes of the five expressions are: of *T*₁ and of *T*₂, −2(*p*·*q*)(*q*·*q*)*η*_μν; of *T*₃, −(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ); of *T*₄, −2(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν; of *T*₅, −(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ) − 2(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν. The change of *A*_μν, which must vanish, is −2(*a*₁ + *a*₂)(*p*·*q*)(*q*·*q*)*η*_μν − (*a*₃ + *a*₅)(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ) − 2(*a*₄ + *a*₅)(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν. With *q* the unit vector of the first space axis and *p* that of the second, *p*·*q* = 0 and *q*·*q* = 1, so *a*₃ + *a*₅ = 0. With *q* the sum of the unit vectors of the time axis and the first space axis, *q*·*q* = 0, and with *p* the unit vector of the first space axis, *p*·*q* = 1, so *a*₄ + *a*₅ = 0. With *p* = *q* the unit vector of the first space axis, the remaining term gives *a*₁ + *a*₂ = 0.

Step 4. Hence *A*_μν = *a*₅(*T*₅ − *T*₃ − *T*₄) + *a*₂(*T*₂ − *T*₁) + *β* *η*_μν, which by Lemma II.9.2.1(ii) is 2*a*₅ *R*_μν + *a*₂ *R* *g*_μν + *β* *g*_μν at *x*₀. Both sides are tensors, so the equation *A*_μν = *α* *R*_μν + *γ* *R* *g*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, with *α* = 2*a*₅ and *γ* = *a*₂, holds in all coordinates, at every point and for every metric, with the same constants. Step 5. By Lemma II.9.2.1(iv) and ∇_λ*g*_μν = 0, ∇^μ*A*_μν = (*α*/2 + *γ*) ∂_ν*R*. This vanishes for every metric. The metric of Theorem II.8.2.7 with *k* = 0 and *a*(*t*) = *t*^(2/3) has *R* = 4/(3*c*²*t*²), which is not constant, so *γ* = −*α*/2 and *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν. □

The hypotheses of Theorem II.9.2.2 are those of Lovelock’s theorem together with linearity in the second derivatives. For the field equations of §II.8.2 either theorem gives the same conclusion: the tensor equated to the matter tensor is *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, with *β*/*α* = Λ and the constant of proportionality 8π*G*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit.

**Closure of GR from McGucken.** What Lovelock requires is exactly what the McGucken Principle supplies. (i) The 4-dimensionality is manifest in the four-coordinate structure (*t*, **x**) of the principle. (ii) The Lorentzian signature is calculated in §II.9.1. (iii) Diffeomorphism invariance follows from the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (§II.8.2, proved below): the Lemma selects the foliation-preserving subgroup Diff_McG(*M_G*) as the off-shell symmetry of the matter action, and the full Diff(*M_G*), with ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 in all four directions, is recovered on shell (Theorem 9 of [71], reproduced below). (iv) The geodesic action *S* = −*mc* ∫ √−*g*_μν·ẋ^μẋ^ν *dλ* is the variational extremum of proper-time arc length on *the McGucken Sphere Σ_M*. Lovelock’s theorem then closes the deduction: the unique field equation derivable from the McGucken hypersurface and divergence-freeness in 4D is *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν, with the Newtonian limit fixing the constant 8π*G*/*c*⁴ and observation bounding Λ. The full Einstein-equation derivation chain—Lovelock-uniqueness applied on the McGucken hypersurface, with Schwarzschild, FLRW, light-bending, perihelion precession, and gravitational-wave polarizations as downstream theorems—is presented in McGucken [71, 71], with the action and Lagrangian formulation in [77] and the joint GR–QM unification in [72].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Lovelock’s Hypotheses: The McGucken-Invariance Lemma and the Factorisation of Diff(*M*).**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For Lovelock’s hypotheses it predicts two things: the rate of *x*₄-advance is the same at every event whatever the metric there, and the diffeomorphisms that preserve that rate off shell are those that preserve the foliation by spatial slices. Both are theorems of [71]. Their statements and proofs are reproduced here from [71, §1.1, §1.4, §1.5.1]. In them the McGucken Space *M_G* denotes the spacetime of expanding McGucken Spheres at every point, foliated by the spatial slices Σ_*t*.

**Component (i) of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (Invariance)** [71, §1.1]. The rate *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is the same at every spacetime event *p* ∈ *M_G* and is unaffected by the presence of mass, energy, or curvature in the three spatial dimensions. Formally, for any two events *p*, *q* ∈ *M_G*, the differential rate *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* equals *dx*₄/*dt*|_*q* identically.

**Lemma (McGucken-Invariance Lemma; Lemma 6 and Theorem GR T2 of [71]).** Let *g*_μν(*x*) be the spacetime metric on the McGucken Space *M_G*. Then

∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν = 0 for all μ, ν ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3}.

That is, the *x*₄-expansion rate is independent of the metric components: it is an input to the geometry, not a derived quantity.

*Proof.* By the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, component (i), the rate *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* = i*c* for every *p* ∈ *M_G*, regardless of the local value of *g*_μν(*p*). Suppose, for contradiction, that ∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν ≠ 0 at some event *p*. Then there exist two events *p*, *q* ∈ *M_G* with *g*_μν(*p*) ≠ *g*_μν(*q*) such that *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* ≠ *dx*₄/*dt*|_*q*, contradicting the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, component (i). Hence the partial derivative vanishes identically. □

**Theorem (Factorisation of Diff(*M*) via McGucken; Theorem 9 of [71]).** Hilbert’s diffeomorphism invariance of the gravitational action is factored by *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* into two physically distinct components: (a) A kinematic symmetry: Diff_McG(*M_G*)-invariance of the matter action, where Diff_McG(*M_G*) is the foliation-preserving subgroup of Diff(*M_G*) generated by vector fields *ξ*^μ = (*ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^i(*x*⁰, *x*^j)) with *ξ*⁰ depending only on the time coordinate *x*⁰. (b) A constitutive identity: *u*^μ*u*_μ = −*c*² enforced pointwise on every massive worldline. Off shell, only Diff_McG(*M_G*) acts as a symmetry. On shell, given the matter equations of motion, the full Diff(*M_G*) is recovered, and the Einstein field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8*πG*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν follow from Lovelock uniqueness plus Newtonian matching.

*Proof.* **Step 1 (the subgroup Diff_McG).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes the rate of *x*₄-advance to i*c* at every point of the McGucken Space *M_G*. Under a diffeomorphism *φ*: *M_G* → *M_G* generated by a vector field *ξ*^μ, the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* requires *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* to be invariant. The constraint is that *ξ*⁰ cannot depend on the spatial coordinates *x*^j in a way that varies the rate at different points of the same spatial slice; otherwise the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* would be broken because *dx*₄/*dt* would acquire spatial dependence. Hence ∂*ξ*⁰/∂*x*^j = 0 for *j* = 1, 2, 3, i.e., *ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰) only. The spatial components *ξ*^j are unconstrained because the spatial sector is deformable. The vector field is therefore of the form *ξ*^μ = (*ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^j(*x*⁰, *x*^k)), which defines Diff_McG(*M_G*).

**Lemma 9.1 of [71].** Diff_McG(*M_G*) is a strict subgroup of Diff(*M_G*). *Proof.* The general vector field on the McGucken Space *M_G* has *ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰, *x*^i) depending on all coordinates. Restricting *ξ*⁰ to depend only on *x*⁰ yields a strict subgroup, since vector fields with ∂*ξ*⁰/∂*x*^j ≠ 0 are not in the McGucken subgroup. □

**Step 2 (the constitutive identity).** By the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, every massive particle’s worldline *x*^μ(*τ*) on the McGucken Space *M_G* has *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Parametrising by proper time *τ* via *dτ*² = −*c*⁻² *g*_μν *dx*^μ *dx*^ν, the four-velocity is *u*^μ = *dx*^μ/*dτ*. The four-velocity budget identity

*u*^μ*u*_μ = *g*_μν *u*^μ *u*^ν = −*c*²

follows directly from the definition of *τ*: indeed *g*_μν *u*^μ *u*^ν = (*dx*^μ *dx*_μ)/*dτ*² = −*c*². The stress-energy tensor of a perfect fluid of such particles is

*T*^μν = (*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ*u*^ν/*c*² + *p* *g*^μν, (T)

with the constraint *u*^μ*u*_μ = −*c*² enforced pointwise.

**Step 3 (Noether’s second theorem applied to Diff_McG).** Apply Noether’s second theorem to the matter action the McGucken Sphere Σ_M[*φ*, *g*_μν] invariant under Diff_McG(*M_G*). Noether’s second theorem is here the standard variational result applied to framework-derived inputs: the McGucken Lagrangian, the Diff_McG-symmetry, and the +i*c* foliation all descend from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (precise status in Remark 10.3.1.1 of [71]). The variational theorem itself is general calculus of variations, a tool, not re-derived from the Principle; what the Principle supplies are its inputs. Therefore the Algebraic Channel rests on no physical input external to *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* for this step.

The conserved currents associated with Diff_McG-invariance are weaker than those of full Diff(*M_G*) because the symmetry group is smaller. Specifically: (S) spatial diffeomorphism invariance (*ξ*^j unconstrained, *ξ*⁰ = 0) yields the spatial conservation law ∇_i*T*^iν = 0 on each spatial slice Σ_*t*; (T-int) time-reparametrisation invariance (*ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^j = 0) yields the integrated time-conservation (*d*/*dt*) ∫_{Σ_t} *T*^0ν √*h* *d*³*x* = 0. The local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 in all four directions is not given by Diff_McG alone because Diff_McG is a strict subgroup (Lemma II.9.1).

**Step 4 (on-shell enhancement).** Claim: given the matter equations of motion (relativistic energy equation and Euler equation) together with the constitutive identity (Step 2), the local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 holds in all four directions.

*Proof of the on-shell enhancement.* The perfect-fluid stress-energy tensor (T) gives, upon differentiation,

∇_μ*T*^μν = ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] *u*^ν + (*ρc*² + *p*) (*u*^μ/*c*²) ∇_μ*u*^ν + *g*^μν ∂_μ*p*. (†)

*Timelike projection.* Contract (†) with *u*_ν and apply the constitutive identity *u*_ν*u*^ν = −*c*² together with its consequence *u*_ν∇_μ*u*^ν = 0 (from differentiating *u*_ν*u*^ν = −*c*², a constant on the constraint surface):

*u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = −*c*² ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] + 0 + *u*^μ∂_μ*p*.

Expanding the first term:

−*c*² ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] = −∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ] = −(*ρc*² + *p*) ∇_μ*u*^μ − *c*² *u*^μ∂_μ*ρ* − *u*^μ∂_μ*p*.

Combining:

*u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = −(*ρc*² + *p*) ∇_μ*u*^μ − *c*² *u*^μ∂_μ*ρ*.

Setting this to zero gives the relativistic energy equation (the perfect-fluid matter equation of motion for energy conservation along the flow):

*u*^μ∂_μ*ρ* + (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²) ∇_μ*u*^μ = 0. (Energy-EOM)

The timelike projection *u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 is therefore equivalent to the perfect-fluid energy equation, which is one of the matter equations of motion.

*Spatial projection.* Project (†) orthogonal to *u*^ν using the spatial projector *h*^ν_λ = δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² (with *h*^ν_λ *u*^λ = 0 and *h*^ν_λ *h*^λ_σ = *h*^ν_σ):

*h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*T*^μλ = (*ρc*² + *p*) (*u*^μ/*c*²) (*h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*u*^λ) + *h*^νμ ∂_μ*p*,

where the first term of (†) drops out under the spatial projection because *h*^ν_λ *u*^λ = 0, and the third term contracts to *h*^νμ∂_μ*p* via *h*^ν_λ *g*^λμ = *h*^νμ. Setting this to zero gives the relativistic Euler equation (the perfect-fluid matter equation of motion for spatial momentum):

(*ρc*² + *p*) [*c*⁻²] *u*^μ∇_μ*u*^ν = −*h*^νμ ∂_μ*p*, (Euler-EOM)

where the projection *h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*u*^λ ≡ ∇_μ*u*^ν on the spatial subspace (using *u*_λ∇_μ*u*^λ = 0) eliminates the redundant temporal component. The spatial projection *h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*T*^μλ = 0 is therefore equivalent to the relativistic Euler equation.

*Span and conclusion.* The timelike projector −*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² and the spatial projector *h*^ν_λ = δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² together span the full tangent space at every event:

−*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² + *h*^ν_λ = −*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² + δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² = δ^ν_λ.

Hence the vanishing of ∇_μ*T*^μν on both subspaces implies ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 identically. On shell, given the relativistic energy equation (Energy-EOM) and the relativistic Euler equation (Euler-EOM) as the perfect-fluid matter equations of motion, the local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 holds in all four directions. The matter action becomes invariant under arbitrary diffeomorphisms acting consistently on metric and matter, recovering the full Diff(*M_G*) on shell. □ (end of Step 4)

**Step 5 (Einstein field equations from Lovelock).** With ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 established, the geometric side of the field equations is forced by Lovelock’s theorem [117]: in four dimensions, the unique divergence-free symmetric rank-2 tensor built from the metric *g*_μν and its first two derivatives, linear in second derivatives, is

*L*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, (Lovelock)

where *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½ *R* *g*_μν is the Einstein tensor and *α*, *β* are constants. The priority of Lovelock as a theorem of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* operates through the McGucken-Invariance Lemma: the McGucken-Invariance Lemma restricts the allowed geometric structure to one in which *g*_μν is the deformable-sector variable (spatial sector) coupled to matter through the universal Sphere geometry; the Lovelock uniqueness then applies to the divergence-free combinations on the four-dimensional manifold the McGucken Space *M_G*. Setting *L*_μν = *κ* *T*_μν with *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ and *β* = Λ gives

*G*_μν + Λ *g*_μν = (8*πG*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν. (Einstein field equations)

They are GR T11 of the 24-theorem gravitational chain of [71].

**Step 6 (Newtonian matching fixes *κ*).** In the weak-field static limit *g*₀₀ = −(1 + 2Φ/*c*²), *g*_ij = δ_ij, the dominant component of the Einstein field equations at 00 reduces to Poisson’s equation ∇²Φ = 4*πGρ*. The coupling constant *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ is forced by this matching. □ (end of the Theorem)

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Lovelock’s uniqueness theorem enters at Step 5 as an external mathematical result. Each of its hypotheses is supplied by a theorem of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*: the four dimensions and the Lorentzian signature by §II.9.1 (Lemma 1 of [74]); the off-shell symmetry Diff_McG(*M_G*) by the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (GR T2 of [71]); the divergence-free source ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 by the on-shell enhancement of Step 4. This is the *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Algebraic Channel derivation of the Einstein field equations (GR T11 of [71]).

### II.9.3 The Born Rule via Symmetry of the Spherical Measure

**Claim.** The probability assigned to outcome corresponding to state |φ⟩, given system in |ψ⟩, is P(φ|ψ) = |⟨φ|ψ⟩|².

**Derivation.** The McGucken Sphere Σ_M(τ) of radius *cτ* carries the unique rotation-invariant (Hausdorff) probability measure μ_SM (Lemma 7.1.5 of [114], reproduced below). By the co-generation of the McGucken Operator D_M with the McGucken Sphere Σ_M (Theorem II.4.1) and the wave equation □ψ = 0 derived in §II.8.3, every physically realizable amplitude ψ is a complex-valued function on the McGucken Sphere Σ_M. The space of square-integrable amplitudes is *L*²(McGucken Sphere Σ_M, μ_SM) with inner product ⟨φ|ψ⟩ = ∫ φ^∗ψ *dμ*_SM; this is the spherical specialization of the complex Hilbert space derived in §II.8.4.

We now invoke **Gleason’s theorem** (1957) [119]: any σ-additive probability measure ν on the lattice of closed subspaces of a separable complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 is of the form ν(*P*) = tr(ρ*P*) for a unique density operator ρ. A system prepared in |*ψ*⟩ has ν(*P*_ψ) = 1, which forces ρ = |*ψ*⟩⟨*ψ*|, and this gives ν(*P*_φ) = |⟨*φ*|*ψ*⟩|², the Born rule.

**What the McGucken Principle supplies.** Gleason’s theorem requires (a) a complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 and (b) the requirement that probability is σ-additive on closed subspaces. (a) is supplied by §II.8.4 (the McGucken Sphere yields infinite dimension via spherical harmonics). (b) is supplied by Theorem 7.2 of [114], reproduced below, in its projective form: the density |*ψ*|² assigns to the closed subspace with orthogonal projection *Π* the probability ⟨*ψ*|*Πψ*⟩, and for mutually orthogonal projections *Π*_k the sum Σ_k *Π*_k converges strongly to the projection onto the closed span, so the assignment is σ-additive. The Born rule is therefore not a postulate of quantum mechanics in the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework but a theorem: the unique phase-invariant quadratic probability measure on the sphere of complex amplitudes generated by the McGucken Principle. □ The full derivation, including the sphere–amplituhedron correspondence and the symmetry argument that fixes the unique measure, is presented in McGucken [73, 74, 75, 129, 114].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Born Rule: The Unique Invariant Measure on the McGucken Sphere and the Uniqueness of *P* = |*ψ*|².**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For probability it predicts two things: the isotropy of the expansion fixes one and only one rotation-invariant probability measure on the Sphere, and the density that the expansion of *x*₄ conserves is |*ψ*|² and no other function of the modulus. Both are theorems of [114]. Their statements and proofs are reproduced here from [114, §7.3–§7.5]. In [114] the McGucken Sphere of an event *p* is written Σ₊(*p*); it is the McGucken Sphere, written the McGucken Sphere Σ_M(*τ*) in the present paper.

**The McGucken wavefunction (Definition 2.6 of [114]).** For a system whose *x*₄-advance propagates from an event *E*, the McGucken wavefunction evaluated at spatial point **x** ∈ ℝ³ and parameter time *t* is the ℂ-valued field *ψ*(**x**, *t*) := Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ), the sum running over paths *γ* from *E* to (**x**, *t*).

**The four requirements (R1)–(R4) of [114, §7.3].** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* predicts that a probability density *P* on ℝ³ derived from the McGucken wavefunction *ψ* satisfies four requirements, each supplied by a property of the Sphere, and that these four fix *P* = |*ψ*|² with the quadratic form as the conclusion rather than an input.

**(R1) Reality.** *P* is real-valued. (A probability density counts detection events; it is real by physical interpretation.)

**(R2) Non-negativity.** *P* ≥ 0. (Detection counts are non-negative; the meeting of the emitter’s and the absorber’s McGucken Spheres at the detection event has the non-negative rate *κ*|*ψ*|², Theorem II.9.3.5 below, which is Theorem 7.7(iv), (v) of [114].)

**(R3) Phase invariance under global *x*₄-shift.** *P*(*e*^iα *ψ*) = *P*(*ψ*) for all real α. (A global phase is a shift of the *x*₄-origin, and the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes no origin of *x*₄: Proposition II.9.3.2 below.)

**(R4) Locality and conservation under *x*₄’s expansion.** (a) The density at an event *B* is a function of the wavefront at *B*; with (R3) this means *P*(**x**) = *F*(|*ψ*(**x**)|). This is Theorem II.9.3.3 below (Theorem 7.4 of [114]): the density at *B* is the overlap at *B* of the forward and conjugate *x*₄-expansions. (b) The total ∫ *P* *d*³*x* is the same at every time. The expansion of *x*₄ carries the wavefront forward by the propagation *W*(*t*) = exp(−i*t*Ω), where Ω = *c*(−∇² + *μ*²)^(1/2) with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ is the positive-energy square root of the Klein–Gordon operator, the Klein–Gordon equation (□ − *m*²*c*²/ℏ²)*ψ* = 0 being the differential expression of the McGucken-Sphere wavefront (Theorem II.8.3.3; Theorem 9.0 of [129] and theorem QM T8 of [73]), and the McGucken Sphere’s weight, in standard terms the total probability it carries, is conserved along its expansion (Lemma II.9.3.6(ii) below; Lemma 6.6C, step 2, of [73]), so a detection is certain at every time. In the non-relativistic regime *W*(*t*) reduces to the Schrödinger evolution of Theorem II.8.3.4 (Theorem 9.2 of [129]).

Requirement (R4)(b) is the McGucken Sphere’s conservation law, Lemma II.9.3.6 below (Lemma 6.6C of [73]), applied to the wavefront: the weight the source deposits on its McGucken Sphere is neither created nor destroyed as *x*₄ advances. No bilinearity in (*ψ*, *ψ*∗) is required; Lemma 7.1 derives it.

Step 2 of Lemma 6.6C of [73] reads: “By Theorem 6.6B Step 2 the wavefront carries the SO(3)-invariant measure of total mass 1, and by C1 the rate of advance is the same at every event, so no weight is created or destroyed as the wavefront expands.” Requirement (R4)(b) uses this step alone. The quadratic form of the density enters Lemma 6.6C only at its Step 3, which is not used here, so Lemma 7.1 below derives the quadratic form without assuming it.

**Lemma (Quadratic form from conservation under *x*₄’s expansion; Lemma 7.1 of [114]).** Let *F* be continuous on [0, ∞) and twice continuously differentiable on (0, ∞), with *F*(0) = 0, *F*′ bounded on bounded sets, and *F*(*s*) and *sF*′(*s*) both of order *s*^δ as *s* → 0 for some δ > 0. If ∫ *F*(|*ψ*(**x**, *t*)|) *d*³*x* is independent of *t* for every wavefront *ψ*(*t*) = *W*(*t*)*f* with *f* a Schwartz function, then *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for a constant *κ*.

*Notation for the proof.* *R̂* is the operator that multiplies the Fourier transform by *r*(*k*) = *ω*_C + *c*|*k*|²/2*μ* − *ω*(*k*), where *ω*(*k*) = *c*(|*k*|² + *μ*²)^(1/2) and *ω*_C = *cμ* is the Compton frequency. For *x* ≥ 0, 0 ≤ 1 + *x*/2 − (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≤ *x*²/8, which gives 0 ≤ *r*(*k*) ≤ *c*|*k*|⁴/(8*μ*³).

*Proof.* Take a profile *f* = *g* *e*^iθ with *g* > 0 a Gaussian and *θ* a real smooth function with bounded derivatives, and its dilations *f*_λ(*x*) = *f*(*x*/*λ*), *λ* ≥ 1.

*Step 1, the derivative.* Since ∂*ψ*/∂*t* = −iΩ*ψ* and |*f*| > 0, the derivative of |*ψ*| at *t* = 0 is Im(*e*^−iθ Ω*f*), and the difference quotients of *F*(|*ψ*|) are dominated by an integrable function, because *F*′ is bounded on the range of |*ψ*| and *ψ*, Ω*ψ* are Schwartz functions uniformly for *t* in a bounded interval. Hence

(*d*/*dt*) ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* = ∫ *F*′(*g*) Im(*e*^−iθ Ω*f*) *d*³*x*, *t* = 0.

*Step 2, splitting Ω.* By the bound just stated, Ω = *ω*_C + (*c*/2*μ*)(−∇²) − *R̂*, where *R̂* multiplies the Fourier transform by *r*(*k*) with 0 ≤ *r*(*k*) ≤ *c*|*k*|⁴/(8*μ*³). The term *ω*_C contributes Im(*e*^−iθ *ω*_C *f*) = 0.

*Step 3, the main term.* Im(*e*^−iθ (−∇²*f*)) = −(1/*g*) ∇·(*g*²∇*θ*). Integrating by parts twice, with *H*(*s*) = *sF*′(*s*) − 2*F*(*s*), so that *H*′(*s*) = *sF*″(*s*) − *F*′(*s*), and with no boundary terms because *H*(*g*) and *gF*′(*g*) are of order *g*^δ and *g* is a Gaussian,

∫ (*F*′(*g*)/*g*) (−∇·(*g*²∇*θ*)) *d*³*x* = ∫ ∇(*H*(*g*))·∇*θ* *d*³*x* = −∫ *H*(*g*) ∇²*θ* *d*³*x*.

*Step 4, scaling.* For *f*_λ the main term equals −(*c*/2*μ*) *λ* ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x*, since the volume element contributes *λ*³ and the Laplacian *λ*⁻². The remainder is bounded by sup|*F*′(*g*)| · ‖(1 + |*x*|²/*λ*²)⁻¹‖₂ · ‖(1 + |*x*|²/*λ*²)*R̂f*_λ‖₂ ≤ *C* *λ*^(3/2) · *λ*^(3/2 − 4) = *C* *λ*⁻¹, because *λ*⁴ *r*(*k*/*λ*) and its first two derivatives are bounded by polynomials in |*k*| uniformly in *λ* ≥ 1.

*Step 5, conclusion.* Conservation makes the derivative vanish for every *λ*. Dividing by *λ* and letting *λ* → ∞ gives ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x* = 0 for every admissible *θ*, in particular for every smooth *θ* of compact support. So *H*(*g*(*x*)) is a continuous weakly harmonic function on ℝ³ that tends to *H*(0) = 0 at infinity, and it vanishes identically by the maximum principle. A Gaussian of height *a* takes every value in (0, *a*], so *H*(*s*) = 0 for all *s* > 0. Then *sF*′(*s*) = 2*F*(*s*), whose solutions are *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*². □

For the Schrödinger propagation of Theorem 9.2 of [129], Ω is replaced by −(ℏ/2*m*)∇² up to the rest-energy constant, Steps 2 and 4 are void, and Step 3 alone gives ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x* = 0.

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The exponent 2 is the exponent that *x*₄’s expansion conserves. The rank-2 character of the interval (i*ct*)² = −*c*²*t*² enters through the Klein–Gordon equation, which is second order because the interval is quadratic, and the lemma shows that its propagation conserves the quadratic density and no other function of the modulus. The conservation itself is the Sphere’s: Lemma 6.6C of [73], the weight of the Sphere conserved along its advance [114, §7.4].

**Lemma (Uniqueness of the SO(3)-invariant probability measure on the McGucken Sphere; Lemma 7.1.5 of [114]).** The McGucken Sphere Σ₊(*p*) at parameter time *t* is a round 2-sphere *S*² of radius *ct*, carrying the transitive action of the rotation group SO(3) fixed by the isotropy of the *x*₄-expansion from *p*. Then there exists a unique SO(3)-invariant Borel probability measure on the Sphere, namely the normalized round measure *dμ*(*θ*, *φ*) = sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ*/(4π), and every SO(3)-invariant probability density on the Sphere is this *dμ*. This is the measure that supplies the Born-rule probability over detection directions.

*Proof.* The Sphere is a homogeneous space for SO(3): the isotropy of the expansion makes the action transitive, and the stabilizer of any direction is the subgroup SO(2) of rotations about that axis, so Σ₊(*p*) ≅ SO(3)/SO(2) = *S*². SO(3) is compact; by Haar’s theorem (1933) [39] every compact group carries a unique normalized invariant measure, which pushes forward to a unique invariant probability measure on any homogeneous space. Existence: *dμ* = sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ*/(4π) is invariant because rotations are isometries of the round metric and sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ* is its area element. Uniqueness: if *ν* is any SO(3)-invariant probability measure, then for continuous *f*, invariance plus transitivity give ∫ *f* *dν* = ∫ *f* *dμ* (both equal the round average of *f*); since this holds for all continuous *f*, *ν* = *μ* by the Riesz representation theorem. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Requirements (R1)–(R4): the Conjugate Expansion, the Overlap at the Detection Event, the Meeting of Two McGucken Spheres, and the Inverse-Square Law of the McGucken Sphere.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the four requirements it predicts the following. (R3) holds because the Principle fixes no origin of *x*₄ (Proposition II.9.3.2). (R1), (R2) and (R4)(a) hold because the density at an event is the overlap, at that event, of the expansion with its conjugate reading (Theorem II.9.3.3, Lemma II.9.3.4, Theorem II.9.3.5). (R4)(b) holds because each McGucken Sphere carries the total weight 1 at every radius (Lemma II.9.3.6). The statements are Definition 7.3, Theorem 7.4, Lemma 7.7.5 and Theorem 7.7(ii), (iv), (v) of [114] and Lemma 6.6C of [73]. The proofs are given here in full.

**Definition II.9.3.1 (Conjugate expansion; Definition 7.3 of [114]).** The conjugate of the McGucken expansion *x*₄ = i*ct* is the expansion obtained by complex-conjugating both sides: *x*₄∗ = (i*ct*)∗ = −i*ct*. Complex conjugation reverses the orientation of the perpendicular *x*₄-axis. The conjugate is the same expansion read in the opposite orientation; it has no independent physical existence. For a wavefunction *ψ* = Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ) the conjugate wavefunction is *ψ*∗ = Σ_γ exp(−i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ): the same paths with the opposite phase.

**Proposition II.9.3.2 (The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes no origin of *x*₄; the ground of (R3)).** (i) If *x*₄(*t*) satisfies *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, then *x*₄(*t*) + *a* satisfies it for every constant *a*. (ii) The amplitude of a body at rest, *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) of Theorem II.8.3.1, is on *x*₄ = i*ct* the function exp(−*μ* *x*₄) with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ. Replacing *x*₄ by *x*₄ + i*cτ*₀ with *τ*₀ real multiplies it by exp(−i*ω*_C *τ*₀), and every number exp(i*α*) with *α* real is obtained, for *τ*₀ = −*α*/*ω*_C. (iii) If the amplitude at the emission event is multiplied by exp(i*α*), then the wavefunction is multiplied by exp(i*α*) at every later event. Consequently *ψ* and exp(i*α*)*ψ* are the wavefunctions of one emission referred to two origins of *x*₄, and a density determined by the Principle has the same value on both. This is (R3).

*Proof.* (i) *d*(*x*₄ + *a*)/*dt* = *dx*₄/*dt*. (ii) On *x*₄ = i*ct*, *t* = *x*₄/(i*c*), so −i*ω*_C *t* = −*ω*_C *x*₄/*c* = −*μ* *x*₄, because *ω*_C/*c* = *mc*/ℏ. Then exp(−*μ*(*x*₄ + i*cτ*₀)) = exp(−i*μc* *τ*₀) exp(−*μ* *x*₄) and *μc* = *ω*_C. (iii) The wavefunction at a later event is obtained from the amplitude at emission by the superposition of Theorem II.8.3.3(ii) or by the integral of Theorem II.8.3.8(ii). Both are linear in the amplitude at emission, so a constant factor passes through them. □

**Theorem II.9.3.3 (The density is the overlap of the forward and the conjugate expansion; Theorem 7.4 of [114]).** (i) Let *γ*₁, …, *γ*_N be paths from an event *A* to an event *B* with real actions *S*₁, …, *S*_N, and let *ψ*(*B*) = Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ). Then the product of the conjugate and the forward wavefunction at *B* is

*ψ*∗(*B*) *ψ*(*B*) = Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ) = *N* + 2 Σ_(jS_j − *S*_k)/ℏ),

a real number with 0 ≤ *ψ*∗*ψ* ≤ *N*². The *N* terms with *j* = *k* each equal 1, one for each path. The terms with *j* ≠ *k* are the interference terms, each fixed by the difference of the actions of two paths. (ii) For the free evolution of Theorem II.8.3.8, with *ψ*(*x*, *t*) = ∫ *K*_t(*x* − *y*) *φ*₀(*y*) *d*³*y* and *S*(*x*, *y*) = *m*|*x* − *y*|²/(2*t*) the action of the straight path from *y* to *x*,

*ψ*∗(*x*, *t*) *ψ*(*x*, *t*) = (*m*/(2πℏ*t*))³ ∫∫ exp(i(*S*(*x*, *y*) − *S*(*x*, *y*′))/ℏ) *φ*₀∗(*y*′) *φ*₀(*y*) *d*³*y* *d*³*y*′.

(iii) In both cases *ψ*∗*ψ* at the event is |*ψ*|² there: a real, non-negative function of the value of the wavefunction at that event and of nothing else.

*Proof.* (i) Multiply the two sums: *ψ*∗*ψ* = (Σ_k exp(−i*S*_k/ℏ)) (Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ)) = Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ). The terms with *j* = *k* are exp(0) = 1 and there are *N* of them. The terms (*j*, *k*) and (*k*, *j*) with *j* < *k* are complex conjugates and add to 2 cos((*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ). Since *ψ*∗ is the complex conjugate of *ψ*, *ψ*∗*ψ* = |*ψ*|² ≥ 0, and |*ψ*| ≤ *N* because each term has modulus 1. (ii) By Theorem II.8.3.8(i), *K*_t(*x* − *y*) = (*m*/(2πiℏ*t*))^(3/2) exp(i*S*(*x*, *y*)/ℏ), and |(*m*/(2πiℏ*t*))^(3/2)|² = (*m*/(2πℏ*t*))³. The conjugate of the integral for *ψ* is the integral of the conjugate integrand, with integration variable *y*′, and the product of the two integrals is the double integral because *φ*₀ is a Schwartz function and the exponentials have modulus 1, so the double integral converges absolutely. (iii) is the identity *z*∗*z* = |*z*|² for a complex number *z*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.9.3.3.** The forward wavefunction carries the phase exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause for *x*₄ = i*ct*, and the conjugate wavefunction carries the phase of the same paths for *x*₄∗ = −i*ct* (Definition II.9.3.1). The product at *B* is the overlap at *B* of the two readings of one expansion. Its value depends on the wavefront at *B* alone, which is (R4)(a), and it is real and non-negative, which is (R1) and (R2). That no other pairing of the two readings is available is the next lemma.

**Lemma II.9.3.4 (Coincidence of two McGucken Spheres: the product and the conjugation; Lemma 7.7.5 of [114]).** Let two McGucken Spheres, of a source *A* and a source *B*, arrive at the spatial point *P* at the time *t*, with amplitudes *ψ*_A(*P*) and *ψ*_B(*P*). (a) Product. Let *ψ*_A(*P*) = Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) over the paths of *A* and *ψ*_B(*P*) = Σ_k exp(i*T*_k/ℏ) over the paths of *B*, and let the action of a pair of paths, one of each source, be the sum *S*_j + *T*_k. Then the amplitude of the pair of arrivals, summed over the pairs of paths, is the product *ψ*_A(*P*) *ψ*_B(*P*). (b) Conjugation. Let *V* be a real vector space and *β* a real symmetric bilinear form on *V* that is not identically zero. On the complexification, whose elements are *z* = *u* + i*v* with *u*, *v* in *V*, *β* has exactly one extension *β*_bil that is complex-linear in both arguments and exactly one extension *β*_ses that is conjugate-linear in the first argument and complex-linear in the second. The diagonal value *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) has a non-zero imaginary part for some *z*. The diagonal value *β*_ses(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) + *β*(*v*, *v*) is real for every *z*. (c) Uniqueness. Let *R*(*z*, *w*) be a complex-valued function of two complex numbers that is additive and real-homogeneous in *z*, complex-linear in *w*, unchanged when *z* and *w* are multiplied by the same exp(i*α*) (the requirement (R3)), and real for *z* = *w* (the requirement (R1)). Then *R*(*z*, *w*) = *κ* *z*∗*w* with *κ* a real constant, and *R*(*z*, *z*) ≥ 0 for every *z* (the requirement (R2)) exactly when *κ* ≥ 0.

*Proof.* (a) By exp(*a* + *b*) = exp(*a*) exp(*b*) and the distributive law, the double sum Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j + *T*_k)/ℏ) equals Σ_j Σ_k exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) exp(i*T*_k/ℏ), which is the product of the sum Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) and the sum Σ_k exp(i*T*_k/ℏ). The sum of the actions is the action of two worldlines without interaction: the functional of Proposition II.8.7.1 is an integral along each worldline, and by the action clause each action is ℏ times the phase of the advance of *x*₄ along its path, so the phases of two separate advances add. The value of the pair amplitude is therefore the product, and the other symmetric combinations of two amplitudes differ from it: for *ψ*_A = *ψ*_B = i the product is −1, the sum is 2i, and the maximum, the minimum, the arithmetic mean, the geometric mean and the harmonic mean of the moduli are each 1.

(b) A form that is complex-linear in both arguments and equals *β* on *V* must satisfy *β*_bil(*u* + i*v*, *u*′ + i*v*′) = *β*(*u*, *u*′) − *β*(*v*, *v*′) + i(*β*(*u*, *v*′) + *β*(*v*, *u*′)), by expanding in both arguments with i·i = −1, and this formula defines such a form. A form that is conjugate-linear in the first argument, complex-linear in the second and equal to *β* on *V* must satisfy *β*_ses(*u* + i*v*, *u*′ + i*v*′) = *β*(*u*, *u*′) + *β*(*v*, *v*′) + i(*β*(*u*, *v*′) − *β*(*v*, *u*′)), by expanding with (−i)·i = 1, and this formula defines such a form. Put *z* = *w* and use *β*(*u*, *v*) = *β*(*v*, *u*): *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) − *β*(*v*, *v*) + 2i *β*(*u*, *v*) and *β*_ses(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) + *β*(*v*, *v*). Since *β* is symmetric and not identically zero, the identity 4*β*(*u*, *u*′) = *β*(*u* + *u*′, *u* + *u*′) − *β*(*u* − *u*′, *u* − *u*′) gives a vector *u*₀ with *β*(*u*₀, *u*₀) ≠ 0, and for *z* = *u*₀ + i*u*₀ the imaginary part of *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) is 2*β*(*u*₀, *u*₀) ≠ 0. For the values of amplitudes at a point, *V* is the real line and *β*(*u*, *u*′) = *uu*′; then *β*_bil(*z*, *w*) = *zw* and *β*_ses(*z*, *w*) = *z*∗*w*, and for *z* = i the first gives (i)² = −1 while the second gives (−i)·i = 1 = |i|².

(c) For fixed *z*, *R*(*z*, *w*) = *L*(*z*) *w* with *L*(*z*) = *R*(*z*, 1), because *R* is complex-linear in *w*. *L* is additive and real-homogeneous, so with *z* = *x* + i*y*, *L*(*z*) = *x* *L*(1) + *y* *L*(i) = *a* *z* + *b* *z*∗, where *a* = (*L*(1) − i*L*(i))/2 and *b* = (*L*(1) + i*L*(i))/2. Hence *R*(*z*, *w*) = *a* *zw* + *b* *z*∗*w*. Multiplying *z* and *w* by exp(i*α*) multiplies *zw* by exp(2i*α*) and leaves *z*∗*w* unchanged, so the invariance, taken at *z* = *w* = 1 and *α* = π/2, gives −*a* + *b* = *a* + *b*, that is *a* = 0. Then *R*(*z*, *z*) = *b* |*z*|², which is real at *z* = 1 only if *b* is real. With *κ* = *b*, *R*(*z*, *w*) = *κ* *z*∗*w*, and *κ*|*z*|² ≥ 0 for every *z* exactly when *κ* ≥ 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Lemma II.9.3.4.** The symmetric real form of part (b) is the one the Principle supplies: (i*c*)² = −*c*² gives the interval and its symmetric metric *η*_μν (§II.8.1), and the factor i of *x*₄ = i*ct* makes the amplitudes complex (Theorem II.8.3.1). Part (c) shows that the requirements (R1) and (R3), with linearity in the arriving amplitude, leave the pairing *z*∗*w* and no other, so the conjugation in the Born density is the conjugate reading of Definition II.9.3.1 and is obtained without a separate postulate of time reversal.

**Theorem II.9.3.5 (The rate at the meeting of the emitter’s and the absorber’s McGucken Spheres; Theorem 7.7(ii), (iv), (v) of [114]).** (i) Conjugation and the direction of the expansion. If *φ*(*x*, *t*) satisfies the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* with real *V*, then *φ*∗(*x*, *t*) satisfies the equation with i replaced by −i, which is the equation of the conjugate expansion *x*₄∗ = −i*ct*, and *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*) satisfies the original equation. For *V* = 0 and *r* > 0, the outgoing spherical wave exp(i(*kr* − *ωt*))/*r* with ℏ*ω* = ℏ²*k*²/2*m* is a solution, and the operation *φ*(*x*, *t*) → *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*) carries it to exp(−i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*, the spherical wave of the same frequency converging on the centre. (ii) Rate. The rate of the coincidence at *P* of an arriving amplitude *ψ*_A(*P*) with an absorbing amplitude *ψ*_B(*P*), being a pairing that satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma II.9.3.4(c), is *κ* *ψ*_B∗(*P*) *ψ*_A(*P*) with *κ* > 0. (iii) Detection. For an absorber at *P* that takes up the wavefront arriving there, the absorbing amplitude is the arriving amplitude *ψ*(*P*) in its conjugate reading, and the rate is *κ* *ψ*∗(*P*) *ψ*(*P*) = *κ* |*ψ*(*P*)|².

*Proof.* (i) Take the complex conjugate of the equation; *V*, *m*, ℏ and the operators ∂ₜ and ∇² are real, so −iℏ ∂ₜ*φ*∗ = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ*∗ + *Vφ*∗. Put *χ*(*x*, *t*) = *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*); then ∂ₜ*χ*(*x*, *t*) = −(∂ₜ*φ*∗)(*x*, −*t*), and the last equation becomes iℏ ∂ₜ*χ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*χ* + *Vχ*. For a function of *r* alone, ∇²*g* = (1/*r*) ∂²(*rg*)/∂*r*², so ∇²(exp(i*kr*)/*r*) = −*k*² exp(i*kr*)/*r* for *r* > 0, and iℏ ∂ₜ gives ℏ*ω*; the two sides agree when ℏ*ω* = ℏ²*k*²/2*m*. The conjugate of exp(i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*, which is the wave at −*t*, is exp(−i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*; its surfaces of constant phase are *kr* + *ωt* = constant, on which *r* decreases at the rate *ω*/*k* as *t* increases. (ii) is Lemma II.9.3.4(c), with *κ* > 0 because a rate that is not identically zero is positive somewhere. (iii) By Definition II.9.3.1 the conjugate reading is of the same expansion, so both arguments of the pairing are the one number *ψ*(*P*), and *R*(*ψ*(*P*), *ψ*(*P*)) = *κ* |*ψ*(*P*)|². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem II.9.3.5.** The McGucken Sphere of the emitter arrives at *P* as an outgoing wavefront. The conjugate reading *x*₄∗ = −i*ct* of that wavefront is, by part (i), the wavefront converging on *P*. A detection is the meeting at *P* of the two, and its rate is |*ψ*(*P*)|² up to the constant *κ* fixed by normalization. Requirements (R1) and (R2) are therefore properties of this rate, and Theorem 7.2 below fixes the density from (R1)–(R4).

**Lemma II.9.3.6 (Inverse-square law of the McGucken Sphere and the exponent 2; Lemma 6.6C of [73]).** Write *r* = |*x*| and let *ψ* be a solution of the wave equation □*ψ* = 0 of the McGucken Sphere’s wavefront (Theorem II.8.3.3 with *μ* = 0) that depends on *r* and *t* alone. (i) For *r* > 0, *ψ* = (*f*(*r* − *ct*) + *g*(*r* + *ct*))/*r* with *f*, *g* twice continuously differentiable functions of one variable. The outgoing wavefront is *f*(*r* − *ct*)/*r*: its amplitude at the radius *r* = *c*(*t* − *t*_p) from its source event *p* falls as 1/*r*. (ii) The McGucken Sphere of radius *r* has area 4π*r*², and its rotation-invariant probability measure (Lemma 7.1.5) has the weight 1/(4π*r*²) per unit area, the same total weight 1 at every radius. (iii) Let *F* be a continuous function on [0, ∞). If ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* has the same value at every *t* > 0 for every outgoing wavefront *ψ* = *f*(*r* − *ct*)/*r* with *f* twice continuously differentiable and zero outside a bounded interval [0, *ε*], then *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for a constant *κ*. (iv) With the density *κ*|*ψ*|², a source at distance *r* from an event *E* deposits there a density proportional to 1/*r*², and the sources in the shell between *r* and *r* + *dr* about *E*, of volume 4π*r*² *dr*, deposit together an amount proportional to *dr*, which is finite on every bounded range of distances.

*Proof.* (i) For a function of *r* and *t*, ∇²*ψ* = (1/*r*) ∂²(*rψ*)/∂*r*², so *u* = *rψ* satisfies ∂²*u*/∂*r*² − *c*⁻² ∂²*u*/∂*t*² = 0. In the variables *ξ* = *r* − *ct* and *η* = *r* + *ct* this equation is 4 ∂²*u*/∂*ξ*∂*η* = 0, whose solutions are *u* = *f*(*ξ*) + *g*(*η*). The term *f*(*r* − *ct*) keeps its value along *r* = *ct* + constant, so it moves outward at the rate *c*, and |*ψ*| = |*f*|/*r*. (ii) The area of a round sphere of radius *r* is 4π*r*². By Lemma 7.1.5 the invariant probability measure is the normalized area measure, whose weight per unit area is 1/(4π*r*²) and whose total is 1.

(iii) Fix *a* > 0 and *ε* > 0. Choose twice continuously differentiable functions *f*_n with 0 ≤ *f*_n ≤ *a*, zero outside [0, *ε*], and *f*_n(*u*) → *a* for every *u* strictly between 0 and *ε*. Where *f*_n(*r* − *ct*) is different from zero the radius is *r* = *ct* + *u* with *u* ≥ 0, hence *r* ≥ *ct* > 0, so each *f*_n(*r* − *ct*)/*r* is a solution for *t* > 0 by (i), and in polar coordinates ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* = 4π ∫ *F*(*f*_n(*r* − *ct*)/*r*) *r*² *dr*, the integral running over *ct* ≤ *r* ≤ *ct* + *ε*. *F* is continuous, hence bounded on [0, *a*/(*ct*)], so by dominated convergence the limit as *n* → ∞ is 4π ∫ *G*(*r*) *dr* over *ct* ≤ *r* ≤ *ct* + *ε*, with *G*(*r*) = *F*(*a*/*r*) *r*², and this limit has the same value at every *t* > 0 because each term of the sequence has. Take two times *t* and *t*′. The two integrals of *G*, over [*ct*, *ct* + *ε*] and over [*ct*′, *ct*′ + *ε*], are equal for every *ε* > 0; *G* is continuous, so differentiating with respect to *ε* gives *G*(*ct* + *ε*) = *G*(*ct*′ + *ε*). Any two radii 0 < *r*₁ < *r*₂ are of this form, with *ε* = *r*₁/2, *ct* = *r*₁/2 and *ct*′ = *r*₂ − *r*₁/2. So *G* is a constant *C*(*a*) on (0, ∞): *F*(*a*/*r*) = *C*(*a*)/*r*². Every *s* > 0 is *a*/*r* with *r* = *a*/*s*, so *F*(*s*) = *C*(*a*) *s*²/*a*² for all *s* > 0 and every *a* > 0; with *a* = 1, *F*(*s*) = *C*(1) *s*², and *F*(0) = 0 by continuity. (iv) By (i) the amplitude at distance *r* from the source is proportional to 1/*r*, so *κ*|*ψ*|² is proportional to 1/*r*²; multiplied by the volume 4π*r*² *dr* of the shell this is proportional to *dr*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Lemma II.9.3.6.** The expansion of *x*₄ is spherically symmetric, so the McGucken Sphere has area 4π*r*² and its amplitude falls as 1/*r*; the expansion has the same rate *c* at every event, so the weight of the McGucken Sphere stays 1 as it advances. Part (iii) shows that these two facts fix the exponent: a density that is a function of the modulus of the amplitude, and whose total over the McGucken Sphere is the same at every radius, is the square of the modulus. This is the inverse-square law, and it is the requirement (R4)(b) for the wavefront of the McGucken Sphere itself. Lemma 6.6C of [73] states the same relation between the 1/*r* amplitude and the 1/*r*² density. Lemma 7.1 above proves (R4)(b) ⇒ *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for the Klein–Gordon propagation of a body of rest mass *m*; for that propagation ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x* is conserved because *W*(*t*) multiplies the Fourier transform by exp(−i*ω*(*k*)*t*), a number of modulus 1, and Plancherel’s identity applies as in the proof of Theorem II.8.4.1(i).

**Theorem (Born rule from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; Theorem 7.2 of [114]).** Let *ψ*: ℝ³ → ℂ be the McGucken wavefunction. The unique density *P*: ℝ³ → ℝ_{≥0} satisfying (R1), (R2), (R3), (R4) is

*P*(**x**) = |*ψ*(**x**)|² / ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x*.

*Proof.* By (R3) and (R4)(a), *P* = *F*(|*ψ*|). By (R4)(b) and Lemma 7.1, *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*². (R1) and (R2) hold for *κ* > 0, and normalization fixes *κ*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Gleason’s theorem.** Gleason’s theorem (1957) [119] is an external mathematical result: on a separable complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 every σ-additive probability measure on closed subspaces has the trace form. In the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework its two hypotheses are supplied by theorems: the Hilbert space by Theorem 6.1 of [129] (§II.8.4), and the σ-additive assignment by Theorem 7.2 of [114] above. The Born rule itself is Theorem 7.2, proved from (R1)–(R4) with the quadratic form derived in Lemma 7.1 and without Gleason’s theorem; Gleason’s theorem adds that no other σ-additive assignment exists on the derived Hilbert space. Lemma 7.1 is the Conservation Route to the exponent 2, and Lemma 7.1.5 is the route through the symmetry group of the McGucken Sphere [114, §7.4–§7.5].

### II.9.4 Gauge Connection from Covariantization of the McGucken Operator D_M

**Claim.** The gauge-covariant derivative ∇_μ = ∂_μ + i*qA*_μ/ℏ arises uniquely from local *U*(1) covariantization of the McGucken Operator D_M, and the Yang–Mills connection arises by the same construction with SU(*N*) in place of U(1).

**Derivation.** Under a global phase rotation ψ → *e*^iαψ (with α constant), the McGucken operator D_M commutes with the transformation: D_M(*e*^iαψ) = *e*^iα(McGucken Operator D_Mψ). Promote α to a function α(*x*): now D_M(*e*^iα(x)ψ) = *e*^iα(McGucken Operator D_M + i(McGucken Operator D_Mα))ψ, and the operator no longer commutes. To restore covariance we introduce a 1-form field *A*_μ transforming as *A*_μ → *A*_μ − (ℏ/*q*)∂_μα and replace ∂_μ by ∇_μ = ∂_μ + i*qA*_μ/ℏ. This is the unique minimal coupling consistent with local *U*(1) gauge invariance and reproducing the Maxwell equations (via *F*_μν = ∂_μ*A*_ν − ∂_ν*A*_μ) when the action − (1/4) *F*_μν*F*^μν is added. Replacing *U*(1) by *SU*(*N*) yields the non-abelian *A*_μ = *A*_μ^a*T*^a and the corresponding Yang–Mills field strength *F*_μν^a = ∂_[μ*A*_ν]^a + *gf*^abc*A*_μ^b*A*_ν^c. □ The covariantization of the McGucken Operator D_M as the source of the gauge connection, and the associated Lagrangian, are developed in McGucken [74, 77]. The covariance of the field strength is proved in Lemma II.8.7.6, and the uniqueness of the Lagrangian −¼ *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν of the connection in Theorem II.8.7.8.

### II.9.5 The Strict Second Law and Bekenstein–Hawking Entropy

We give two explicit derivations: the strict second law *dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*/*t* from the McGucken Operator D_M-driven Brownian motion, and the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy *S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²) from the McGucken Wick rotation.

**Claim 1 (Strict second law).** For an ensemble of test particles subject only to the McGucken Operator D_M-driven isotropic spatial displacement, the Boltzmann–Gibbs entropy satisfies *dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*/*t* > 0 for all *t* > 0.

**Derivation.** By Theorem 5 of McGucken (2026e), the spatial projection of the *x*₄-driven displacement at any instant is isotropic on the spherical wavefront. By Theorem 6, iterated isotropic displacements give Wiener-process motion: Var(**r**(*t*)) = 6*Dt*, with the diffusion constant *D* fixed by the Compton-coupling formula of Theorem 14. The probability density of the test ensemble is therefore the Gaussian fundamental solution of the heat equation:

*ρ*(**r**, *t*) = (4*πDt*)^−3/2 exp(−|**r**|²/(4*Dt*)).

Substitute into the Boltzmann–Gibbs entropy:

*S*(*t*) = −*k*_*B* ∫ *ρ*(**r**, *t*) ln *ρ*(**r**, *t*) *d*³*r*.

Using ln *ρ* = −(3/2) ln(4*πDt*) − |**r**|²/(4*Dt*), the integral splits into the normalization piece and the second-moment piece. The first gives (3/2)*k*_*B* ln(4*πDt*); the second uses ∫ |**r**|²*ρ* *d*³*r* = 6*Dt* and yields (3/2)*k*_*B*. Adding:

*S*(*t*) = (3/2)*k*_*B*[1 + ln(4*πDt*)].

Differentiating with respect to *t*:

*dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*(1/*t*) > 0.

The inequality is strict for every finite *t* > 0, with no appeal to the Past Hypothesis, no ergodic hypothesis, and no coarse-graining. The second law is the rate at which the McGucken Operator D_M-driven Gaussian spreading on the McGucken Sphere Σ_M(τ) widens the spatial-projection support. □

**Remark on Loschmidt.** The Loschmidt reversibility paradox (1876) observes that any Hamiltonian dynamics is time-reversal symmetric, hence cannot derive a strict *dS*/*dt* > 0. The McGucken resolution (Theorem 12) is that the spatial Hamiltonian channel (the Algebraic Channel) is indeed reversible, but it is not the only channel: the geometric expansion *R*(*t*) = *ct* on the McGucken Sphere Σ_M(τ) (the Geometric Channel) is monotone in *x*₄ by definition of the McGucken Principle. Boltzmann’s H-theorem (1872) and the Loschmidt objection both concern the Algebraic Channel alone; the Geometric Channel supplies the geometric monotonicity they were missing. The strict inequality of Claim 1 is a Geometric Channel identity, not a Algebraic Channel theorem.

**Claim 2 (Bekenstein–Hawking entropy [121, 122]).** A Killing horizon of area *A* in the McGucken hypersurface carries entropy *S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²), where *ℓ*_*P* = (ℏ*G*/*c*³)^1/2 is the Planck length.

**Derivation.** The argument has six steps.

**Step 1 (Horizon as *x*₄-stationary surface).** A Killing horizon is the locus where a timelike Killing vector becomes null. In McGucken-coordinates this is the locus where the *x*₄-component of motion vanishes for stationary observers; the horizon is *x*₄-stationary in the instantaneous co-moving frame.

**Step 2 (McGucken Wick rotation).** Define *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* = i*t*. Then *dx*₄/*dτ*_E = *c*: read in *τ*_E, the law carries no i. In the variables (*t*, *τ*_E) the McGucken Operator is the McGucken Operator D_M = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ = ∂ₜ + i∂_τE = 2∂/∂*z̄* with *z* = *t* + i*τ*_E, the Cauchy–Riemann operator, which is elliptic; the derivative identity ∂_τ = −i∂ₜ is Theorem 10.1 of [87], where *τ*_E is written *τ*. The Lorentzian metric becomes the Euclidean Riemannian metric *dσ*² = *c*²*dτ*_E² + *d***x**².

**Step 3 (Euclidean cigar).** Near the horizon, the Euclideanized metric is *dσ*² = *κ*²*ξ*²*dθ*² + *dξ*² + *dA*, with *ξ* the proper distance to the horizon and *κ* the surface gravity. Regularity at *ξ* = 0 (no conical singularity) requires *θ* to be periodic with period 2*π*/*κ*; the Euclideanized time circle is the cigar.

**Step 4 (Hawking temperature).** The Matsubara identification *β* = 1/(*k*_*B* *T*) = 2*π*/*κ* in natural units, with the ℏ and *c* restored from *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c*:

*T*_H = ℏ*κ*/(2*πck*_*B*).

This is Theorem 16; it falls out of the Wick rotation without a separate quantization of the matter field.

**Step 5 (Mode counting at the Planck density).** On the Euclidean horizon the *x*₄-stationary modes are quantized at one mode per Planck area: *N*(*A*) = *A*/*ℓ*_*P*². By the Planck-scale quantization of *x*₄-oscillation, the smallest independent mode of *x*₄-oscillation has wavelength *ℓ*_*P* = (ℏ*G*/*c*³)^(1/2); on the two-dimensional horizon the minimum cross-sectional area per mode is *ℓ*_*P*², and for a black hole, the maximum-entropy configuration of given mass, charge and angular momentum, the count is saturated ([80], Theorem 15, Step B3). Each mode contributes *k*_*B* ln 2 in the naive Bekenstein argument [121], but the precise normalization is fixed by Step 6.

**Step 6 (First-law normalization).** The thermodynamic identity *dE* = *T*_H *dS*_BH at the horizon, combined with the first law of black-hole mechanics *dE* = (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) *dA* [128] (for a Schwarzschild black hole *E* = *Mc*², *κ* = *c*⁴/(4*GM*), *A* = 16*πG*²*M*²/*c*⁴, so that (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) *dA* = *c*² *dM*), fixes the proportionality constant:

*dS*_BH/*dA* = (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) / (ℏ*κ*/2*πck*_*B*) = *k*_*B* *c*³/(4ℏ*G*) = *k*_*B*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²),

yielding

*S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²).

The factor of 1/4 is fixed by the Wick-rotation period of the Euclidean cigar; no additional axiom is required. The generalized second law *dS*ₜₒₜₐₗ/*dt* ≥ 0 (Theorem 17) now follows by adding Claim 1 (matter entropy) to the area increase theorem of Hawking (1971) [127], which holds, under the null energy condition and cosmic censorship, on the Lorentzian causal structure derived in §II.9.1. With Hawking radiation, where the horizon area decreases, the generalized second law is Theorem 17 of [80], stated as the non-decrease of the total *x*₄-flux through closed hypersurfaces. □

**Remark.** In the standard derivation of Bekenstein–Hawking the Wick rotation *t* → −i*τ*_E is performed by hand as a calculational device. In the McGucken framework it is part of the law: the i is already in the law *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, and the rotation *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* simply reads the law in its Euclidean chart. The Hawking temperature, Bekenstein area-entropy, and generalized second law are theorems about the inverse of the same i that produced the Lorentzian signature in §II.9.1 and the complex Hilbert space in §II.8.4.

### II.9.6 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Operator Non-Identity and Maximality Theorems, the Category of the Source-Pair, the Symmetry Theorems, Twistor Space, the Unit i, and the Spectral Triples of Connes

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. This section proves, inside the paper, six groups of results that the companion papers [74, 75, 87, 91, 78, 79] state about the McGucken Operator D_M = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ and the McGucken Space ℳ_G: the non-identity and maximality of the McGucken Operator; the category of the source-pair with its functors; the symmetry results of Noether, Wigner and of the CPT theorem, with the exact standing of the gauge group, of supersymmetry and of the string dualities; twistor space and the kinematic data of the amplituhedron from the McGucken Sphere; the origin of the unit i in the formulas of quantum theory; and the relation to the spectral triples of Connes. Each result is stated with its hypotheses and proved step by step. Where a statement of a companion paper rests on a theorem that is not proved here, the theorem is named, attributed and marked as external, and the part proved here is stated separately.

**9.6.1 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Operator Non-Identity and Maximality Theorems.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle makes *x*₄ a coordinate that advances, and the McGucken Operator D_M differentiates along that advance. The operators of the earlier literature act on functions of the event (*t*, **x**) alone. The commutator with *x*₄ separates the two classes.

**Lemma II.9.6.1 (Commutators of the McGucken Operator).** Let 𝒦 be the space of the functions Ψ(*t*, **x**, *x*₄) of Definition II.6.3, with values in ℂ or in ℂ^N, differentiable in *t* and **x** and holomorphic in *x*₄. Write *x*₄ also for the operator of multiplication by *x*₄. Call a linear operator *T* on 𝒦 an operator of spacetime if there is a linear operator *T*₀ on functions of (*t*, **x**) with (*T*Ψ)(·, ·, *x*₄) = *T*₀(Ψ(·, ·, *x*₄)) for every value of *x*₄. Then (i) [McGucken Operator D_M, *x*₄] = i*c*; (ii) [McGucken Operator D_M, *t*] = 1; (iii) every operator of spacetime commutes with *x*₄; (iv) the operators on 𝒦 that commute with *x*₄ are closed under sums, products and multiplication by numbers; (v) with (*R*Ψ)(*t*, **x**, *x*₄) = Ψ(*t*, **x**, −*x*₄), *R* the McGucken Operator D_M *R*⁻¹ = ∂ₜ − i*c*∂₄.

*Proof.* (i) D_M(*x*₄Ψ) = *x*₄∂ₜΨ + i*c*(Ψ + *x*₄∂₄Ψ) = *x*₄the McGucken Operator D_MΨ + i*c*Ψ. (ii) D_M(*t*Ψ) = Ψ + *t*∂ₜΨ + i*c* *t*∂₄Ψ = *t* the McGucken Operator D_MΨ + Ψ. (iii) For a fixed value of *x*₄ the function (*x*₄Ψ)(·, ·, *x*₄) is the number *x*₄ times Ψ(·, ·, *x*₄), and *T*₀ is linear, so *T*(*x*₄Ψ) = *x*₄ *T*Ψ. (iv) If *A* and *B* commute with *x*₄, then (*A* + *B*)*x*₄ = *x*₄(*A* + *B*), *AB* *x*₄ = *A* *x*₄ *B* = *x*₄ *AB*, and (λ*A*)*x*₄ = *x*₄(λ*A*). (v) *R* maps 𝒦 onto itself, *R*² = 1, *R* commutes with ∂ₜ, and ∂₄(*R*Ψ) = −*R*(∂₄Ψ), so *R*∂₄*R*⁻¹ = −∂₄. □

**Theorem II.9.6.2 (Non-identity of the McGucken Operator; Theorems 34.4–34.9 of [87] in the form proved here).** (a) The McGucken Operator D_M is different from every operator of spacetime, and from every sum of products of operators of spacetime. In particular it is different from the Hamiltonian *Ĥ* = iℏ∂ₜ and from every Schrödinger operator iℏ∂ₜ + (ℏ²/2*m*)∇² − *V*(*t*, **x**) of Theorem II.8.3.4; from the momentum operator −iℏ∇ and the position operators of Theorem II.8.3.5; from the d’Alembertian □ on functions of (*t*, **x**); from the Dirac operator iℏγ^μ∂_μ + *mc* of Theorem II.8.5.3; from every gauge-covariant derivative ∂_μ + *A*_μ(*t*, **x**); and from the operators *D* and π(*a*) of every spectral triple (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) whose Hilbert space consists of functions or spinor fields on spacetime, extended to 𝒦 with *x*₄ as a parameter. (b) the McGucken Operator D_M is different from the Dirac operator *D_E* = −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ of the Euclidean four-coordinate space of Theorem II.9.6.20: for Ψ = *x*₁², McGucken Operator D_M²Ψ = 0 and *D_E*²Ψ = −2. (c) the McGucken Operator D_M is different from the Wick substitution (*W*Ψ)(τ, **x**) = Ψ(−iτ, **x**): *W* satisfies *W*(ΨΦ) = *W*(Ψ)*W*(Φ), the McGucken Operator D_M satisfies D_M(ΨΦ) = Ψthe McGucken Operator D_MΦ + Φthe McGucken Operator D_MΨ, and a linear map with both properties is zero. (d) The Wheeler–DeWitt operator acts on functionals of the metrics of a three-dimensional space; its domain has no function of (*t*, **x**, *x*₄) in it, so it and the McGucken Operator D_M are operators on different spaces.

*Proof.* (a) By Lemma II.9.6.1(iii) and (iv) every operator of spacetime, and every sum of products of such operators, commutes with *x*₄. By Lemma II.9.6.1(i) the commutator of the McGucken Operator D_M with *x*₄ is i*c*, and *c* > 0. Each operator in the list acts on the variables (*t*, **x**) and on the components of Ψ alone, with *x*₄ as a parameter, so each is an operator of spacetime. (b) Ψ = *x*₁² is independent of *t* and *x*₄, so the McGucken Operator D_MΨ = 0, and *D_E*² = −(∂₁² + ∂₂² + ∂₃² + ∂₄²) by Theorem II.9.6.20(i), which gives −2. (c) If a linear map *L* satisfies both rules, then *L*(1) = *L*(1·1) = *L*(1)² by the first and *L*(1) = 2*L*(1) by the second, so *L*(1) = 0, and then *L*(Ψ) = *L*(1·Ψ) = *L*(1)*L*(Ψ) = 0 for every Ψ. The McGucken Operator D_M is different from zero, since the McGucken Operator D_M *t* = 1. (d) is the comparison of the two domains. □

**Theorem II.9.6.3 (Maximality of the McGucken Operator; Section II.33.10 of [87] in the form proved here).** (i) Generation. The Hamiltonian, the d’Alembertian, the Dirac operator and the gauge-covariant operator are obtained from the McGucken Operator D_M: iℏD_M = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄ (Theorem II.8.5.1); the four-coordinate Laplacian restricted to the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* is □ (Theorem II.8.5.2); □ − μ² is the product of the two Dirac operators (Theorem II.8.5.3); and the McGucken Operator D_M + *A_M* is the gauge-covariant operator (Theorem II.8.5.4). (ii) No return. No sum of products of the operators of spacetime of Theorem II.9.6.2(a) equals the McGucken Operator D_M. (iii) Orientation. Let *E_R* be the set of the operators on 𝒦 that commute with the reflection *R* of Lemma II.9.6.1(v); it contains ∂ₜ, ∇, ∂₄², the four-coordinate Laplacian ∇² + ∂₄², and the multiplications by functions of (*t*, **x**) and of *x*₄². *E_R* is closed under sums and products, and the McGucken Operator D_M does not belong to *E_R*.

*Proof.* (i) is the statement of the four theorems cited. (ii) is Theorem II.9.6.2(a). (iii) ∂ₜ, ∇ and the multiplications by functions of (*t*, **x**) commute with *R*; ∂₄² = (−∂₄)² and (−*x*₄)² = *x*₄² give the same for ∂₄² and for functions of *x*₄². If *A*, *B* commute with *R* so do *A* + *B* and *AB*. By Lemma II.9.6.1(v), *R* the McGucken Operator D_M *R*⁻¹ − the McGucken Operator D_M = −2i*c*∂₄, which is different from zero on Ψ = *x*₄. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The commutator [McGucken Operator D_M, *x*₄] = i*c* is the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in operator form: the McGucken Operator D_M advances *x*₄ at the rate i*c* per unit of *t*. An operator of spacetime leaves *x*₄ where it is. Part (iii) states the same for the sign: the operators that are even under *x*₄ → −*x*₄ cannot produce the operator that carries the orientation +i*c* of the expansion. The sentence of [87] that no operator of the earlier literature coincides with the McGucken Operator is a statement about the literature; what is proved here is the list (a)–(d) of Theorem II.9.6.2 and parts (ii), (iii) of Theorem II.9.6.3. One identity belongs with that list. As a differential expression in two real variables *t* and *s* = *x*₄/*c*, ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ = ∂ₜ + i∂_s = 2∂/∂*z*∗ with *z* = *t* + i*s*, the Cauchy–Riemann operator in the notation of Wirtinger [151], and *z* = (i/*c*)Φ_M. The McGucken Principle supplies what the expression by itself lacks: the variable *x*₄ is the fourth dimension of the physical world, the surface Φ_M = 0 is the set of events, and the three spatial dimensions with the McGucken Sphere at each event complete the McGucken Space.

**9.6.2 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Category of the Source-Pair: Selection of the Rate i*c*, the Obstruction Theorem, the Six Functors and the Initial Object.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The source-pair theorems of §II.6 (Theorems II.6.12–II.6.23) show that the McGucken Operator and the McGucken Space each contain and each generate the other. This subsection determines which part of that reciprocity belongs to every law of constant rate and which part belongs to the rate i*c* alone, and it gives the category of the source-pair with exact definitions.

**Proposition II.9.6.4 (The reciprocal properties for a law of constant rate).** For a complex number *k* ≠ 0 let a coordinate *y* obey *dy*/*dt* = *k*, and put Φ_k = *y* − *kt* and *D_k* = ∂ₜ + *k*∂_y. Then (a) *D_k*Φ_k = 0; (b) a function Ψ(*t*, *y*), differentiable in *t* and holomorphic in *y*, satisfies *D_k*Ψ = 0 exactly when it is a function of *y* − *kt* alone; (c) *D_k* is the only operator ∂ₜ + *b*∂_y with constant *b* and (∂ₜ + *b*∂_y)Φ_k = 0, and Φ_k is the only function *y* − *bt* with *D_k*(*y* − *bt*) = 0. Consequently Lemmas II.6.9–II.6.11, Theorems II.6.12–II.6.16 and II.6.18, Steps 1–3 of Theorem II.6.17, and the parts of Theorems II.6.19–II.6.23 that do not involve Step 4 of Theorem II.6.17, hold for the pair (Φ_k, *D_k*) with *k* in place of i*c*. For *k* = i*c* and *y* = *x*₄ the pair is the constraint function Φ_M together with the McGucken Operator D_M.

*Proof.* (a) ∂ₜΦ_k = −*k* and ∂_y Φ_k = 1. (b) Put *u* = *y* − *kt* and Ψ(*t*, *y*) = *F*(*t*, *u*). Then ∂ₜΨ + *k*∂_y Ψ = ∂ₜ*F* − *k*∂_u*F* + *k*∂_u*F* = ∂ₜ*F*, which vanishes exactly when *F* is independent of *t*. (c) (∂ₜ + *b*∂_y)(*y* − *kt*) = *b* − *k* and *D_k*(*y* − *bt*) = *k* − *b*. The proofs of the results listed use (a), (b), (c) and the chain rule *d*Ψ/*dt* = ∂ₜΨ + (*dy*/*dt*)∂_y Ψ, and no other property of the number i*c*. Step 4 of Theorem II.6.17 uses (i*c*)² = −*c*²; it is the subject of the next theorem. □

**Theorem II.9.6.5 (The rate i*c* is selected by the Lorentzian interval and by propagation at a finite speed).** In the situation of Proposition II.9.6.4 let the four coordinates (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *y*) carry the form *d*ℓ² = *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dy*². (i) On the surface *y* = *kt* the form is *d***x**² + *k*²*dt*². (ii) It is real for all real displacements exactly when *k*² is real. It has one negative and three positive squares exactly when *k*² < 0, that is, *k* = ±iκ with κ > 0; it is positive definite exactly when *k* is real. (iii) For *G*(**x**, *y*) holomorphic in *y* and *g*(*t*, **x**) = *G*(**x**, *kt*), (∂_y²*G* + ∇²*G*)(**x**, *kt*) = ∇²*g* + *k*⁻²∂ₜ²*g*. For *k* = ±iκ this is the wave operator ∇² − κ⁻²∂ₜ² with the speed κ; for real *k* it is the Laplace operator in the four real variables (**x**, *kt*). (iv) With κ = *c*, the invariant speed, the two laws are *dy*/*dt* = ±i*c*. The reflection *y* → −*y* exchanges them, and the McGucken Principle fixes the sign +, the direction in which *x*₄ advances.
