*Proof.* (i) *dy* = *k* *dt*. (ii) The coefficient of *dt*² is *k*²; the three coefficients of *dx_j*² are 1. (iii) ∂ₜ*g* = *k* ∂_y*G* and ∂ₜ²*g* = *k*² ∂_y²*G* at *y* = *kt*, by the chain rule, as in Theorem II.8.5.2. (iv) Under *y* → −*y* the law *dy*/*dt* = *k* becomes *dy*/*dt* = −*k*. □

**Proposition II.9.6.6 (The single-relation obstruction; the earlier arena–operator pairs. Theorems 6.11 and 6.12 of [91] in the form proved here).** (i) Obstruction. Let *X* be the data of an arena, and suppose that two different operators *L*₁ ≠ *L*₂ are each compatible with the same data *X*. Then no procedure whose only input is *X* produces both, so at least one of the pairs (*X*, *L*₁), (*X*, *L*₂) fails generation of the operator by the arena (Definition II.6.8). (ii) The Hilbert space *L*²(ℝ³) carries the Hamiltonians −(ℏ²/2*m*)∇² and −(ℏ²/2*m*)∇² + ½*m*ω²|**x**|². (iii) If (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) is a spectral triple, so is (𝒜, ℋ, 2*D*), with the same algebra and Hilbert space and with every distance of Connes halved. (iv) A smooth manifold without metric carries the Laplace operators of the metrics *g* and 2*g*, which differ by the factor ½. (v) A Riemannian manifold (*M*, *g*) and its Laplace operator Δ_g determine each other: Δ_g = *g*^ij∇_i∇_j is built from *g*, and *g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f* = ½(Δ_g(*f*²) − 2*f*Δ_g*f*) for every smooth *f*. (vi) The complex plane with the operator ∂/∂*z*∗ is the pair (Φ_k, *D_k*) of Proposition II.9.6.4 with *k* = i up to the constant factors stated after Theorem II.9.6.3, and has the properties (a)–(c) of that proposition.

*Proof.* (i) A procedure in the sense of Definition II.6.8 returns one output for one input. (ii) Both are self-adjoint operators on domains in *L*²(ℝ³), and they differ on every function that is not zero almost everywhere. (iii) 2*D* is self-adjoint with the resolvent of *D* rescaled, and [2*D*, *a*] = 2[*D*, *a*] is bounded when [*D*, *a*] is; the condition ‖[2*D*, *f*]‖ ≤ 1 is ‖[*D*, *f*]‖ ≤ ½, so the supremum of |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| in Theorem II.9.6.20(iii) is halved. (iv) The inverse of the metric 2*g* is ½*g*^ij, and the connection coefficients of *g* and 2*g* agree. (v) By the product rule for ∇, Δ_g(*f*²) = 2*f*Δ_g*f* + 2*g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f*; the values of *g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f* for all *f* determine *g*^ij at each point. (vi) is Proposition II.9.6.4. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Proposition II.9.6.4 and Theorem II.9.6.5 separate two things. Mutual containment and reciprocal generation hold for the transport operator of every law of constant rate, and Proposition II.9.6.6(v), (vi) records two earlier pairs, the Riemannian manifold with its Laplace operator and the complex plane with the Cauchy–Riemann operator, in which the arena and the operator also determine each other. What the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* adds, and what the earlier pairs do not have, is stated in Theorem II.9.6.5 and in Definitions II.6.4–II.6.5: a law of advance in the physical time *t*; the rate i*c*, which among the constant rates is the one that yields the Lorentzian interval and the wave operator with the invariant speed *c*; the orientation +i*c*; and the McGucken Sphere at every event. The statement of [91], Theorem 6.11, that the McGucken source-pair is the first pair for which the three source-pair theorems hold, is to be read with these qualifications, and its remark that the Riemannian pair fails by the isospectral manifolds answering the question of Kac is to be replaced by Proposition II.9.6.6(v): isospectral manifolds share the spectrum of the Laplace operator, while the operator itself determines the metric.

**Definition II.9.6.7 (The category of the McGucken source-pair).** Let ISO⁺(1,3) denote, as in Lemma II.6.26, the group of the maps *x* → Λ*x* + *a* of the events *x* = (*ct*, **x**), with *a* a translation and Λ a Lorentz transformation preserving the direction of time and the orientation (Definition II.5.1(c), Lemma II.6.26). **McG** is the category with one object, the McGucken source-pair (McGucken Space ℳ_G, McGucken Operator D_M), whose morphisms are the elements of ISO⁺(1,3) with the composition of maps. Each element of ISO⁺(1,3) preserves the interval *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² that Step 4 of Theorem II.6.17 obtains from the source-pair, and preserves the direction of the advance of *x*₄ = i*ct*.

**Theorem II.9.6.8 (The six functors of the source-pair; Theorems 7.10–7.18 of [91] in the form proved here).** Each of the following assigns to the object of **McG** an object of the category named and to each *g* = (*a*, Λ) in ISO⁺(1,3) an isomorphism *F*(*g*) of that object, with *F*(1) = 1 and *F*(*gh*) = *F*(*g*)*F*(*h*); each is therefore a functor. (1) *F*_(spacetime), to the Lorentzian manifolds with their isometries preserving the direction of time: the object ℝ⁴ with the interval of Lemma II.6.25, and *F*(*g*) = *g*. (2) *F*_(Hilbert), to the Hilbert spaces with unitary maps: the Fock space ℱ of Definition II.5.1 and *F*(*g*) = *V*(*a*, Λ). (3) *F*_(Clifford), to the algebras with their isomorphisms: the algebra *Cl*, the quotient of the tensor algebra of ℝ⁴ by the two-sided ideal generated by the elements *v* ⊗ *v* + η(*v*, *v*)·1, and *F*(*g*) the automorphism induced by *v* → Λ*v*; the matrices of Theorem II.8.5.3 represent *Cl* by *v* → *v*_μ γ^μ. (4) *F*_(gauge), for each group *G* of Definition II.8.7.5, to the principal *G*-bundles with their isomorphisms: the product bundle ℝ⁴ × *G* and *F*(*g*)(*x*, *u*) = (*gx*, *u*). (5) *F*_(algebra), to the C∗-algebras with their ∗-isomorphisms: the algebra of the bounded operators on ℱ and *F*(*g*)(*B*) = *V*(*g*)*B* *V*(*g*)⁻¹. (6) *F*_(Klein), to the sets with a transitive action of a group, where a morphism is a pair of an isomorphism φ of the groups and a bijection *f* of the sets with *f*(*h*·*x*) = φ(*h*)·*f*(*x*): the set ℝ⁴ with the action of ISO⁺(1,3), which is the Klein pair (ISO⁺(1,3), SO⁺(1,3)) of Theorems II.6.28 and II.6.30, and *F*(*g*) = (*h* → *ghg*⁻¹, *x* → *gx*).

*Proof.* (1) Each *g* is an isometry by Lemma II.6.26, and the composition in **McG** is the composition of maps. (2) *V* is a unitary representation of ISO⁺(1,3) by Theorem II.5.4 (W1). (3) The linear map Λ extends to an automorphism of the tensor algebra, multiplicative by construction; it maps *v* ⊗ *v* + η(*v*, *v*) to Λ*v* ⊗ Λ*v* + η(Λ*v*, Λ*v*), since η(Λ*v*, Λ*v*) = η(*v*, *v*), so it maps the ideal onto itself and induces an automorphism of the quotient; the extension of ΛΛ′ is the product of the extensions. The relation (*v*_μ γ^μ)² = −η(*v*, *v*) of Theorem II.8.5.3 shows that *v* → *v*_μ γ^μ respects the ideal. (4) *F*(*g*) is a bijection of ℝ⁴ × *G* that covers *g* and commutes with the right multiplication of *G* on the second factor. (5) Conjugation by a unitary operator is a ∗-isomorphism, and *V*(*gh*) = *V*(*g*)*V*(*h*). (6) The translations act transitively, the subgroup fixing the origin is the group of the Λ, and *g*(*h*·*x*) = (*ghg*⁻¹)·(*gx*); both components are multiplicative in *g*. □

**Theorem II.9.6.9 (The initial-object statement; Theorem 7.21 of [91] in the form proved here).** Let *S* be a set of mathematical structures that contains the McGucken source-pair, and write *X* ≤ *Y* when *Y* is generated from *X* in the sense of Definition II.6.8. Regard (*S*, ≤) as a category with one morphism from *X* to *Y* when *X* ≤ *Y* and none otherwise. (i) The source-pair is an initial object of this category exactly when every member of *S* is generated from it. (ii) This holds for the set *S* consisting of the source-pair together with the Lorentzian spacetime (Lemma II.6.25), the Klein pair of spacetime (Theorem II.6.30), the Hilbert space of states (Theorem II.8.4.3), the Fock space with its free fields (Definition II.5.1, Theorems II.5.4 and II.5.7), the operators of Theorems II.8.5.1–II.8.5.4, and the Klein pairs of Theorems II.6.33, II.6.34, II.6.36–II.6.39 and II.6.41–II.6.43. (iii) In the category **McG** of Definition II.9.6.7 the single object has the automorphism group ISO⁺(1,3), which has more than one element, so it is not an initial object of **McG**.

*Proof.* (i) An initial object is one with exactly one morphism to each object; in a category with at most one morphism between two objects this is the existence of the morphism, that is, *X*₀ ≤ *Y* for every *Y*. (ii) Each theorem cited constructs the structure named from the source-pair by an explicit procedure. (iii) An initial object has the identity as its only morphism to itself. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Theorems II.9.6.8 and II.9.6.9 give the two senses in which the source-pair of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* stands at the base of the categories of mathematical physics: as the object whose automorphisms act, functorially, on spacetime, on the space of states, on the Clifford algebra, on the gauge bundles, on the algebra of operators and on the Klein pair; and as the least element of the order of generation among the structures constructed in this paper. Three corrections are owed in [91], §II.7: the morphisms defined there by the conditions (C1), (C2) include maps that are not isometries, for example (*t*, **x**, *x*₄) → (*t*, 2**x**, *x*₄), so the functor to the Lorentzian manifolds requires the morphisms of Definition II.9.6.7; the uniqueness of the morphism in its Theorem 7.21 holds in the sense of part (i) above; and part (iii) above shows that no category in which the Poincaré group acts on the source-pair has it as an initial object.

**9.6.3 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Noether’s Theorem, the Classification of Wigner, the CPT Theorem, the Gauge Group, Supersymmetry and the String Dualities.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle yields the group ISO⁺(1,3) of Definition II.9.6.7 (Theorems II.6.28, II.6.30) and the Lagrangian of Theorem II.8.7.9. The symmetry results of [74] are consequences of these two together with the theorems proved or named below.

**Theorem II.9.6.10 (Noether’s first theorem [143]; Theorem 30 of [74]).** Let ℒ(φ, ∂φ) be a twice differentiable function of fields φ^a(*x*) on ℝ⁴ and of their first derivatives, π_a^μ = ∂ℒ/∂(∂_μ φ^a), and *E_a* = ∂ℒ/∂φ^a − ∂_μ π_a^μ, so that the field equations of §II.8.7 are *E_a* = 0. Suppose that for functions Δ^a of the fields, of their derivatives and of *x* the first-order change of ℒ under φ^a → φ^a + εΔ^a is ε∂_μ*K*^μ for every field configuration, with *K*^μ a function of the fields, their derivatives and *x*. Then *j*^μ = π_a^μ Δ^a − *K*^μ satisfies ∂_μ*j*^μ = −*E_a*Δ^a for every field configuration, and ∂_μ*j*^μ = 0 for every solution of the field equations. If the fields vanish at spatial infinity fast enough for the integral to exist and for the surface term to vanish, *Q* = ∫ *j*⁰ *d*³*x* is independent of *t*.

*Proof.* The first-order change of ℒ is (∂ℒ/∂φ^a)Δ^a + π_a^μ∂_μ Δ^a = *E_a*Δ^a + ∂_μ(π_a^μ Δ^a), by the product rule. Setting it equal to ∂_μ*K*^μ gives ∂_μ(π_a^μ Δ^a − *K*^μ) = −*E_a*Δ^a. For a solution the right side is zero; then *dQ*/*dt* = *c*∫ ∂₀*j*⁰ *d*³*x* = −*c*∫ ∂_k*j*^k *d*³*x*, a surface integral at infinity. □

**Corollary II.9.6.11 (The conserved quantities of the group of the McGucken Principle).** Let ℒ have no explicit dependence on *x*. (i) Translations: Δ^a = *a*^ν∂_ν φ^a gives *K*^μ = *a*^μℒ and *j*^μ = *a*^ν*T*^μ_ν with *T*^μ_ν = π_a^μ∂_ν φ^a − δ^μ_νℒ; the four conserved quantities are the energy and the three components of the momentum. (ii) Lorentz transformations, for scalar fields and a Lagrangian that is a Lorentz scalar: Δ^a = ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ∂_ν φ^a with ω_νρ = −ω_ρν gives *K*^μ = ω^μ_ρ*x*^ρℒ and *j*^μ = ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ*T*^μ_ν; the six conserved quantities are the three components of the angular momentum and the three of the motion of the center of energy. For fields with components transforming by matrices *S*^ρσ the change Δ^a gains the term ½ω_ρσ(*S*^ρσ φ)^a and the current the corresponding term; the proof is the same. (iii) Phase: for a complex field with ℒ unchanged under φ → exp(iα)φ, Δφ = iφ, Δφ∗ = −iφ∗ and *K*^μ = 0 give *j*^μ = i(π^μ φ − (π^μ)∗φ∗), where π^μ = ∂ℒ/∂(∂_μ φ) and ℒ is real; the conserved quantity is the charge.

*Proof.* (i) ℒ(*x* + ε*a*) − ℒ(*x*) = ε*a*^ν∂_ν ℒ = ε∂_μ(*a*^μℒ) to first order. (ii) The change of a scalar ℒ is ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ∂_ν ℒ = ∂_ν(ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρℒ), because ∂_ν(ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ) = ω^ν_ν = 0. (iii) ℒ is unchanged, so *K*^μ = 0. Theorem II.9.6.10 applies in each case. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The ten conserved quantities of (i) and (ii) belong to the ten parameters of the group ISO⁺(1,3), which Theorems II.6.28 and II.6.30 obtain from the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; the charge of (iii) belongs to the phase of Theorem II.8.5.4, the phase of the advance along *x*₄. The Lagrangian of Theorem II.8.7.9 is invariant under all of them. Noether’s theorem itself is the variational identity proved above and holds for every Lagrangian; the Principle supplies the group and the Lagrangian to which it is applied.

**Theorem II.9.6.12 (The orbits and the stabilizers in the classification of Wigner [144]; Theorem 33 of [74]).** Let *U*(*a*, *A*) be a strongly continuous unitary representation of the group of pairs (*a*, *A*), *a* a translation and *A* in SL(2, ℂ), acting on events by *x* → Λ(*A*)*x* + *a* (Theorem II.6.38), and let it be irreducible. Let *E* be the spectral measure of the translations, *U*(*a*, 1) = ∫ exp(−i*p*·*a*/ℏ) *dE*(*p*). (i) *U*(0, *A*)*E*(*S*)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ = *E*(Λ(*A*)*S*) for every Borel set *S*. (ii) *E* is concentrated on one orbit of the Lorentz group in the space of the *p*: the mass shell η(*p*, *p*) = −*m*²*c*², *p*⁰ > 0, with *m* > 0; its mirror image with *p*⁰ < 0; the forward or the backward null cone without the origin; the origin; or a hyperboloid η(*p*, *p*) = λ > 0. (iii) The subgroup of SL(2, ℂ) fixing the point (*mc*, 0, 0, 0) of the mass shell is SU(2). (iv) The subgroup fixing the null vector (1, 0, 0, 1) consists of the matrices with the rows (exp(iθ/2), *b*) and (0, exp(−iθ/2)), θ real, *b* complex; it covers twice the group of the rotations and translations of a plane.

*Proof.* (i) *U*(0, *A*)*U*(*a*, 1)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ = *U*(Λ*a*, 1) by the group law, and *p*·Λ*a* = (Λ⁻¹*p*)·*a*. So the measure *S* → *U*(0, *A*)*E*(*S*)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ and the measure *S* → *E*(Λ*S*) are both spectral measures of the unitary group *a* → *U*(Λ*a*, 1), and the spectral measure of a unitary representation of ℝ⁴ is unique (Stone’s theorem in four variables). (ii) If *S* is invariant under the Lorentz group, *E*(*S*) commutes with every *U*(0, *A*) by (i) and with every *U*(*a*, 1); its range is an invariant closed subspace, so *E*(*S*) is 0 or 1. Apply this to the sets η(*p*, *p*) ≤ λ: the projections increase from 0 to 1 with λ and are continuous from the right, so there is one value λ₀ with *E*(η(*p*, *p*) = λ₀) = 1. For λ₀ < 0 the two sheets *p*⁰ > 0 and *p*⁰ < 0 are invariant sets; for λ₀ = 0 the forward cone, the backward cone and the origin are. The group acts transitively on each set named: on a sheet with λ₀ < 0 a boost along **p** with the velocity *c*|**p**|/|*p*⁰| brings **p** to zero; on a null cone a rotation brings **p** to the third axis and the boost of Theorem II.6.38(iii) along it multiplies *p* by exp(±*s*); for λ₀ > 0 a boost along **p** with the velocity *c* *p*⁰/|**p**| brings *p*⁰ to zero and a rotation brings **p** to a fixed direction. (iii) *M*(*p*) = *mc*·1 for *p* = (*mc*, 0, 0, 0), and *A* *M*(*p*) *A*† = *M*(*p*) is *AA*† = 1. (iv) *M*(*p*) for *p* = (1, 0, 0, 1) is the matrix with the single non-zero entry 2 in the first place of the diagonal. With the first column of *A* equal to (α, γ), *A* *M*(*p*) *A*† has the entries 2|α|², 2αγ∗, 2γα∗, 2|γ|², so the condition is γ = 0 and |α| = 1, and det *A* = 1 makes the second diagonal entry 1/α. The matrices with θ = 0 form a group isomorphic to the translations of a plane, on which the diagonal matrices act by *b* → exp(iθ)*b*, a rotation by θ; θ and θ + 2π give the same rotation. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** For *m* > 0 the representative point (*mc*, 0, 0, 0) is the particle at rest in space, all of whose motion is the advance along *x*₄ at the rate *c* (Theorem II.8.1.6); the stabilizer SU(2) is the group of Theorem II.8.3.7 and its representations carry the spin. For *m* = 0 the representative is the photon, which is at rest in *x*₄ and moves on the McGucken Sphere; the rotations about its direction of motion carry the helicity. The remaining step of the classification, that every irreducible representation with the spectral measure on a given orbit is induced from an irreducible representation of the stabilizer, is the theorem of Wigner [144] and Mackey [145] and is used as an external theorem; it gives the labels mass and spin *s* = 0, ½, 1, … for *m* > 0 and the helicity for *m* = 0. The representations of mass *m* > 0 and spin 0 and ½ are constructed in this paper (Theorems II.5.4 and II.5.7).

**Theorem II.9.6.13 (The total reflection is a rotation of the four-coordinate carrier; the CPT operator of the free field. Theorem 45 of [74]).** (i) For real θ let *R*(θ) be the rotation by θ in the (*x*₁, *x*₂)-plane together with the rotation by θ in the (*x*₃, *x*₄)-plane of the carrier of Definition II.6.1. *R*(θ) preserves *x*₁² + *x*₂² + *x*₃² + *x*₄², *R*(0) = 1 and *R*(π) = −1. On the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* the second rotation is *x*₃′ = *x*₃ cos θ − i*ct* sin θ, *t*′ = *t* cos θ − i(*x*₃/*c*) sin θ, which is real exactly when sin θ = 0. (ii) The map *x* → −*x* of the events does not belong to the group SO⁺(1,3) of Lemma II.6.35. (iii) On the Fock space of Definition II.5.1 let (ΘΨ)_n(*k*₁, …, *k_n*) = Ψ_n(*k*₁, …, *k_n*)∗. Then Θ is antiunitary, Θ² = 1, ΘΩ = Ω, Θ*V*(*a*, Λ)Θ = *V*(−*a*, Λ), and ΘΦ(*f*)Θ = Φ(*f_r*) with *f_r*(*x*) = *f*(−*x*)∗.

*Proof.* (i) A rotation of a coordinate plane preserves the sum of the two squares; at θ = π each of the two rotations is −1 on its plane. Substituting *x*₄ = i*ct* and *x*₄′ = i*ct*′ in *x*₃′ = *x*₃ cos θ − *x*₄ sin θ, *x*₄′ = *x*₃ sin θ + *x*₄ cos θ gives the two formulas. (ii) Its matrix has Λ⁰₀ = −1. (iii) Complex conjugation preserves the norms and conjugates the inner products. From Definition II.5.1(e), Θ*A*(*g*)Θ = *A*(*g*∗) and Θ*A*†(*g*)Θ = *A*†(*g*∗). From Definition II.5.1(f), (*Ef*)(*k*)∗ = ∫ *f*(*x*)∗ exp(i*k*·*x*) *d*⁴*x* = ∫ *f*(−*x*)∗ exp(−i*k*·*x*) *d*⁴*x* = (*Ef_r*)(*k*), and in the same way (*E*(*f*∗))∗ = *E*((*f_r*)∗). Therefore ΘΦ(*f*)Θ = *A*(*E*((*f_r*)∗)) + *A*†(*Ef_r*) = Φ(*f_r*). From Definition II.5.1(c), Θ*V*₁(*a*, Λ)Θ multiplies φ(Λ⁻¹*k*) by exp(i*k*·*a*), which is *V*₁(−*a*, Λ). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Part (i) is where the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* enters the CPT theorem. In real spacetime the total reflection cannot be reached from the identity (part (ii)). In the four coordinates (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *x*₄), with *x*₄ the fourth dimension, it is the end of a path of rotations, and on the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* that path is the path of complex Lorentz transformations on which the proof of Jost [146] rests. Part (iii) exhibits the antiunitary operator for the free neutral scalar field, for which charge conjugation is the identity. The CPT theorem for every field theory satisfying the Wightman properties (W1)–(W6) is the theorem of Jost [146], given in [116], and is used as an external theorem; Lemma 47 of [74] states the same condition.

**Proposition II.9.6.14 (The form of a supersymmetry algebra over the spacetime of the Principle; Theorem 51 of [74]).** Let *Q*₁, *Q*₂ be operators on a Hilbert space with a common dense domain mapped into itself by them and by their adjoints, and let *U*(*A*) be a unitary representation of SL(2, ℂ) with *U*(*A*)*Q*_α*U*(*A*)⁻¹ = Σ_β (*A*⁻¹)_αβ*Q*_β. For a vector ψ of the domain let *N*(ψ) be the 2 × 2 matrix with the entries ⟨ψ, (*Q*_α*Q*_β† + *Q*_β†*Q*_α)ψ⟩. Then *N*(ψ) is Hermitian and positive semidefinite, so *N*(ψ) = *M*(*v*) for exactly one real four-vector *v* = *v*(ψ), with *v*⁰ ≥ |**v**|; and *N*(*U*(*A*)⁻¹ψ) = *A*⁻¹*N*(ψ)(*A*⁻¹)†, so *v* transforms as a four-vector under Λ(*A*⁻¹).

*Proof.* The adjoint of *Q*_α*Q*_β† + *Q*_β†*Q*_α is *Q*_β*Q*_α† + *Q*_α†*Q*_β, so *N* is Hermitian. For complex *z*₁, *z*₂ put *q* = Σ_α *z*_α*Q*_α; then Σ *z*_α*z*_β∗*N*_αβ(ψ) = ‖*q*†ψ‖² + ‖*q*ψ‖² ≥ 0. A Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix is *M*(*v*) for one *v* (Theorem II.6.38); it is positive semidefinite exactly when its trace 2*v*⁰ and its determinant (*v*⁰)² − |**v**|² are non-negative. The transformation rule follows by applying the rule for *U*(*A*)*Q*_α*U*(*A*)⁻¹ to *Q*_α and its adjoint to *Q*_β†; then *M*(*v*) is replaced by *A*⁻¹*M*(*v*)(*A*⁻¹)† = *M*(Λ(*A*⁻¹)*v*). □

**Remark II.9.6.15 (The exact standing of the gauge group, of supersymmetry and of the string dualities).** (a) Gauge group. Proved in this paper: the phase covariance of the McGucken Operator (Theorem II.8.5.4) and, for *G* = U(1) or SU(*N*), the uniqueness of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian (Theorem II.8.7.8). The choice of the group U(1) × SU(2) × SU(3) is taken from experiment in [74], whose Level 4 statement reads: “The Standard Model gauge group SU(3) × SU(2) × U(1) is the empirical content”. A derivation of the group itself is the subject of [133]; it has not been checked for this paper and no result here depends on it. (b) Supersymmetry. Proposition II.9.6.14 shows that the anticommutators of spinor charges form a four-vector in the closed forward cone. That this four-vector is twice the energy–momentum, and that the resulting algebra is the only graded extension of the algebra of ISO⁺(1,3) under the hypotheses of the theorem, is the theorem of Haag, Łopuszański and Sohnius [147], used in [74] as an external theorem. Whether such charges exist in nature is open; Lemma 54 of [74] states that its Theorem 51 does not assert that supersymmetry is realised in nature. (c) String dualities. Theorem 55 of [74] is conditional on its Hypotheses A1–A22, and its Proposition 57′ A states that the carriers of the five dualities are postulated. No statement about the string dualities is proved in this paper, and none is used.

**9.6.4 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Twistor Space and of the Kinematic Data of the Amplituhedron.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The McGucken Sphere of an event *x* is the set of the points reached from *x* at the velocity *c*; each of its points moves along a light ray through *x*. The light rays of spacetime are therefore the worldlines of the points of the McGucken Spheres. The next theorem shows that these rays are the points of a real hypersurface of the complex projective space ℂP³, the projective twistor space of Penrose [97], and that the McGucken Sphere of each event is a projective line in it.

**Theorem II.9.6.16 (Twistor space from the McGucken Sphere; Theorem 13.1 of [75] in the form proved here).** For an event *x* let *M*(*x*) = *x*⁰ + *x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃ be the Hermitian matrix of Theorem II.6.38, with *x*⁰ = *ct*. For a point *Z* = (ω, π) of ℂ⁴, ω and π in ℂ², put *s*(*Z*) = π†ω + ω†π. (i) i*M*(*x*) = *x*₄ + i(*x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃) with *x*₄ = i*ct*. (ii) For every event *x* and every π ≠ 0 the point (i*M*(*x*)π, π) satisfies *s* = 0. (iii) For π ≠ 0 let ρ be a non-zero vector with π†ρ = 0 and let *n*(π) be the vector with *M*(*n*(π)) = ρρ†. It is a null vector pointing to the future, and its direction depends only on the ray of π. (iv) Two pairs (*x*, π), (*y*, π′) give the same point of ℂP³ exactly when π′ is a multiple of π and *y* − *x* is a real multiple of *n*(π). (v) Every point (ω, π) with π ≠ 0 and *s* = 0 is (i*M*(*x*)π, π) for some event *x*. (vi) Consequently the map that sends the light ray through *x* with the direction *n*(π) to the point [i*M*(*x*)π, π] is a bijection of the set of the light rays of spacetime onto the set *PN* of the points of ℂP³ with *s* = 0 and π ≠ 0, a real hypersurface of dimension 5. The rays of the McGucken Sphere of the event *x* form the projective line *L_x* of the points [i*M*(*x*)π, π], π ≠ 0, a copy of ℂP¹, the sphere *S*². (vii) *L_x* and *L_y* have a common point exactly when *y* − *x* is null or zero, that is, when one of the two events lies on a McGucken Sphere of the other or the events coincide. (viii) The form *s* has two positive and two negative squares.

*Proof.* (i) i*x*⁰ = i*ct* = *x*₄. (ii) With ω = i*M*π, π†ω + ω†π = iπ†*M*π − iπ†*M*†π = 0, since *M*† = *M*. (iii) ρρ† is Hermitian with determinant 0 and positive trace, so by det *M*(*n*) = −η(*n*, *n*) the vector *n* is null with *n*⁰ > 0. The vectors orthogonal to π form a line, so ρ is determined up to a factor λ, which multiplies *n* by |λ|²; replacing π by a multiple leaves the line unchanged. (iv) The two points of ℂ⁴ are proportional exactly when π′ = λπ and i*M*(*y*)λπ = λ i*M*(*x*)π, that is, *M*(*y* − *x*)π = 0. A Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix *H* with *H*π = 0 maps ρ to a vector orthogonal to π, hence to a multiple of ρ, with a real factor; so *H* is a real multiple of ρρ†. (v) Put *w* = −iω. The condition *s* = 0 states that π†*w* is real. The matrix *H* = (*w*π† + π*w*†)/(π†π) − (π†*w*)ππ†/(π†π)² is Hermitian, and *H*π = *w* + π(*w*†π)/(π†π) − (π†*w*)π/(π†π) = *w*, because *w*†π = (π†*w*)∗ = π†*w*. With *H* = *M*(*x*), i*M*(*x*)π = ω. (vi) follows from (ii), (iv) and (v): the events *x* + *s* *n*(π), *s* real, are the light ray through *x* with the direction *n*(π), and by (iii) every future null direction occurs. The equation *s* = 0 is one real equation on the six real dimensions of ℂP³. (vii) A common point exists exactly when (*M*(*x*) − *M*(*y*))π = 0 for some π ≠ 0, that is, det *M*(*x* − *y*) = 0. (viii) With *u* = (ω + π)/√2 and *v* = (ω − π)/√2, *s* = *u*†*u* − *v*†*v*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** In Theorem II.9.6.16 the complex structure of twistor space comes from the unit i of *x*₄ = i*ct*: by (i) the incidence matrix i*M*(*x*) is the fourth coordinate *x*₄ of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* together with i times the three spatial coordinates, and by (ii) it is this i that places the rays on the hypersurface *s* = 0. Twistor theory takes ℂP³ and the incidence relation ω = i*M*(*x*)π as its starting point (Penrose [97]). The Principle gives them a physical origin: the points of *PN* are the worldlines of the points of the expanding McGucken Spheres, and the line *L_x* is the McGucken Sphere of the event *x*. The group preserving the form *s* is SU(2,2), which acts on the rays through the conformal group of Theorem II.6.41; that statement is used here as a remark and is not needed for (i)–(viii).

**Proposition II.9.6.17 (The kinematic data of the amplituhedron are meeting points of lines of McGucken Spheres; Theorem 9.2 of [75] in the part proved here).** Let *p*₁, …, *p_n* be non-zero null vectors with *p*₁ + ⋯ + *p_n* = 0, the energy–momenta of *n* massless particles, each at rest in *x*₄. Choose an event *y*₁ and put *y*_(j+1) = *y_j* − *p_j*. Then *y*_(n+1) = *y*₁; consecutive events *y_j*, *y*_(j+1) are null separated; and the lines *L*_(y_j) and *L*_(y_(j+1)) of Theorem II.9.6.16 meet in exactly one point *Z_j* of ℂP³, so that *L*_(y_j) is the line through *Z*_(j−1) and *Z_j* when these two points differ.

*Proof.* The sum of the *p_j* is zero, which gives *y*_(n+1) = *y*₁. *y_j* − *y*_(j+1) = *p_j* is null, so by Theorem II.9.6.16(vii) the two lines meet; the common points are the [i*M*(*y_j*)π, π] with *M*(*p_j*)π = 0, and the Hermitian matrix *M*(*p_j*) has determinant 0 and is not zero, so its null space is one line. Two different points of a projective line determine it, and *Z*_(j−1), *Z_j* lie on *L*_(y_j). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The points *Z*₁, …, *Z_n* are the momentum twistors of Hodges [148]. The amplituhedron of Arkani-Hamed and Trnka [149] is defined from them: for real data, the *Z_j* with *k* further components adjoined, whose ordered maximal minors are positive, it is the set of the *Y* = *C*·*Z* with *C* a *k* × *n* matrix of non-negative ordered maximal minors, and its canonical form is identified in [149] with the tree amplitudes and the loop integrands of the planar maximally supersymmetric Yang–Mills theory. Proposition 9.6.17 proves the part of Theorem 9.2 of [75] that concerns the data: each *Z_j* is the common ray of two McGucken Spheres whose centers are separated by the momentum of one massless particle. The further statement of [75], that the canonical form is the sum over the chains of intersecting McGucken Spheres and that the positivity is the orientation +i*c*, is not proved in this paper; [75] states of the explicit equivalence that “the calculation has not been written”. It is recorded here as an open problem of the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework.

**9.6.5 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Unit i in the Formulas of Quantum Theory.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle contains the unit i once, in the rate i*c*, where it marks the perpendicularity of *x*₄ to the three spatial dimensions. The companion paper [78] derives the appearances of i in quantum theory from this one occurrence in thirty-four theorems. The mechanism and the cases proved in this paper follow.

**Theorem II.9.6.18 (The three substitutions that carry the unit i; Theorems 1, 6 and 9 and Lemma 14 of [78]).** (i) If *G*(**x**, *x*₄) is holomorphic in *x*₄ and *g*(*t*, **x**) = *G*(**x**, i*ct*), then (∂₄*G*)(**x**, i*ct*) = (i*c*)⁻¹∂ₜ*g* = −(i/*c*)∂ₜ*g*: on the constraint surface every derivative along *x*₄ is −i/*c* times the derivative in *t*. (ii) With τ = *x*₄/*c*, the relation *x*₄ = i*ct* is *t* = −iτ. (iii) For the action *S* = ∫ (½*m*(*d***x**/*dt*)² − *V*(**x**)) *dt* of §II.8.7 the substitution *t* = −iτ gives i*S* = −*S_E* with *S_E* = ∫ (½*m*(*d***x**/*d*τ)² + *V*(**x**)) *d*τ, so that exp(i*S*/ℏ) = exp(−*S_E*/ℏ).

*Proof.* (i) The chain rule gives ∂ₜ*g* = i*c*(∂₄*G*). (ii) *t* = *x*₄/(i*c*) = −i*x*₄/*c*. (iii) *dt* = −i *d*τ and *d***x**/*dt* = i *d***x**/*d*τ, so (*d***x**/*dt*)² = −(*d***x**/*d*τ)² and *S* = ∫ (−½*m*(*d***x**/*d*τ)² − *V*)(−i) *d*τ = i*S_E*. □

**Proposition II.9.6.19 (The term +iε; Theorem 12 of [78]).** Let *H* be a self-adjoint operator on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, *E* real and ε > 0. Then (i) exp(−i*H* *t*(1 − iε)/ℏ) = exp(−i*Ht*/ℏ) exp(−ε*Ht*/ℏ), and for *H* ≥ 0 and *t* ≥ 0 its norm is at most 1; (ii) the integral of exp(i(*E* − *H* + iε)*t*/ℏ) over *t* from 0 to ∞ equals iℏ(*E* − *H* + iε)⁻¹. The time *t*(1 − iε) is the time *t* displaced by ε*t* in the direction −i, the direction of τ in *t* = −iτ of Theorem II.9.6.18(ii).

*Proof.* (i) The two exponents commute and add. (ii) On an eigenvector of *H* with the eigenvalue *h* the integrand is exp(i(*E* − *h*)*t*/ℏ) exp(−ε*t*/ℏ), and its integral is ℏ/(ε − i(*E* − *h*)) = iℏ/(*E* − *h* + iε). □

The twelve insertions are those listed in Theorem 16 of [78].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Every row of the table that displays the unit i and has an entry in the second column is an instance of Theorem II.9.6.18: the unit i of the formula is the unit i of *x*₄ = i*ct*, carried by a derivative along *x*₄ (part (i)), by the substitution *t* = −iτ (part (ii)), or by both in the action (part (iii)). The Hawking temperature and the Born rule are listed because [78] counts them among its theorems. The count thirty-four in the title of [78] is the count of its theorems. Seventeen rows of the table are proved in this paper; the rows of the last line are results of [78] that this paper does not reproduce and does not use.

**9.6.6 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Spectral Triples of Connes.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. A spectral triple (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) in the sense of Connes [16] is an algebra 𝒜 with an involution, represented by bounded operators on a Hilbert space ℋ, together with a self-adjoint operator *D* with compact resolvent such that [*D*, *a*] is bounded for every *a* in 𝒜. The four-coordinate carrier of the Principle, taken with *x*₄ real, carries the Dirac operator of such a triple, and on the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* that operator becomes the Dirac operator of Theorem II.8.5.3.

**Theorem II.9.6.20 (The Dirac operator of the four-coordinate carrier, the distance of Connes, and the restriction to the constraint surface; Definition 4.1, Lemma 3.11 and Theorem B of [79] in the form proved here).** With the matrices of Theorem II.8.5.3(i) put γ_E^j = iγ^j for *j* = 1, 2, 3 and γ_E⁴ = −γ⁰, and on the functions of the real variables (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *x*₄) with values in ℂ⁴ let *D_E* = −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ, the sum over μ = 1, …, 4. (i) The γ_E^μ are Hermitian, γ_E^μ γ_E^ν + γ_E^ν γ_E^μ = 2δ^μν, and *D_E*² = −(∂₁² + ∂₂² + ∂₃² + ∂₄²). (ii) For a smooth real function *f*, [*D_E*, *f*] is the multiplication by the matrix −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ*f*, whose norm at each point is the Euclidean length |∇*f*| of the gradient. (iii) For two points *p*, *q* the supremum of |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| over the smooth real functions *f* with ‖[*D_E*, *f*]‖ ≤ 1 is the Euclidean distance |*p* − *q*|. (iv) *D_E* is symmetric on the smooth functions of compact support with the inner product ∫ ψ†φ *d*⁴*x*. (v) *D_E* = Σ_j γ^j∂_j + iγ⁰∂₄; for Ψ holomorphic in *x*₄ and ψ(*t*, **x**) = Ψ(**x**, i*ct*), (*D_E*Ψ)(**x**, i*ct*) = γ^μ∂_μ ψ, so that iℏ*D_E* + *mc* restricts to the Dirac operator iℏγ^μ∂_μ + *mc* of Theorem II.8.5.3, and *D_E*² restricts to −□.

*Proof.* (i) γ⁰ is Hermitian with square 1 and each γ^j is anti-Hermitian with square −1 (Lemma II.8.7.2), so the γ_E^μ are Hermitian with square 1; they anticommute in pairs because the γ^μ do. Then *D_E*² = −Σ_μ Σ_ν γ_E^μ γ_E^ν∂_μ∂_ν = −Σ_μ ∂_μ², the mixed terms cancelling in pairs. (ii) By the product rule *D_E*(*f*ψ) = *f* *D_E*ψ − i(Σ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ*f*)ψ. For real numbers *v*_μ the matrix Σ_μ *v*_μ γ_E^μ is Hermitian with the square |*v*|², so its norm is |*v*|. (iii) If |∇*f*| ≤ 1 everywhere, then |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| ≤ |*p* − *q*| by the mean value theorem along the segment from *q* to *p*. The linear function *f*(*x*) = *u*·(*x* − *q*), with *u* the unit vector along *p* − *q*, has |∇*f*| = 1 and *f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*) = |*p* − *q*|. (iv) Integration by parts moves ∂_μ to the other factor with a change of sign, the conjugation changes the sign of −i, and the γ_E^μ are Hermitian. (v) −iγ_E^j = γ^j and −iγ_E⁴ = iγ⁰. By Theorem II.9.6.18(i), (∂₄Ψ)(**x**, i*ct*) = −(i/*c*)∂ₜψ, so the last term becomes γ⁰*c*⁻¹∂ₜψ = γ⁰∂₀ψ. The statement on the square is Theorem II.8.5.2. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Theorem II.9.6.20 is the relation of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* to the geometry of Connes that is proved in this paper. The triple of Connes takes the algebra, the Hilbert space and the operator as three data (Proposition II.9.6.6(iii)); the Principle yields the operator *D_E* from the four coordinates and the Dirac operator of physics from *D_E* by the one substitution *x*₄ = i*ct*, and the distance of Connes computed from *D_E* is the distance of the four-coordinate form of Lemma II.6.25. Three limits of the theorem are stated. The resolvent of *D_E* on the whole of ℝ⁴ is not compact, so the axioms of [16] apply after restriction to a compact manifold such as a torus. On the constraint surface the form is Lorentzian and iℏγ^μ∂_μ is not symmetric for the positive inner product of part (iv), so the axioms, which are Riemannian, apply to the real section and reach the Lorentzian operator through part (v). The verification of the seven axioms, the reconstruction theorem of Connes [150] and the correspondence of the spectral action with the Lagrangian of Theorem 8.7.9 are results of [79] that rest on external theorems of spectral geometry; [79] grades them accordingly and takes the finite algebra of the gauge group as empirical input. They are not used in this paper. The McGucken Operator D_M and the operator *D_E* are different operators (Theorem II.9.6.2(b)): the McGucken Operator D_M is the transport along the expansion of *x*₄, of the first order in *t*, and *D_E* is a square root of minus the four-coordinate Laplacian.

**Summary of §II.9.6 by item.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* yields: the commutator [McGucken Operator D_M, *x*₄] = i*c* that separates the McGucken Operator from the operators of spacetime (Theorems II.9.6.2, II.9.6.3); the rate i*c* as the one constant rate with the Lorentzian interval and the wave operator (Theorem II.9.6.5); the six functors of the source-pair and its place as the least element of the order of generation (Theorems II.9.6.8, II.9.6.9); the group and the Lagrangian to which Noether’s theorem is applied (Theorem II.9.6.10, Corollary II.9.6.11); the orbits and stabilizers of the classification of Wigner, with the particle at rest advancing along *x*₄ and the photon at rest in *x*₄ (Theorem II.9.6.12); the path of rotations of the four coordinates that ends in the total reflection of the CPT theorem (Theorem II.9.6.13); the light rays of the McGucken Spheres as the points of twistor space and the momentum twistors as their meeting points (Theorem II.9.6.16, Proposition II.9.6.17); the unit i of seventeen formulas of quantum theory (Theorem II.9.6.18 and the table); and the Dirac operator of Connes on the four-coordinate carrier with its restriction to the Dirac operator of physics (Theorem II.9.6.20). External theorems used with attribution: Wigner [144] and Mackey [145], Jost [146], Haag, Łopuszański and Sohnius [147], Arkani-Hamed and Trnka [149], Connes [16, 150].

### Appendix II.A. Derivation of the Lorentz Transformation from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

To make the claim that special relativity descends from the McGucken Principle as a theorem rather than as a postulate-set, we exhibit the derivation in full. The argument occupies four short steps; no assumption beyond *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, holding with spherical symmetry from every event, and its integrated form *x*₄ = i*ct* is used.

**Step 1 (Four-velocity normalization).** A worldline parameterized by proper time *τ* has four-velocity *u^μ* = *dx^μ*/*dτ*. From *x*₄ = i*ct* and the McGucken Principle:

*u*₄ = *dx*₄/*dτ* = i*c* *dt*/*dτ* = i*cγ*.

Here *γ* denotes *dt*/*dτ*. Proper time is defined from the line element of §II.9.1 by *c*²*dτ*² = −(*dx*₄² + *d***x**²) = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**², so that (*dτ*/*dt*)² = 1 − *v*²/*c*² and *γ* = *dt*/*dτ* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^−1/2: the factor *γ* is derived from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Squaring and using the quadratic form *dx*₄² + *d***x**² on the (*x*₄, **x**) chart:

*u_μu^μ* = *u*₄² + |**u**|² = −*c*²*γ*² + *γ*²|**v**|² = −*c*²,

the standard relativistic normalization, in agreement with the value of *γ* just derived.

**Step 2 (Time dilation).** The temporal component of *u^μ* is *dt*/*dτ* = *γ*. Inverting, *dτ* = *dt*/*γ*; for a moving clock, less proper time elapses per unit of coordinate time, exactly the time-dilation formula.

**Step 3 (Lorentz boost).** Let *S* and *S*′ be two inertial frames with relative velocity *v* along *x*¹. Each frame measures the same *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* because the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is a statement about the fourth dimension *x*₄, holding from every event and in every inertial frame. Demanding linearity, isotropy of *x*², *x*³, and the constancy of *dx*₄² + *d***x**² uniquely selects the rotation in the (*x*¹, *x*₄) plane:

*x*′₄ = *x*₄ cos*θ* − *x*¹ sin*θ*, *x*′¹ = *x*¹ cos*θ* + *x*₄ sin*θ*.

Substituting *x*₄ = i*ct* and fixing *θ* by the motion of the origin of *S*′ (*x*′¹ = 0 along *x*¹ = *vt* gives tan*θ* = −*x*¹/*x*₄ = −*vt*/(i*ct*) = i*v*/*c*, so that cos*θ* = *γ* and sin*θ* = i*γv*/*c*) reproduces, after using cos(i*φ*) = cosh*φ* and sin(i*φ*) = i sinh*φ*:

*t*′ = *γ*(*t* − *vx*¹/*c*²), *x*′¹ = *γ*(*x*¹ − *vt*),

the standard Lorentz boost. Length contraction *L* = *L*₀/*γ* follows by setting *dt*′ = 0; mass-energy equivalence *E*₀ = *mc*² follows from the temporal component of four-momentum, *p*₄ = *mu*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*, by reading off the modulus.

**Step 4 (Constancy of *c*).** The invariant statement of the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is that *x*₄ advances at rate i*c*: any inertial frame agrees on this rate, since it is the law itself. A light signal satisfies *dx*₄² + *d***x**² = 0, that is |*d***x**/*dt*| = *c*, and the boost of Step 3 preserves *dx*₄² + *d***x**², so |*d***x**′/*dt*′| = *c* in every inertial frame. The constancy of the speed of light, taken as Einstein’s second postulate of 1905, is in the McGucken framework a corollary of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* being a frame-independent statement.

**Remark.** Einstein’s 1905 derivation took the constancy of *c* as a postulate and derived the Lorentz transformation by demanding linearity. The McGucken Principle moves the postulate one level deeper: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is the law, and the constancy of *c* is what the law states once *x*₄ is read off in the (*x*₄, **x**) chart. □
